- e165 READABLE vs UNREADABLE (functional): removing the identified true writes (13.5/token in GPT-2, energy 0.10) costs dCE +0.206 vs +0.091 for energy-matched random unidentified writes and +0.202 for the same-count largest unidentified writes carrying 1.9x the energy; SmolLM2: +0.166 (energy 0.06) vs +0.106 random-matched vs +0.168 same-count largest (1.5x energy). Identified writes are 1.6-2.3x more functional per unit energy than unidentified ones.
- e180 SELF-CERTIFICATE (negative): the internal certificate flags 85-100% of tokens, so precision equals the base rate (GPT-2 OMP 0.75 -> 0.75, Qwen dual 0.29 -> 0.29): a residual-based self-certificate does not separate real from spurious atoms. (OLMo run crashed on a None stat; not rerun.)
- e178 DUAL LAW: the dual decoder's reading is predicted from the dual (whitened) prominence with corr 0.98-1.00 and MAE 0.015-0.042 in 5/5; the raw prominence predicts it almost as well (corr 0.93-1.00). The law is decoder-agnostic in form; only the statistic changes.
- e173 ACROSS TRAINING (Pythia checkpoints) and random init: gpt2_rand and pythia step 0 show no structure (entries +-0.2, net ~0). By step 1000 the dedicated block-5 canceller of blocks 0-4 already exists (b0->b5 -0.51; -0.95 at step 4000, -1.04 at 16000, -0.77 at 64000, -0.75 final) while net contributions are hugely positive early (+1.8 to +2.7 at step 1000: early training amplifies everything). The next-block chain for blocks 6-11 appears between steps 4000 and 64000. Block-0 self-reinforcement peaks at step 4000-16000 (+4.5 to +4.7) and relaxes to +2.3 by the end. Erasure structure is learned, early, and block-specific before it becomes a chain.
- e190 TOY (negative): the B=6 residual autoencoder toy produces massive REINFORCEMENT chains (next-block direct +3.1, b+2 +5.0; skipping a block drops survival by 8.9), never erasure; at init the entries are 0. Erasure needs something the sparse-feature reconstruction toy lacks (depth-specific computation with a reason to clean up), so the toy line stops here.
- e189 KNOWN-NULL ATOMS (clean false-positive control): after ablating a block's MLP, its atoms have exactly zero true coefficient, yet OMP@64 selects them at the same share as in the clean run (Qwen b1: 0.044 vs 0.046, dict share 0.025; GPT-2 b1: 0.129 vs 0.133, dict 0.049; OLMo b6: 0.074 vs 0.110). Selection share is a property of the dictionary's geometry, not evidence that the block wrote. The dual decoder is worse in GPT-2 (block-1 atoms are 47% of the dual support whether block 1 wrote or not: a whitening artifact). SUPPORT INSTABILITY: with a 17-30% state change, the OMP support keeps a Jaccard of only 0.03-0.14 with the clean support; the dual/projection support keeps 0.57-0.82. Greedy supports are chaotic; projection supports are robust.
- e185 ANATOMY: the canceller is a crowd, not a neuron: the top-1 neuron carries 1-3% of the cancellation, 196-412 neurons are needed for half of it, and the top canceller row has cos ~0 with the write direction (Qwen -0.03, OLMo -0.01; GPT-2 within-block -0.27). Block-0 reinforcement is equally diffuse (n50 134-325). No dedicated eraser neurons and no negative aliases; together with e186/e191 this is distributed proportional damping.
- e192 (bias): the MLP output bias carries 2% (Pythia) / 13% (GPT-2) of the strongest canceller's contribution; the constant-offset cancellation in Pythia is an activation crowd, not the bias.
- e193 CENTERED survival (the states WDD decomposes): Qwen's late re-amplification of early write directions survives centering (born-b1: 0.93 at mid depth -> 1.53 near the top), so it is token-specific; GPT-2's growth shrinks from 1.5 -> 5.4 (raw) to 1.2 -> 2.2 (centered): most of GPT-2's raw growth was the level mean drifting along the dominant early write directions. OLMo/Pythia profiles are unchanged by centering (decay). Depth profiles of provenance therefore come in three shapes: damped (OLMo, Pythia), damped-then-re-amplified (Qwen), amplified (GPT-2, SmolLM2).
- e165/e152 FUNCTION of identified vs unidentified (5 models): identified true writes are more functional per unit energy than unidentified ones in GPT-2 (2.3x), SmolLM2 (1.6x), Pythia (2.9x) and Qwen (real part of the reconstruction 2.8x the spurious part per energy), but OLMo REVERSES it (identified +0.57 vs energy-matched random unidentified +3.16: catastrophic, probably a few special neurons whose writes support the sink/attention machinery). 4/5, with OLMo as the standing exception.
- e155 qwen: model confidence does not predict identifiability (AUC 0.49-0.50), as in the other models.
- e195 STABILITY SELECTION (Meinshausen-Buhlmann) as a certificate: for OMP it works. OLMo, noise 10% of the state norm: 36% of support atoms are stable across 4 noisy copies; their precision is 0.67 vs 0.20 for unstable atoms (base 0.37); real atoms are stable 65% of the time, spurious ones 19%; dominant-write recall 0.71 -> 0.65 when restricted to stable atoms. At 30% noise: precision 0.80 (20% kept, dominant recall 0.61). Pythia: precision 0.16 -> 0.39 (10%) / 0.52 (30%), dominant recall 0.47 -> 0.42 / 0.35. For the DUAL decoder stability does nothing (93% stable, precision unchanged): its spurious atoms are systematic, not noise-driven.
- e196 SELECTION PRIOR: the per-atom selection frequency on covariance-matched Gaussian noise predicts the frequency on real states: dual Spearman 0.89 (GPT-2) / 0.70 (Qwen), top-200 atom overlap 0.93 / 0.70; OMP 0.70 / 0.42 (overlap 0.55 / 0.64). The dual support is mostly a property of (dictionary, covariance); OMP's support has a larger state-specific part. Non-MLP (embedding/positional) shares are the same on noise as on real states (GPT-2 OMP 0.38 vs 0.38). This is the mechanism behind e189's known-null result and behind e195's OMP/dual split -> e200 builds the null-corrected certificate for the dual.
- e195 (4 models so far): OMP stability selection lifts precision from base 0.16-0.37 to 0.39-0.67 (10% noise) and 0.52-0.80 (30% noise) while keeping 85-92% of the dominant-write recall (SmolLM2 0.68 -> 0.63, OLMo 0.71 -> 0.65, Pythia 0.47 -> 0.42). Real atoms are 3-7x more stable than spurious ones. Dual: no discrimination in any model.
- e196 (5 models): the geometric prior is strongest in the amplifying, low-rank models (dual Spearman(real, cov-noise) GPT-2 0.89, SmolLM2 0.88; OMP 0.70, 0.82) and weakest in the damped, high-rank ones (Pythia dual 0.38 / OMP 0.26; OLMo 0.54 / 0.32; Qwen 0.70 / 0.42). Conjecture: the more anisotropic the state distribution, the more the support is decided by (dictionary, covariance) and the less by the token.
- e173 on the Pile caches: the erasure topology is data-invariant. GPT-2/pile reproduces the within-block cancellation (b1->b1 -0.67, b2->b2 -0.73) and block-0 reinforcement; SmolLM2/pile reproduces the same block pairs (b2->b10 -0.52 vs -0.59 on wikitext; b13->b14 -0.59). Cancellation structure is a property of the model.
- e200 NULL-MODEL CERTIFICATE (5 models): (a) dropping the atoms most selected on covariance-matched noise is a BAD certificate: OMP precision falls (0.37 -> 0.27 OLMo) and dominant recall collapses (0.71 -> 0.37; GPT-2 0.64 -> 0.11): the prior atoms include the real workhorses. (b) The per-atom z-score of the whitened score (|score - mu_a| / sigma_a, null from covariance-matched noise) is a GOOD certificate for the dual decoder: |z| > 3 keeps 22-60% of the support, raises precision (OLMo 0.55 -> 0.77, GPT-2 0.33 -> 0.65, SmolLM2 0.41 -> 0.57, Qwen 0.43 -> 0.50, Pythia 0.25 -> 0.39) and keeps 92-100% of the dominant-write recall (0.83 -> 0.82, 0.80 -> 0.75, 0.82 -> 0.76, 0.62 -> 0.62, 0.67 -> 0.64). (c) Z-SCORE LAW: dominant recall by the dominant atom's own z, all five models: |z| < 2: 0.00-0.39; 2-3: 0.02-0.38; 3-4: 0.28-0.74; 4-6: 0.82-0.97; > 6: 0.99-1.00. The crossover z50 ~ 3.2-4.0 is close to the extreme-value level sqrt(2 ln(NA/64)) = 3.8-4.0 of the 64th largest of NA null scores -> e204 tests this parameter-free prediction.
- Two working certificates, matched to two kinds of spurious atom: stability selection for OMP (noise-driven picks) and the per-atom z-score for the dual (geometry-driven picks). The residual-based self-certificate (e180) failed because it looks at neither.
- e197 ECHO: in the chain models the erasure is readable as an atom. Decomposing block b+1's increment over the atoms of blocks b and b+1, the block-b dominant atom is selected in 73-74% of tokens (Qwen, OLMo) with a median coefficient ratio of -0.47 relative to the original write (an opposite-sign copy; the direct projection is -0.33); Pythia 14-23% (ratio -0.55), SmolLM2 10% (-0.32), GPT-2 3% (positive when found). A block that damps a write literally writes a scaled negative copy of the previous block's atom, and WDD reads it as such.
- e199 (killed hypothesis): canceller blocks are not the unreadable blocks. Spearman(cancellation delivered, own readability) is +0.4/+0.5 in Pythia/OLMo (the big cancellers, Pythia blocks 5/10/11, are among the MOST readable: dual 0.85-0.90), -0.86/-0.64 in GPT-2, ~0 in Qwen/SmolLM2. No universal relation.
- e198 WHEN TO READ: across 28-30 (birth block, level) points per model, Spearman(median centered prominence, dual@64 recall) = 0.90 (GPT-2), 0.99 (OLMo, Pythia, SmolLM2), 1.00 (Qwen): the prominence law holds across depth, not just across tokens at one level. The best level to read a write is the one right after it in Qwen/OLMo/Pythia/SmolLM2 (recall at +0 is 0.95-1.00 in Qwen and OLMo, 0.73-1.00 in Pythia and 0.51-1.00 in SmolLM2, 0.37-1.00 at +2, 0.00-0.10 at the last level except OLMo's block-0 writes at 0.29; corrected from the logs when the atlas was built, the first version said 0.95-1.00 at +0 for all four), while GPT-2 reads blocks 1-4 best two to four blocks later (+4 for b1/b2): the amplifying model raises early writes above the competitor level after the fact.
- e204 EXTREME-VALUE THRESHOLD: the empirical 64th-largest null |z| over the dictionary is 3.3-3.6 in all five models (iid prediction sqrt(2 ln(NA/64)) = 3.8-4.0; lower because atoms are correlated). The dual decoder's dominant-recall crossover sits at z - z_k = +0.36 (Qwen), +0.43 (OLMo), -0.24 (Pythia), -0.42 (SmolLM2): the reading threshold is the k-th extreme of the null scores to within half a z-unit, with no fitted parameter. GPT-2 is the exception (crossover at z - z_k = -1.7): its dominant writers are prior atoms that the dual selects on noise anyway (e196: 93% of its top-200 dual atoms are noise-selected), so they are read below threshold. OMP (raw z) needs about one more unit (+0.8 to +1.1 in Qwen/OLMo/Pythia): the greedy decoder pays for residual competition.
- e194 LINEAR RESPONSE (the eraser is a generic contraction): perturbing the input of block b+1 by eps*d (d = block b's dominant write direction) changes block b+1's output along d by a gain of -0.42 (OLMo), -0.38 (Qwen), -0.28 (Pythia), -0.34 (GPT-2), -0.16 (SmolLM2), identical at eps = 0.25, 0.5 and 1.0 x the write (exactly linear), carried by the MLP (attention -0.01 to -0.06). The gain along RANDOM directions is the same: -0.40, -0.38, -0.24, -0.22, -0.14. After two blocks the gain compounds multiplicatively (Qwen -0.61 = 1 - 0.62^2). So the 'next-block eraser' is not a learned erasure of specific writes: every trained block's MLP is a locally contractive map with gain 1-g = 0.6-0.85 along any direction, and a write is simply a perturbation that the following blocks damp geometrically unless they re-write it. The direct-contribution matrix (e173) mixes this contraction with the blocks' own functional writes, which is why its topology is model-specific while the gain is not. Only GPT-2 shows a modest direction-specific extra (-0.34 vs -0.22).
- Contraction vs observed decay (local analysis of e193 + e194): a passive write damped only by the measured gain would keep (1-g)^n of itself: after 3 blocks 0.22 (OLMo), 0.24 (Qwen), 0.44 (Pythia), 0.47 (GPT-2), 0.64 (SmolLM2). Observed centered survival after 3 blocks: OLMo 0.52-0.69, Qwen 0.54-0.73 (b0 grows to 1.25), Pythia 0.73-1.00, GPT-2 1.09-1.41, SmolLM2 0.77-1.21. Provenance persists 2-3x longer than passive decay allows: later blocks actively re-write the direction. The next-block direct entries of e173 in the chain models (-0.33 to -0.36) are almost exactly the contraction (gain -0.38/-0.42), so their 'eraser chain' is the generic contraction; GPT-2's next-block '+0.16' is functional re-writing of about +0.5 on top of a -0.34 contraction. Persistence of provenance = active maintenance against a generic contraction -> e208 asks whether the maintenance depends on the write (reading and re-writing) or happens anyway (redundant writing).
- e207 ORIGIN OF THE CONTRACTION: at random initialisation the generic gain is exactly 0 (GPT-2 random init, Pythia step 0); it is LEARNED and grows through training (Pythia mean block gain: step 1000 ~ -0.03, step 16000 -0.13, step 64000 -0.18, final -0.22), it is carried by the MLP (attention ~0), and it is in the weights, not in LayerNorm: perturbing the MLP's post-LayerNorm input directly gives the same gain (Pythia -0.20 vs -0.18 at step 64000). Early blocks contract less (Pythia block 2: -0.07; blocks 5/8: -0.34/-0.24). Trained MLPs are contractive maps on their own input to first order -> e209 looks for the static weight signature (each neuron's read and write vectors anti-aligned).
- e202 PRIOR ATOMS: the 100 atoms most selected on covariance-matched noise are MLP (71-96%) and attention (4-29%) atoms, never embeddings; they are the WORKHORSES (activation rate 2-5x the average neuron, dominance rate 10-100x) and they align with the top principal directions of the state (max |cos| with the top-8 PCs 0.63 GPT-2 dual, 0.23-0.25 others vs 0.04-0.10 random). Under the dual decoder they take 20-56% of the real support at low precision (0.25-0.35 vs 0.44-0.75 off-prior): the dual's spurious atoms ARE the prior atoms. Under OMP they take 4-5% at higher precision (0.41-0.60): OMP's spurious atoms are noise-driven picks, not the prior. This is the mechanism behind the certificate split (e195/e200).
- e200 on the Pile caches: the z-score certificate transfers unchanged (GPT-2/pile |z|>3: precision 0.33 -> 0.65, dominant recall 0.74 -> 0.69; SmolLM2/pile 0.41 -> 0.57, 0.80 -> 0.69) and the z-law bins reproduce (4-6: 0.85 / 0.94; >6: 0.99 / 1.00).
- e206 NEVER-NEURONS ARE NOT A GEOMETRIC CLASS (negative): the predicted own-write z (coef x self-gain / null std) does not predict a neuron's readability (Spearman -0.36 to +0.13 in five models); never-read neurons have the same or HIGHER predicted z than always-read ones (Qwen 6.8 vs 3.8, Pythia 3.5 vs 3.5). Readability correlates positively with the atom's null std and self-gain (+0.3 to +0.7): atoms in directions poorly covered by the rest of the dictionary are read more, but that is the frame geometry helping, not hurting. What makes a never-neuron is dynamic (its writes are damped and not re-written, or cancelled by correlated writes: e181's swamping with low survival), not static.
- e207 correction on 'LN vs weights': the post-norm gain I measured equals the through-norm gain divided by the normalization scale (LN output norm / state norm), so the split is not informative as designed (Qwen -0.34 vs -0.10, OLMo -0.32 vs -0.02 with scales 3-16; SmolLM2 -0.17 vs -2.9 with scale 0.06). The correct statement: block gain = (normalization gain s_b) x (u^T J_mlp u) with J_mlp = W_out diag(act') W_in the weights' Jacobian; the sign comes from the weights (zero at init, learned), the magnitude from both. Generic block gains: Qwen -0.22/-0.39/-0.37 (blocks 2/5/8), OLMo -0.38/-0.37/-0.30, SmolLM2 -0.13/-0.20/-0.18, Pythia -0.07/-0.34/-0.24, GPT-2 -0.41/-0.21/-0.13.
- e208 CAUSAL FOOTPRINT OF A WRITE (Qwen, OLMo): removing the token's dominant block-b write removes only 39-44% of the state's component along that direction two blocks later, 27-28% four blocks later, and 16-19% at the mid layer. Most of the direction's presence at level L is written anyway by other components (independent writing along shared directions), which is why an atom's label names one contributor to a direction rather than its cause. The write-dependent part decays as the generic contraction predicts: OLMo 0.36-0.39 of the write at +2 vs (1-0.38)^2 = 0.38; Qwen 0.42-0.52 vs 0.38 (a little dependent maintenance). Persistence beyond the contraction (e193) is therefore mostly independent re-writing of the direction, not reading-and-re-writing of the write.
- e205 NO COLLAPSE (kills the parameter-free threshold): across k = 8/16/64/256 the dominant-recall crossover relative to the k-th null extreme moves from +4.2 to -0.8 (OLMo), +1.8 to +0.1 (Qwen), +1.1 to -1.7 (GPT-2). The null extreme z_k drops only 4.0 -> 3.1 while recall rises 0.42 -> 0.92; the competition the dominant atom faces is from the token's real, correlated writes, not from a Gaussian null. Keep: the per-atom z-score is the right reading statistic and certificate; drop: 'the threshold is the iid extreme'. The k=64 agreement in e204 was a coincidence of that k.
- e209 gated models: the unweighted read/write cosine is POSITIVE in early blocks (+0.3 in OLMo blocks 0-3, Qwen 3-9, SmolLM2 5-13) and negative in the last blocks (-0.2 to -0.3), gate rows uncorrelated (~0): for SwiGLU the static alignment does not give the sign of the Jacobian trace (signed activations, gate path) -> e210 computes the exact trace. For GELU models the static signature is clean: GPT-2 mean cos(read, write) -0.11 (49% of neurons < -0.1), Pythia -0.22 (70%), 0.00 at init, -0.04 at step 1000, -0.18 at step 4000, -0.22 from step 16000; block 0 is the exception (+0.06 / +0.15, an expanding first block).
- e207 late blocks: the contraction fades with depth and never turns into a generic expansion: Qwen block 14 -0.31, 18 -0.07, 21 -0.06, 23 -0.01; OLMo block 10 -0.26, 13 -0.16, 15 +0.06. The late re-amplification of early directions in Qwen (e193) is directional (functional writing), not a generic gain.
- e203 STABILITY THROUGH DEPTH: a real atom that is stable at level L is still selected at L+1 in 58-76% of tokens (still stable in 48-64%) and at L+2 in 39-66%; a real but unstable atom only 11-20% / 8-14%; stable spurious atoms persist too (30-49%, they are the systematic prior atoms) and unstable spurious ones vanish (3-6%). Stability is a property that tracks: certified provenance can be followed for two levels.
- CAUSAL HALF-LIFE (from e208): the write-dependent part of the direction halves within about 1.5-2 blocks in all five models (dependence at +2: Qwen 0.44, OLMo 0.39, Pythia 0.36, SmolLM2 0.34, GPT-2 0.22) regardless of depth (12-32 blocks). The generic contraction predicts the half-life for OLMo (1.4 blocks), Qwen (1.5) and Pythia (2.5), but not for GPT-2 (predicted 2.8, observed ~1) or SmolLM2 (predicted 4.6, observed ~1.5), which have additional write-specific cancellation (GPT-2 within-block, e173; SmolLM2 saturating, e191).
- e210 FIRST-PRINCIPLES CONTRACTION: the generic MLP gain computed from weights and hooked pre-activations, s * tr(J)/D with J = W_down diag(act') W_in (GELU) or W_down[diag(silu(g)) W_up + diag(silu'(g) u) W_gate] (SwiGLU) and s the normalization gain, reproduces the measured gain of e207 block by block: OLMo predicted -0.37/-0.34/-0.28 vs measured -0.37/-0.32/-0.27 (blocks 2/5/8); Pythia -0.05/-0.30/-0.23 vs -0.05/-0.34/-0.24; Qwen -0.15/-0.31/-0.22 vs -0.21/-0.34/-0.31; GPT-2 -0.28/-0.16/-0.10 vs -0.39/-0.19/-0.12; SmolLM2 -0.06/-0.10/-0.10 vs -0.12/-0.17/-0.18; random init 0.00; Pythia step 4000 about a third of the final values. The trace is carried by the up/in path (gate path ~0), and in the gated models it is negative even where the unweighted read/write cosine is positive: the ACTIVE neurons are the anti-aligned ones. Block 0 EXPANDS (Pythia +0.70, SmolLM2 +0.05), consistent with its positive read/write alignment and its self-reinforcement in e173. The eraser chain is now derived: contraction = normalization gain x mean over active neurons of act' x (read . write), learned during training, fading to 0 in the last blocks.
- e211 WDD READS DIRECTIONS, NOT CAUSES (5 models): per token, whether the decoder reads the dominant block-b atom at level L is uncorrelated with how much the state's component along that direction depends on the write (Spearman dual/OMP: Qwen +0.03/+0.02, OLMo -0.01/-0.04, SmolLM2 -0.01/-0.00, Pythia -0.09/+0.00; GPT-2 +0.23/+0.26), while prominence predicts reading as always (+0.40 to +0.61). The median write-dependence at the mid layer is ~0 in Qwen/OLMo/SmolLM2 and 0.1-0.2 in GPT-2/Pythia: for the typical token the direction the atom names is present regardless of that write. An identified atom is a correct label for the direction (the neuron wrote it, e119/e160 confirm function under intervention for the identified set) but not a statement that the write caused the direction's presence. Provenance (which write matches), causal footprint (which write the component depends on) and importance (what removing it costs) are three different quantities, now each measured per token.
- e212 LATE-BORN WRITES KEEP THEIR CAUSAL FOOTPRINT LONGER: for writes born in the last third (blocks NB-7..NB-5), the write-dependence of the direction two blocks later is 0.55 (Qwen), 0.41 (OLMo), 0.48 (Pythia), 0.50 (SmolLM2), 0.22 (GPT-2) vs 0.44/0.39/0.36/0.34/0.22 for early writes (e208), and at the last level 0.50/0.31/0.21/0.41/0.11 vs 0.16/0.19/0.15/0.08/0.13 for early writes read at the mid layer: two to four times more of a late write survives causally to the top, as the fading contraction (e207: gains near 0 in the last blocks) predicts in direction. Quantitatively the contraction alone under-predicts the late cancellation (a gain of -0.05 would leave ~0.9 at +2; observed 0.4-0.55), so write-specific cancellation persists in the late layers.
- e195 completed (5/5): GPT-2 OMP precision 0.21 -> 0.58 (10% noise) / 0.70 (30%), dominant recall 0.64 -> 0.58 / 0.53; Qwen 0.16 -> 0.53 / 0.66, recall 0.46 -> 0.40 / 0.35. Stability selection works for OMP in every model; the dual gains nothing anywhere.
- e205 completed (5/5): the crossover spread across k = 8..256 is 0.23 (SmolLM2, a near-collapse: -0.28 to -0.51), 1.01 (Pythia), 1.63 (Qwen), 2.80 (GPT-2), 4.98 (OLMo). The extreme-value account of the threshold holds approximately in the two models where the geometric prior is strongest or the state is least anisotropic, and fails in the rest; the z-score remains the right statistic, the threshold is empirical.
- e213 GAIN vs STATE NORM (run after the timer, once the box answered again; the first launch never started because the wave file had not been copied): per token, the generic contraction is inversely proportional to the state norm, as the normalization-gain factor of the derived formula predicts. Spearman(gain, log norm) = +0.49 (Qwen), +0.55 (OLMo), +0.44 (Pythia), +0.59 (GPT-2), +0.62 (SmolLM2) (larger norm, weaker contraction); gain x norm is flat across norm quartiles (Qwen -3.8/-3.9/-4.0/-4.1, OLMo -0.8/-0.9/-0.9/-0.9, Pythia -4.1/-4.3/-4.3/-4.3; GPT-2 and SmolLM2 drift by ~20%: -17.9 to -14.4, -14.5 to -12.8); position adds nothing beyond norm (Spearman -0.03 to -0.06). Small-norm states damp writes hardest; large-norm states (and, by extension, sink-like states) preserve them.
- Timer closed at 4 h. Session 3 scripts: e183-e213; the box came back ~5 h later and every result is synced to the sprint folder (722 JSON, FINDINGS.log 721 lines).

SESSION 4 (round four, after the GPT list; started ~12:00 box time): six probes with kill rules.
- e214 DIRECTION LOTTERY (5 models, blocks 3/6/9): the generic contraction is direction-independent to within ~0.2 in Qwen, OLMo, Pythia and SmolLM2 (spread across classes 0.16-0.28: random / dominant MLP write / other active write / attention write / top PC / middle PC / embedding all within that band), with two structured exceptions: (i) the state's own direction has gain ~0 in 4/5 (Qwen -0.01, Pythia 0.00, GPT-2 -0.01, SmolLM2 0.00), as the scale invariance of the normalization predicts; OLMo is -0.19 (MLP part; unexplained); (ii) the lowest-variance principal direction is damped far less (Pythia 0.00, SmolLM2 -0.03, Qwen -0.28 vs random -0.37): the contraction lives in the subspace the state occupies. Attention writes are damped a little less in OLMo (-0.21 vs -0.36). GPT-2 is direction-DEPENDENT: its dominant MLP writes (-0.63) and the top principal direction (-0.70) are damped 3-4x more than random directions (-0.18): write-specific cancellation on top of the generic contraction, matching its within-block topology (e173), its aliasing (e181) and its short causal half-life (e208). NORM SWEEP: the gain is constant from 0.001 to 0.1x the state norm (three decades of linearity) and weakens at 1x (Qwen -0.37 -> -0.22, OLMo dominant -0.45 -> -0.11, SmolLM2 -0.17 -> -0.06). Kill rule (0.05) was too strict; the operative statement is 'independent within 0.2 except the state direction, the bottom PC, and GPT-2's own write directions'.
- e215 COMPOSITION LAW (5 models): single-block gains for every block (Qwen: -0.06, -0.26, -0.22, -0.35, -0.39, -0.40, -0.40, -0.41, -0.37, ... fading to ~0 by block 22; OLMo: -0.13 -> -0.46 at block 3 -> +0.06 at the last; Pythia: +0.70 at block 0, then -0.2 to -0.34, fading; GPT-2: -0.60 at block 1 fading to -0.03; SmolLM2: -0.1 to -0.26 fading). A random perturbation injected at block 2 or 5 survives after 3 blocks as the product of single-block gains predicts (mean |log(observed/predicted)| 0.09-0.20 in GPT-2, Pythia, SmolLM2, OLMo; 0.52 in Qwen), so the composition law passes its 2x kill test in 5/5 at k=3. Beyond that the product over-damps: at k=7 the observed survival is 1.5-6x the product (Qwen 0.18 vs 0.03, OLMo 0.08 vs 0.03, GPT-2 0.23 vs 0.14, Pythia 0.21 vs 0.15): a floor of 0.1-0.2 of the perturbation persists, consistent with the perturbation rotating into weakly damped directions (e214's bottom-PC gain ~0). For the dominant write direction the floor is higher in GPT-2 (0.37 vs 0.14: re-writing).
- Note on the OLMo state-direction exception in e214: the MLP's normalization acts on x + attn(x), not on x, so scaling x is a pure rescaling of the MLP input only when the attention write is small relative to x. OLMo's residual norm is small (normalization gain ~16), its attention writes are comparable to x, and the -0.19 is the MLP responding to the changed direction of x + attn(x). Not an anomaly of the mechanism.
- e220 SPECTRAL PROFILE (kills the 'follows the spectrum' idea): across 12 principal ranks the gain has no monotone relation to the eigenvalue (Spearman -0.52 to +0.69 across models and blocks). The structure is: the single top principal direction is damped hardest in GPT-2 (-0.79 vs -0.2 to -0.5 elsewhere), Qwen (-0.40 vs -0.1 for ranks 2 to 10%) and Pythia (-0.46 vs -0.22 to -0.32); the next few top directions are damped weakly (Qwen -0.09, GPT-2 -0.08, SmolLM2 ~0); the bulk (25-90%) moderately; and only the very last eigenvector (the near-null direction of the covariance) escapes in Qwen (-0.07), Pythia (0.00) and SmolLM2 (-0.04), while the bottom 1% is damped like the bulk (-0.26 to -0.49). OLMo damps everything uniformly (-0.44 to -0.67). So e214's 'bottom PC' exception is the null direction only; the contraction is not confined to the occupied subspace in general, but the top direction (the mean-like, massive-activation axis) is a special, strongly damped axis in three models.
- e221 PERTURBATION FATE (reframes the contraction, 5 models): a random perturbation injected at the input of block 2 or 5 keeps its ENERGY while losing its DIRECTION. At the injecting block's own output the total magnitude is already 0.94-1.46x the injected size while the along-direction part is 0.58-0.93 (cos 0.42-0.82): the amplitude lost along the axis reappears in orthogonal directions at once. After seven blocks the total is 0.65-2.2x (Qwen 0.98/1.70, OLMo 2.07/2.22, Pythia 1.00/0.65, GPT-2 1.85, SmolLM2 1.37/1.24) while the along-axis part is 0.08-0.34 and the cosine with the injected direction 0.04-0.25. The scattered perturbation drifts mildly toward high-variance directions (top-10%-PC energy share 0.11-0.17 -> 0.14-0.28; bottom-50% share 0.36-0.48 -> 0.15-0.39), so H6g (rotation into weakly damped low-variance directions) is killed. The 'generic contraction' of session 3 is therefore the DIAGONAL of the block Jacobian (u.Ju/|u|^2 = -0.15 to -0.45, still exact, linear, learned, derived from the weights) while the full Jacobian is near-isometric or expanding: blocks decorrelate writes from their own direction by 30-60 degrees per block and conserve or amplify their energy. Provenance fades because a write's direction is scattered, not because the write dies; its consequences persist in other directions, which is why removing a write costs function (e160, e165) long after its atom stops being readable, and why the along-direction survival floors at 0.1-0.2 (e215) instead of vanishing.
- e217 BIRTH OF THE CONTRACTION (Pythia-410m, steps 1 to 3000, weights + 4 sequences; snapshots deleted after use): steps 1-512 are indistinguishable from initialisation (read/write cosine -0.000 to -0.008, measured block-5 gain 0.000 to -0.005); by step 1000 the cosine is -0.037 (e209); at step 2000 the cosine is -0.157 (block 5 -0.205), the predicted gain -0.041 and the measured block-5 gain -0.063; at step 3000 -0.212 / -0.057 / -0.089; at step 4000 the cosine is -0.176 (e209) and the gain about a third of final (e210); final -0.22 / -0.34. The anti-alignment is born between step 512 and step 2000, i.e. as the learning-rate warmup (1430 steps) ends, and it LEADS the gain (cos already 70% of its final value at step 2000 while the gain is 20%), the gain then growing with the activations and the normalization scale. Block 0's positive alignment appears at the same time (+0.09 at step 2000). Note the ordering with e173: Pythia's dedicated block-5 canceller structure (a direct-contribution effect) was already present at step 1000 (b0->b5 -0.51), before the generic contraction: specific cancellation forms before generic decorrelation.
- e222 WRITE ENERGY FATE (direct test of decorrelation; 4 models so far): zeroing a token's dominant block-b write and following it with depth: the atom's readability and the along-direction footprint fall together (Qwen: +1 0.86/0.69, +2 0.68/0.46, +4 0.46/0.25, +6 0.37/0.18; OLMo: +1 0.94/0.58, +2 0.82/0.37, +4 0.55/0.20, +6 0.39/0.13; Pythia +4 0.38/0.38; GPT-2 +4 0.50/0.44) while the TOTAL footprint of the write in the state (||clean - ablated|| over the write's magnitude) stays at or above 1 and grows: Qwen 1.00 -> 1.40 -> 1.59 -> 1.75 -> 1.83 (+0 to +6), OLMo 1.00 -> 1.67 -> 2.02 -> 2.26 -> 2.36 (4.7 at the top), Pythia 1.0-1.15, GPT-2 1.0 -> 1.5. A write's consequences in the state grow with depth while its own direction, and with it its provenance, fades. Provenance fades by decorrelation, not by loss (H6h confirmed directly).
- e216 FROZEN NORMALIZATION: holding both normalizations of a block at a constant per-token scale (the median) removes the norm dependence of the gain: Spearman(gain, log norm) +0.54 -> +0.03 (Pythia), +0.47 -> -0.00 (GPT-2), +0.52 -> -0.01 (Qwen), +0.55 -> -0.10 (SmolLM2), +0.47 -> -0.01 (OLMo), so the 1/||x|| law of e213 is the normalization's per-token scale (H6c passes its kill rule). The median gain is preserved in Pythia (-0.20 vs -0.21) and reduced in GPT-2 (-0.18 -> -0.05), but the intervention moves the gated models off their operating point (SwiGLU is quadratic in input scale): Qwen's median gain becomes +242, OLMo's +18 and SmolLM2's flips to +0.19, so for the gated models only the vanishing correlation is interpretable, not the magnitude.
- e219 ZERO-SUM LEDGER: only GPT-2 loses write energy inside its blocks. Its within-block coherence ratio (energy of the MLP increment over the sum of its neurons' write energies) is 0.31-0.51 in blocks 1-9 (median 0.44) and 66% of all MLP write energy up to the mid layer survives as state. Every other model is CONSTRUCTIVE: Qwen 1.13, OLMo 1.15, Pythia 1.18, SmolLM2 1.08 (medians over blocks; block 0 at 2.1-3.0 and the last block at 1.8-4.0, where neurons write coherently), cross-block ratios 0.93-1.41 and total survival 1.08-1.67. Pythia is a GELU model too, so GPT-2's destructive interference is not the nonlinearity; it is GPT-2's own organisation (aliasing, within-block cancellation, direction-specific damping). 'Computation without state evidence' is a GPT-2 phenomenon at the block level.
- e218 HIERARCHICAL PROVENANCE (kills 'coarser labels help' for MLP writes): 'a substantial atom from the birth block is in the support' is nearly always true for OMP (0.93-1.00) but so is the control block (0.89-0.97); for the dual the gain over the control is +0.03 (OLMo), +0.10 (Pythia), +0.15 (Qwen), +0.27 (GPT-2), +0.31 (SmolLM2): below the 0.2 kill threshold in 3/5, above it in the two amplifying models. When the writer's own atom is not the support atom closest to the write direction, the closest one is from the same block only 2-5% of the time and from another block 15-51%: misses are not 'right block, wrong neuron'. Block-energy recovery is weak (Spearman 0.26-0.38 in OLMo/Pythia/GPT-2, ~0 in Qwen; shuffled ~0). Attention is different: the block-L head with the largest write is identified by an OV atom of that head in 73% (GPT-2) and 100% (Pythia) of tokens under OMP (controls 0.10 / 0.05; dual 0.20 / 0.62), OLMo 0 (no substantial OV atoms selected), Qwen undefined (no dominant head): SmolLM2 0.21 vs 0.08: head identity is observable where neuron identity is not, in the models whose OV atoms are selected at all.

SESSION 5 (round five: where does a write's energy and provenance go?).
- e223 FOOTPRINT LEDGER (exact accounting, 5 models, births 1-3): the footprint of a zeroed write decomposes exactly (identity residual 0.000) into -write + the intervening blocks' MLP responses + their attention responses. Both responses are large and partially cancel: relative to the write, at +2 the MLP-mediated part is 0.9-2.2 and the attention-mediated part 0.5-2.2; at the mid layer 1.2-3.6 and 0.85-3.4 (OLMo: 3.55 and 3.40 combine to a footprint of 2.57). The MLP part is a delta-ledger over KNOWN writers, and it is maximally diffuse: the effective number of writers is 400-1500 per intervening block, growing linearly with depth (Qwen 793 -> 7190, OLMo 1541 -> 10656, Pythia 408 -> 5776, GPT-2 200 -> 1221, SmolLM2 297 -> 3863 from +1 to the mid layer), 90% of the redistributed energy needs 1-17k neurons, the top successor carries 2-8% at +2 and 1-4% at L, and every intervening block takes an equal share (about 1/k each). About half of the redistributed energy lands on neurons already active in the clean run (0.44-0.69 at +2, 0.35-0.64 at L) and half on newly activated ones; the delta-writes are nearly incoherent (coherence 0.8-1.1 at +2, 0.64-0.86 at L). In MAHALANOBIS units (whitened by the level's covariance) the footprint is roughly conserved rather than growing: Qwen 1.11 -> 0.99, OLMo 1.77 -> 1.66, Pythia 0.94 -> 0.56, GPT-2 0.81 -> 0.69, SmolLM2 1.09 -> 0.79 (Euclidean 1.1-2.1 -> 0.8-2.6): the Euclidean growth is the footprint moving into high-variance directions. The provenance horizon (levels until the along-direction footprint halves) has a median of 2-3 and correlates with the state norm (+0.05 to +0.44) more than with the write's size (-0.08 to +0.24) or position (~0). Function: same-position dCE +0.01 to +0.08 and KL 0.002-0.014 per removed write.
- e224 COLLISION (5 models, three largest block-2 writes per token vs two random directions of the same size): distinct real writes NEVER collide: their footprints stay nearly orthogonal through depth (real-real cosine 0.01 -> 0.10 Qwen, 0.02 -> 0.11 OLMo, <= 0.04 Pythia, ~0 GPT-2 and SmolLM2; their write directions have cosine ~0) and their logit effects are only weakly related (0.06-0.17, SmolLM2 0.46). Random (off-manifold) perturbations DO converge: random-random footprint cosine rises to 0.33-0.54 in Qwen, 0.33 in SmolLM2 (at L) and their logit effects are nearly identical (GPT-2 0.92, SmolLM2 0.55, Qwen 0.48): junk is funnelled into a shared disturbance direction, real writes are kept distinct. The dynamics separate real provenance rather than merging it.
- e225 FOOTPRINT DIMENSION (kills the funnel for real writes): across tokens, the cloud of write footprints has an effective dimension as large as or larger than the writes themselves and comparable to the state's own: Qwen 82 -> 145 (writes 66, state 72-97), OLMo 112 -> 280 (writes 54, state 163-175), Pythia 63 -> 217 (writes 44, state 84-129), GPT-2 36 -> 50 (writes 20, state 46-88). Real writes spread OUT with depth. Random-direction footprints do the opposite: high-dimensional at first (Pythia 602, OLMo 319) and shrinking with depth (Pythia 352, Qwen 30 at the mid layer): off-manifold junk is funnelled, real writes are not. Real-write footprints sit a little more in the state's top-10% principal subspace than random ones (0.17-0.33 vs 0.11-0.27). SmolLM2 is uninformative here: its dominant block-2 write is one massive neuron for almost every token (write and footprint clouds of dimension 1).
- Session 5 verdict: a write's energy is fully accounted for (exact identity) as a near-maximal-entropy redistribution over thousands of later writers plus an attention part of the same size that partly cancels it; its distinguishability in whitened units is roughly conserved; its footprint stays orthogonal to other writes' footprints and does not collapse into a shared subspace; only off-manifold perturbations are funnelled. Provenance is not destroyed and not merged: it is dispersed to the point where writer identity is no longer a usable coordinate, while the network keeps distinct writes distinct.

SESSION 6 (round six: transport of descendants; binary splits).
- e226 ADDITIVITY (4 of 5 so far; block-2 writes a, c per token): descendants are approximately linear in Pythia (additivity error 0.05 -> 0.12 from +1 to the mid layer, homogeneity error 0.04 -> 0.10) and GPT-2 (0.12 -> 0.20 / 0.11 -> 0.20), mildly nonlinear in OLMo (0.11 -> 0.25 / 0.15 -> 0.26), and additive-but-inhomogeneous in Qwen: two writes' footprints add within 0.22-0.38, but halving a write gives a footprint 0.77-0.94 away from half the removal footprint: Qwen's dominant block-2 writes act through thresholds (its massive-activation neurons), so their descendants scale nonlinearly with the write's size while distinct writes still superpose. Opposing pairs interact more than reinforcing pairs (GPT-2 0.52 vs 0.20 at L, Pythia 0.23 vs 0.13).
- e229 DISPERSAL ACROSS TRAINING (Pythia, block-2 writes, levels +2 / +4): at initialisation the write is transmitted verbatim along its own direction (along 1.00 / 1.00, total 1.29 / 1.51: the identity path plus orthogonal noise from random MLPs), with no collisions (real-real 0.00, random-random 0.00) and a footprint cloud of the writes' own dimension (66). Dispersal is LEARNED: along 0.87 / 0.79 at step 2000, 0.72 / 0.53 at step 16000, ~0.64 / ~0.38 in the final model (e222/e223). The Mahalanobis footprint is 1.0 at every checkpoint (conservation of distinguishability is a property of the residual architecture, present from the start). The footprint cloud first CONTRACTS (dimension 32 / 52 at step 2000, below the writes' 66: an early funnelling phase) and then expands past the writes (76 / 144 at step 16000, 97 / 197 final). Real-write collisions stay near zero throughout (0.05-0.06).
- e226 SmolLM2: strongly non-additive (additivity error 1.25-1.94, homogeneity 0.22-0.68): its two largest block-2 writes are the massive-activation pathway, and removing both is nothing like the sum of removing each. Additivity summary, 5 models: linear transport in Pythia and GPT-2, mildly nonlinear in OLMo, additive-but-inhomogeneous in Qwen, non-additive in SmolLM2; nonlinearity tracks the massive-activation neurons.
- e229 completed (step 64000 and final): along 0.66 / 0.41 (step 64000) and 0.61 / 0.33 (final) at +2 / +4; Mahalanobis 0.97-1.09 throughout; real-real footprint cosine 0.04-0.06; cloud dimension 87 / 166 (step 64000) and 97 / 197 (final) vs state 90-133. The dispersal curve over training is monotone (along at +4: 1.00 -> 0.79 -> 0.53 -> 0.41 -> 0.33) and the early funnelling phase (dimension 32-52 at step 2000) is transient.
- e228 PATH DECOMPOSITION (4 of 5 so far; freezing later attention outputs = MLP-only transport, freezing later MLP outputs = attention-only transport): the paths are NOT additive. The interaction residual ||F - F_MLP - F_att + write|| / ||F|| is 0.35-0.92 two blocks after the write and 1.1-2.75 at the mid layer (Qwen 0.85 / 1.71, Pythia 0.35 / 2.75, GPT-2 0.67 / 1.10, OLMo 0.92 / 1.55), far above the 0.3 threshold: transport alternates between MLPs and attention and each path's response depends on the other's. Attention-only transport OVERSHOOTS the full footprint at the mid layer (1.4-2.8x the write vs the full 0.8-2.2x): the MLP responses partly cancel the attention-mediated spread (the same cancellation seen in the exact ledger, e223). MLP-only transport is the closer approximation to the descendant's direction (cos with the full footprint 0.62-0.88 at +2, 0.36-0.68 at L; attention-only 0.46-0.64 then 0.16-0.41). Split resolved: alternating, interacting transport, with the MLP chain carrying the descendant's shape and attention adding a large, partly cancelled spread.
- e228 SmolLM2: interaction residual 0.50 / 2.74, attention-only transport 9.1x the write at the mid layer (the massive-activation pathway): alternating transport in 5/5.
- e227 DESCENDANT IDENTITY (the headline of round six, 5 models): a neuron keeps a recognisable descendant signature after its own direction is gone. For neurons dominant at >= 20 tokens (18-29 neurons per birth block), the footprints of the same neuron at the mid layer have a mean pairwise cosine of 0.20-0.31 vs 0.01-0.12 between different neurons, and leave-one-out nearest-centroid identification of the SOURCE NEURON from its footprint succeeds in 88% (SmolLM2), 94% (OLMo), 97% (Qwen), 98% (Pythia, GPT-2) of tokens against a chance of 3-6%; the source BLOCK is identified in 84-95% (chance 33%). Two blocks after the write the within-neuron cosine is 0.25-0.64 and identification is 0.94-1.00. So the transformation destroys the neuron's local coordinates, not the invertibility of its provenance: the descendant is neuron-specific, and provenance is recoverable from a footprint library even where the native atom is unreadable. Logit-space provenance is weak: Spearman of the removal's KL with the write's |coefficient| is +0.06 to +0.30, with its readability at L -0.16 to +0.24, with its along-direction footprint 0.00 to +0.46: the reading does not predict behavioural influence.
- e230 DESCENDANT DICTIONARY FROM THE STATE (5 models; centroids of a neuron's footprints on half its tokens, tested on the other half, 18-29 candidate neurons per birth block): the source neuron is identified from the CENTERED STATE at the mid layer by nearest descendant centroid in 37% (GPT-2), 48% (Pythia), 53% (Qwen), 66% (OLMo; least squares 71%) and 19% (SmolLM2) of tokens against a chance of 3-4%, with the intervention footprint as the ceiling (79-99%). Against the neuron's own atom used the same way (argmax cosine over the same candidates), the descendant centroid wins in the damped, gated models (Qwen 0.53 vs 0.24, OLMo 0.66 vs 0.33, SmolLM2 0.19 vs 0.09) and loses in the GELU models where the write's own direction survives longer (GPT-2 0.37 vs 0.47, Pythia 0.48 vs 0.54). A measured descendant dictionary is therefore a usable state-level observable where the native coordinates fail fastest; it is built from interventions (a footprint library), not from weights alone.
- Session 6 verdict: transport of a write's descendants is linear in the GELU models and increasingly nonlinear where massive-activation neurons dominate (Qwen inhomogeneous, SmolLM2 non-additive); it alternates between MLPs and attention with strong interaction (paths are not additive; attention-only transport overshoots and MLPs cancel part of it); the dispersal is learned (verbatim transmission at initialisation, a transient low-dimensional phase around step 2000, then expansion); and the descendant is neuron-specific: source neuron 88-98% and source block 84-95% identifiable from the footprint, 19-66% from the state via a descendant dictionary. Provenance at depth is a coordinate change, not information loss.

SESSION 7 (round seven: attacking the descendant signature).
- e231 SIGNATURE SOURCE (5 models; block-2 neurons with >= 20 tokens, K = 25-35): the signature is mostly the WRITE VECTOR's image, not the neuron's context. Injecting a neuron's median write vector into foreign states where the neuron is inactive yields a footprint that classifies as that neuron at four blocks in 0.91 (GPT-2), 0.97 (Pythia), 0.89 (OLMo), 0.91 (SmolLM2) and 0.55 (Qwen) of foreign tokens (natural held-out: 0.91-1.00; chance 0.03), and at the mid layer in 0.71 (Pythia), 0.69 (OLMo), 0.36 (Qwen), 0.25 (SmolLM2) (natural 0.71-0.98). The magnitude does not matter (0.5x and 2x give the same accuracy everywhere: the descendant map is linear in the write's size in the transplant regime). Random directions of the same size classify at chance (0.02-0.05), though in SmolLM2 and Qwen they pile onto one 'junk' centroid (max share 0.32 / 0.18). Two neurons' vectors injected together are both recovered in the top two centroids in 0.55-0.83 of tokens at four blocks (Qwen 0.12) and 0.04-0.33 at the mid layer: signatures superpose early and blur later. Within-neuron cosine of transplanted footprints across unrelated states is 0.14-0.39 (natural 0.16-0.51). Verdict: at four blocks the identity is carried by the vector (state-conditioning costs 0.03-0.10, Qwen 0.36); at the mid layer roughly half of the natural identifiability is vector-driven and half comes from the states the neuron naturally fires in.
- e232 PROVENANCE TRANSPORT CURVE (GPT-2, Pythia so far; K = 50-55 neurons, log2 K = 5.6-5.7 bits): Pythia shows the crossover: source identification by the native atom read 0.80 / 0.74 / 0.49 / 0.31 / 0.14 / 0.00 at +1 / +2 / +4 / +6 / mid / block 22, by the descendant centroid on the STATE 0.68 / 0.57 / 0.50 / 0.39 / 0.30 / 0.08 (2.6-3.8 bits down to 1.8), by the footprint 1.00 / 1.00 / 1.00 / 0.99 / 0.96 / 0.44 (5.0 bits down to 2.5). The native coordinate dominates for the first three blocks, the descendant signature overtakes it at four to six blocks, and both fade in the last two blocks, where even the intervention footprint loses half its identity. GPT-2: the state-level descendant signature beats the native read at every depth (0.48 vs 0.33 at +1, 0.41 vs 0.43 at +6, 0.35 vs 0.33 at +8; 2.2-3.0 bits of 5.7) and the footprint carries 3.4-4.4 bits.
- e232 completed (5 models, K = 50-64 neurons, 5.6-6.0 bits): the crossover from the native coordinate to the descendant signature happens at two blocks in Qwen (native 0.63 vs state-centroid 0.72 at +2; 0.16 vs 0.49 at the mid layer, 4.0 of 6.0 bits) and at four blocks in OLMo (0.59 vs 0.70; 0.06 vs 0.27 at L) and Pythia (0.49 vs 0.50; 0.14 vs 0.30 at L); in GPT-2 the state-level signature is at least as good as the native read at every depth; SmolLM2 is the exception, its native read staying ahead until the last blocks (0.30 vs 0.15 at L, 0.02 vs 0.14 at block 28). In every model the intervention footprint keeps 4.7-5.6 bits through the mid layer and loses about half in the last two blocks (0.37-0.49 accuracy, 2.5-3.7 bits at NB-2): the final layers are where provenance is finally scrambled, presumably by the unembedding-preparing rewrite of the state.
- e233 WEIGHT-PERTURBATION ROBUSTNESS (5 models; every parameter perturbed by eps x its RMS x Gaussian noise; centroids from the unperturbed model): source identification from the footprint is unchanged up to eps = 3e-2, where the clean state has already moved by 8-15% and the loss by +0.01 to +0.5 nats (GPT-2 0.97 -> 0.96, Pythia 0.99 -> 0.99, Qwen 0.89 -> 0.89, OLMo 0.99 -> 0.98, SmolLM2 0.73 -> 0.70), and survives partially even at eps = 0.1, where the state has moved by 28-56% and the model is degraded or destroyed (dCE +0.1 to +7.2): Pythia 0.98, OLMo 0.98, GPT-2 0.87, Qwen 0.56, SmolLM2 0.48. The signature is a robust property of the linearised dynamics around the trained weights, not a coincidence of their exact values.
- e235 PATH IDENTITY (GPT-2, Pythia so far): both paths carry the identity nearly fully. Footprints transported with the later attention frozen (MLP-only) classify against the full-footprint centroids at 1.00 / 0.99, and with the later MLPs frozen (attention-only) at 0.94 / 0.91, although attention-only transport overshoots and points far from the full footprint (cos 0.16-0.41 at the mid layer, e228): attention rewrites the vector but preserves the source identity.
- e235 completed (5 models): MLP-only transport preserves the identity everywhere (0.68-1.00 against the full-footprint centroids, 0.89-1.00 self-consistently). Attention-only transport preserves it in GPT-2, Pythia and OLMo (0.91-0.94) but produces a DIFFERENT neuron-specific signature in the gated models: against the full centroids 0.15 (Qwen) and 0.10 (SmolLM2), yet self-consistent at 0.88 and 0.62. Identity is carried redundantly by both paths; the MLP chain carries the signature the full footprint has, attention carries its own version of it.
- e234 CROSS-CORPUS (GPT-2; 15 neurons dominant on both WikiText and the Pile, chance 0.07): centroids fitted on WikiText identify the source neuron on Pile tokens in 0.95 of cases without refitting (within-corpus held-out 0.98 / 0.91), and Pile-fitted centroids identify WikiText sources in 0.83. The signature is not contextual activation statistics of one corpus.
- e234 SmolLM2 (12 shared neurons, chance 0.08): within WikiText 0.61, within Pile 0.45; WikiText -> Pile 0.35, Pile -> WikiText 0.27. Transfer is weak but four times chance; SmolLM2's signatures are the weakest in every test of this round.
- Session 7 verdict: the descendant signature is largely the deterministic image of the write vector under the network's transport (transplants into foreign states classify at 0.89-0.97 four blocks out), linear in the write's size, robust to 3-10% weight noise, portable across corpora in GPT-2 (0.95) and weakly in SmolLM2 (0.35), carried redundantly by the MLP chain and by attention (attention carrying its own version in the gated models), and readable from the state after a crossover at two to four blocks; at the mid layer half of its natural identifiability is state-conditioning; and it is destroyed, with everything else, in the final two blocks.

SESSION 8 (round eight: transported WDD, capacity, resolution and geometry, cross-checkpoint).
- e238 SEPARATION (GPT-2, Pythia so far): transport is ANGLE-PRESERVING on injected pairs. A write vector and a rotated copy with initial cosine 0.99 / 0.95 / 0.90 / 0.75 / 0.50 / 0.00 have descendant cosines 0.99 / 0.95 / 0.89 / 0.73 / 0.49 / 0.03 (GPT-2) and 0.99 / 0.95 / 0.90 / 0.75 / 0.50 / 0.01 (Pythia) at every level from +1 to the mid layer: the descendant map is an isometry on the injected subspace, so there is no resolution limit beyond the initial separation. For natural descendants (which carry state-conditioning) the geometry is partially preserved: Spearman between the pairwise cosine matrices of write vectors and of descendant centroids +0.55 (GPT-2, 24 neurons) and +0.31 (Pythia, 29), 5-nearest-neighbour overlap 0.45 and 0.34 against chance 0.22 and 0.18.
- e238 completed (5 models): angle preservation is exact within +-0.02 in ALL five models (Qwen 0.99 / 0.94 / 0.89 / 0.73 / 0.48 / 0.02, OLMo 0.99 / 0.95 / 0.90 / 0.76 / 0.51 / 0.04, SmolLM2 0.99 / 0.95 / 0.90 / 0.75 / 0.51 / 0.02 at the mid layer for initial cosines 0.99 to 0): within a token's state the descendant map is an isometry on injected directions, in the gated models too. The geometry of NATURAL descendant centroids follows the write vectors' geometry only in the GELU models (Spearman +0.55 / +0.31, 5-NN overlap above chance) and not in the gated ones (Qwen +0.07, OLMo +0.01, SmolLM2 -0.09; overlap at chance): there the centroid geometry is dominated by the contexts the neurons fire in, not by their write vectors, consistent with the transplant gap of e231.
- e236 TRANSPORTED WDD, GPT-2 (kill-test, first model): the transported dictionary is WORSE than the native one. Level 4: dominant recall dual 0.91 -> 0.73, OMP 0.73 -> 0.56; level 6 (mid): dual 0.89 -> 0.62, OMP 0.69 -> 0.46; top-3 recall 0.97 -> 0.87; real share of the support 0.29 -> 0.26. In GPT-2 the write's own direction survives and is amplified, so the native atom is already the right coordinate and the token-averaged transported images add noise.
- e236 Pythia: transported matches native at level 4 (dual 0.89 -> 0.86, OMP 0.81 -> 0.79) and loses at the mid layer (dual 0.68 -> 0.48, OMP 0.53 -> 0.36, top-3 0.90 -> 0.74). Two models, two failures of the kill-test so far.
- e237 CAPACITY (GPT-2, Pythia, Qwen, OLMo): provenance bits from the STATE grow with log2 K without saturating up to K = 128 candidates: Qwen 5.6 bits at +1 and 5.1 at +4 (of 7), OLMo 5.1 / 4.9, Pythia 4.4 / 4.0, GPT-2 2.9 / 3.0 at K = 64 (of 6); the footprint carries 6.0-6.4 bits at K = 128 (near the 7-bit ceiling) at every depth to +6. The residual stream supports at least 128 distinguishable sources at the mid layer in the descendant coordinates; the limit was the number of neurons with enough tokens, not the capacity.
- e236 Qwen: transported equals native, no gain: level 4 dual 0.94 -> 0.92, OMP 0.95 -> 0.90; mid layer dual 0.51 -> 0.49, OMP 0.50 -> 0.42, top-3 0.76 -> 0.75, real share 0.22 -> 0.15. Three models, no improvement over the native dictionary anywhere.
- e237 SmolLM2: state-level provenance collapses at its mid layer (0.2-0.8 bits at K = 8-128, against 4.5 bits at +1 and 4.4 footprint bits at the mid layer): the one model whose state-level signature is unreadable at depth, consistent with every earlier SmolLM2 result.
- e239 CROSS-CHECKPOINT TRANSPLANT (Pythia final vs step 16000; 22 shared neurons, chance 0.05): the two checkpoints' block-2 write vectors have cosine 0.74 and their descendant centroids 0.54. The final model's write vectors pushed through the step-16000 network produce descendants classified correctly by the final model's centroids (1.00) and by the step-16000 model's own (1.00); the step-16000 vectors through the final network are classified by the step-16000 centroids at 0.99 and by the final centroids at 0.93. The signature follows the vector, and the transport is already stable at step 16000 (a different downstream network yields a descendant either checkpoint recognises).
- e240 Pythia (why transported WDD does not beat native): the transported dictionary is not the problem as a dictionary. Transported block-2 atoms keep cosine 0.30 with their native atoms; their Babel-1 coherence is 0.16 against 0.14 for the native atoms (transported random directions 0.17); the top principal direction holds 1.7% of their energy against 1.3%; the effective dimension is 488 against 577; the overlap with the shared random-footprint direction is 0.05 against 0.02. The token-averaged image is simply a blurred version of each token's actual descendant (context-specific part plus cross-token interference), sharp enough to classify among tens of candidates (e230-e232) but not to win a 64-sparse competition among 50,000 atoms.
- e236 completed (5 models): transported WDD is killed in 5/5. OLMo: dominant recall dual 0.94 -> 0.67 at level 4 and 0.82 -> 0.37 at the mid layer (OMP 0.73 -> 0.43, top-3 0.95 -> 0.68); SmolLM2: 0.99 -> 0.97 and 0.78 -> 0.74. Worse in four models, equal in Qwen, better nowhere.
- e240 completed (5 models): transported atoms keep cosine 0.20-0.68 with their native atoms (GPT-2 0.68, SmolLM2 0.42, Pythia 0.30, OLMo 0.21, Qwen 0.20). The transported dictionary is more coherent and lower-dimensional in the gated models Qwen (Babel-1 0.15 -> 0.23, effective dimension 639 -> 275) and SmolLM2 (0.17 -> 0.27, 328 -> 138), matching the coherence of transported random directions (0.25, 0.28), and not in GPT-2, Pythia or OLMo (0.21 -> 0.19, 0.14 -> 0.16, 0.16 -> 0.13), where it still fails: the context-averaged image is too blurred to serve as a sparse-coding atom regardless of coherence.
- Session 8 verdict: transport is an exact isometry within a state and the descendant coordinate carries more than seven bits of source identity, portable across corpora and training checkpoints; but a transported dictionary cannot replace the native one for sparse decomposition. The descendant chart is a classification coordinate, the native dictionary remains the decomposition basis, and the last two blocks read in neither.

SESSION 9 (round nine: compositionality, zero-shot, phase diagram, linearisation).
- e241 GPT-2 (K = 24 candidates): the provenance code is compositional with graceful degradation. Injecting m distinct write vectors at once, the additivity error against the two halves injected separately is 0.22 / 0.30 / 0.39 / 0.49 at the mid layer for m = 2 / 4 / 8 / 16 (below the 0.5 kill line up to 16), the mixture's norm scales as sqrt(m) times a single write (1.05-1.29), and the injected identities are recovered from the mixture's footprint at recall@m 0.88 / 0.77 / 0.68 / 0.66 / 0.77 for m = 1 / 2 / 4 / 8 / 16 (chance 0.04 / 0.08 / 0.17 / 0.33 / 0.67) at the mid layer and 0.79-0.95 two blocks after the write: eight simultaneous writes remain individually decodable at twice chance.
- e243 Pythia step 0 (the initial point of the phase diagram): native readability is absent (dual dominant recall 0.01 at +4, 0.00 at +10: the writes are tiny against the embedding), while transport is the identity (along-component 1.00, pair cosine 0.75 -> 0.75, footprint cloud dimension 70 = the writes' own), the footprint carries 4.6 of 5 bits at every level including the penultimate block, and the state carries 3.1 bits.
- e241 completed (5 models): additivity error at the mid layer for m = 2 / 4 / 8 / 16 simultaneous writes: GPT-2 0.22 / 0.30 / 0.39 / 0.49, Pythia 0.14 / 0.20 / 0.28 / 0.37, SmolLM2 0.18 / 0.25 / 0.36 / 0.47, Qwen 0.24 / 0.34 / 0.46 / 0.60, OLMo 0.21 / 0.32 / 0.50 / 0.68: the code is additive within 0.5 up to eight writes in 5/5 (OLMo at the line) and up to sixteen in the GELU models and SmolLM2. Recovery of the injected identities from the mixture two blocks after the write is 0.61-0.88 at m = 8 (chance 0.28-0.38) in all five; at the mid layer it is 0.66 (GPT-2), 0.53 (Pythia), 0.56 (OLMo) against chance 0.28-0.38, and near chance in Qwen (0.40 vs 0.29) and SmolLM2 (0.40 vs 0.35), whose single-write state-level identification is already weak there (0.32, 0.23). Compositional provenance holds for at least eight simultaneous writes over two blocks everywhere and to the mid layer in the models whose descendant chart survives that far.
- e242 ZERO-SHOT (GPT-2, Pythia so far): a neuron's natural footprints are identified from the transplant image of its write vector alone, with no natural footprint of that neuron used: GPT-2 0.93 / 0.83 (blocks +2 / mid layer, born b2) and 0.87 / 0.77 (born b3) against standard natural-centroid accuracy 1.00 / 0.97 and 0.99 / 0.99; Pythia 1.00 / 0.94 and 0.99 / 0.97 against 1.00 / 0.98-0.99. The transplant image and the natural centroid have cosine 0.44-0.73. The descendant of an unseen neuron is predictable from its write vector: the 'decoder' is the network's own transport.
- e243 PHASE DIAGRAM (Pythia steps 0 / 1000 / 4000 / 16000 so far; block-2 writes): native dual readability at +4 rises 0.01 -> 0.18 -> 0.38 -> 0.40 (writes become prominent with training); the along-direction survival at +4 falls 1.00 -> 0.97 -> 0.64 -> 0.53 (dispersal is learned); an injected pair with initial cosine 0.75 keeps 0.75 / 0.79 / 0.77 / 0.76 (the isometry is there from initialisation); provenance bits (K = 32) from the footprint at +4 go 4.6 -> 3.9 -> 3.4 -> 4.2 and from the state 3.1 -> 2.5 -> 2.2 -> 2.8 (a dip at step 4000, when the footprint cloud transiently contracts to dimension 58 from 70-73, then recovery with expansion to 162); at the penultimate block the footprint bits fall 4.6 -> 3.9 -> 3.3 -> 3.8 (final-layer scrambling develops with training). Ordering: identity transport and isometry (architectural, step 0) -> readability and dispersal (warmup end) -> transient funnel (step 4000) -> expanded descendant chart (16000+) -> final-layer scrambling (grows throughout).
- e242 completed (5 models): zero-shot identification from the transplant image equals the natural-centroid standard two blocks after the write in every model (0.93-1.00 vs 0.93-1.00) and at the mid layer for Pythia (0.94 / 0.97 for births 2 / 3), OLMo (0.96 / 0.91), GPT-2 (0.83 / 0.77), and for block-3 writes in Qwen (0.94) and SmolLM2 (0.86); it fails at the mid layer only for the block-2 writes of Qwen (0.44 vs 0.85, image-centroid cosine 0.08) and SmolLM2 (0.39 vs 0.71, 0.08), which are their massive-activation neurons with nonlinear, context-dependent transport (e226). Outside those neurons an unseen neuron's descendant is predicted by its write vector alone.
- e243 completed (steps 64000 and final): native readability at +4 plateaus at about 0.4 by step 4000-16000 and declines slightly as dispersal continues (0.37 at 64000, 0.32 final); the along-direction survival keeps falling (0.41, 0.34); the isometry holds (pair cosine 0.75 at every checkpoint); footprint bits at +4 recover fully (4.5, 4.7 of 5) and state bits to 2.9; the final-layer scrambling is a LATE-training phenomenon: footprint bits at the penultimate block go 4.6 (init) -> 3.9 -> 3.3 -> 3.8 -> 3.3 -> 2.3 (final), and the footprint cloud dimension at +4 grows to 186 and 223 (state 142-149).
- e244 LINEARISATION VARIABILITY (kills the gating mechanism): the context variability of the MLP's first-order factor does not separate GELU from SwiGLU. Relative deviation of the factor vector from its token mean: GPT-2 1.23, Pythia 1.86 (GELU) vs Qwen 1.16, OLMo 1.30, SmolLM2 1.46 (SwiGLU); cosine with the mean 0.53-0.66; nearly every neuron's factor changes sign across tokens in every model. The gated models' context-dominated centroid geometry is not explained by a more variable linearisation; e242 points instead at their massive-activation neurons, whose descendants alone are unpredictable from the write vector.
- Session 9 verdict: the provenance code is compositional (eight simultaneous writes decodable at two blocks in 5/5, additive within 0.5), the descendant of an unseen neuron is predicted by its write vector alone except for the massive-activation neurons, the training phase diagram orders identity transport and isometry (architectural) before readability and dispersal (warmup end), a transient funnel (step 4000), an expanded descendant chart (16000+) and late final-layer scrambling; and the GELU-vs-gated split is not a linearisation-variability effect.

SESSION 10 (self-directed round: the recoverability frontier and a data-free chart).
- e245 GPT-2 (K = 64 candidates, zero-shot transplant images): the COHERENT FRACTION of transport (energy of a vector's context-independent mean image over the mean energy of its single-context images) is 0.46 two blocks after the write and 0.29 at the mid layer, and identical for random unit vectors (0.49 / 0.31): the context-dependence of transport is a property of the network, not of the neurons. Mixtures: with heavy-tailed amplitudes (1/i) the LARGEST injected write is identified from the mixture's descendant at the mid layer in 1.00 / 0.92 / 0.93 / 0.92 of tokens for k = 1 / 4 / 16 / 64 simultaneous writes (chance 0.02); with equal amplitudes the whole injected set is recovered at identity@k 1.00 / 0.94 / 0.77 for k = 1 / 4 / 16. The NATIVE dictionary reads the largest injected write at 0.01 (dual) at every k including k = 1 (OMP 0.28 at +2, 0.03 at the mid layer): an injected write is not maintained by its context the way a natural dominant write is, and in GPT-2 the prior-dominated dual cannot see it, while its descendant identity is perfect. Random unit vectors behave exactly like neuron writes (top-1 1.00 at k = 1, 0.07 vs 0.06 at k = 16 equal): the code is vector-specific, not neuron-specific.
- e245 Pythia (K = 64): coherent fraction 0.35 at +2 and only 0.07 at the mid layer (random vectors 0.45 / 0.12), yet the largest of k heavy-tailed writes is identified from the mixture's descendant at 0.97 / 0.87 / 0.86 / 0.84 for k = 1 / 4 / 16 / 64, and equal-amplitude sets at identity@k 0.97 / 0.75 / 0.60: a 7% context-independent component of the descendant is enough for 64-way identification. The native dual read of the largest injected write is 0.00 at every k at the mid layer, in the model whose native read of NATURAL dominant writes there is 0.68 (e236): an injected write is not re-written by its context, its along-direction component decays as the contraction predicts (about 0.75^9), and the frozen dictionary has nothing left to read, while the transported identity persists. Native readability of natural writes at depth is a maintenance effect; transported identity is not.
- e247 Pythia (closes the loop on the native failure): the native chart reads an injected write exactly when it is prominent. Two blocks after injection the native dual recall of the injected atom is 0.06 / 0.52 / 0.99 / 1.00 at 1x / 2x / 4x / 8x the neuron's median natural coefficient (prominence 0.06 / 0.11 / 0.22 / 0.42; OMP the same), while zero-shot identity from the descendant is 1.00 at every magnitude; at the mid layer native recall is 0.00 / 0.01 / 0.02 / 0.24 (prominence 0.02-0.08) and identity 0.97-0.98. The foreign tokens' own natural dominant writes (a less prominent population, prominence 0.07 / 0.03) are read natively at 0.19 / 0.01, the same as an injected write of the same prominence. So the k = 1 native failure in e245 is the prominence law, not foreignness: the native chart is a prominence-gated read of the along-direction component, which needs magnitude or context maintenance; the descendant chart reads identity from the transported image regardless of magnitude or prominence.
- e247 GPT-2: the same prominence gating: native dual recall of the injected write 0.01 / 0.20 / 0.80 / 0.98 at 1x / 2x / 4x / 8x two blocks out (prominence 0.07 / 0.14 / 0.30 / 0.58; OMP 0.28 / 0.79 / 0.95 / 0.98) and 0.01 / 0.07 / 0.52 / 0.93 at the mid layer, with identity 0.99-1.00 throughout; the foreign tokens' own dominant writes (prominence 0.17-0.19) are read at 0.38-0.41, matching an injected write between 2x and 4x.
- e245 Qwen (K = 64): coherent fraction 0.27 / 0.14 (random vectors 0.35 / 0.23); the largest of k heavy-tailed writes identified from the mixture at the mid layer at 0.97 / 0.77 / 0.72 / 0.72 for k = 1 / 4 / 16 / 64; equal-amplitude identity@k 0.97 / 0.69 / 0.58; native dual 0.00 at every k; random vectors identical to neuron writes (1.00 / 0.06).
- e247 Qwen and OLMo: prominence gating in both. Qwen native dual 0.05 / 0.42 / 0.95 / 1.00 two blocks out at 1x-8x (mid layer 0.00 / 0.01 / 0.10 / 0.46), OLMo 0.10 / 0.74 / 0.99 / 1.00 (mid layer 0.00 / 0.02 / 0.28 / 0.98), identity 0.97-1.00 at every magnitude in both; natural writes of the same prominence read the same (OLMo reference 0.69 at prominence 0.14, between the 2x and 4x injections).
- e245 SmolLM2 (K = 64): coherent fraction 0.47 / 0.10 (random 0.46 / 0.08); heavy-tailed top-1 from the mixture 0.88 / 0.58 / 0.48 / 0.46 for k = 1 / 4 / 16 / 64 (chance 0.02); equal identity@k 0.88 / 0.54 / 0.49; native dual 0.01-0.03. e245 OLMo was still running at close (its partial log showed equal k = 16 at +2: identity@k 0.95, native dual 0.28, OMP 0.64).
- e246 DATA-FREE CHART, Pythia: transplant images built on uniformly random token sequences identify natural WikiText footprints at 1.00 two blocks out and 0.93 at the mid layer, against 1.00 / 0.95 for text-built images and 1.00 / 0.99 for fitted natural centroids (chance 0.03); random-token and text images have cosine 0.91 / 0.77. The descendant chart needs the model and nothing else. The other four models' e246 runs were queued at close and may finish on the box.
- CLOSE (user: 'I think its done'): ten rounds, e01 to e247, 893 result files synced. The program's summary is the 'What the whole program established' section of SYNTHESIS.md.

SESSION 11 (self-directed, from the reviewer list: characterise the transported object).
- CROSSOVER FROM GAINS (local analysis of e215 gains and e232 curves): the passive along-direction survival predicted by the product of single-block gains, prod(1+g_j) from block 3 to the level, falls below 0.5 at level 6 in Pythia, 4 in Qwen, 4 in OLMo, 6 in GPT-2 and 8 in SmolLM2; the observed level where the state-level descendant chart overtakes the native read is 6 (Pythia), 4 (Qwen), 6 (OLMo), 3 (GPT-2) and 28 (SmolLM2). The weights-and-activations quantity predicts the crossover exactly in Pythia and Qwen and within one measured level in OLMo; it fails where the charts themselves are anomalous: GPT-2's prior-dominated dual loses from the first block, and SmolLM2's state-level descendant signature is too weak to win before the last blocks.
- e250 MASSIVE-ACTIVATION BOUNDARY (Qwen, SmolLM2, GPT-2, Pythia so far): the massive neurons are transported linearly and angle-preservingly like any other vector (homogeneity 0.15-0.45 vs 0.15-0.47 for matched normal neurons; pair cosine 0.74-0.77 vs 0.75-0.83), but their NATURAL descendants are not their transported images. SmolLM2 (massive |coef| 69.6 vs 5.3): two blocks out the massive write's transport is almost context-free (coherent fraction 0.88 vs 0.34) and its transplant image matches its natural centroid (cos 0.99) while native reading of it is trivial (1.00); at the mid layer the transplant image is ANTI-aligned with the natural centroid (cos -0.75 vs -0.18 for normals) and the massive neuron's natural footprints are barely identifiable (0.13 vs 0.92). Qwen (3.5 vs 1.6): the transplant image is anti-aligned with the natural centroid already at two blocks (-0.45 vs -0.14), although natural identification still works (1.00). GPT-2 and Pythia have no massive outlier (10.3 vs 7.6, 2.2 vs 1.5) and show no difference. The massive neurons' natural effect runs through a context mechanism (the sink) that a transplanted copy of their vector does not trigger; they are the boundary of vector-determined transport, not a failure of it.
- e250 OLMo (massive |coef| 0.7 vs 0.5, a mild outlier): the same signature in weaker form: transplant image anti-aligned with the natural centroid at two blocks (-0.51 vs +0.05), natural identification intact (1.00 / 0.97), native reading of the injected write higher (0.61 vs 0.06, it is simply larger). Five models: the largest neuron's natural descendant systematically points away from its transplanted image (Qwen -0.45, OLMo -0.51, SmolLM2 -0.75 at the mid layer), while every other transport statistic is ordinary.
- e249 MINIMUM DIMENSION and LOCATION (5 models): provenance is not low-dimensional. Reaching 90% of full nearest-centroid accuracy from the footprint needs 32-64 random projection dimensions two blocks out (8-32 principal components of the footprint cloud) and 128-256 at the mid layer (32-128 components); from the state 32-128 random dimensions (16-128 components). LOCATION: within each block the along-direction component of the write is unchanged by the attention residual add (block 3: 1.00 -> 1.00 / 0.94 / 0.96 / 1.01 / 1.00 in the five models) and drops at the MLP add (-> 0.78 / 0.64 / 0.55 / 0.83 / 0.93), block after block (Qwen 1.00 -> 0.64 -> 0.44 -> 0.33 -> 0.28 at the outputs of blocks 3-6); the footprint identity is untouched by either sub-operation at every block through block 6 (0.91-1.00 at input, after attention and at output). The native coordinate dies one MLP at a time; the descendant coordinate does not die at any sub-operation in the middle of the network.
- e248 LINEAR TRANSPORT OPERATOR (5 models; ridge map fitted on 1024 random unit directions injected at the block-3 input): (A) it predicts the mean descendant of held-out random directions with cosine 0.58-0.94 two blocks out and 0.41-0.73 at the mid layer (global-mean baseline ~0; per-token cosine 0.34-0.71 then 0.15-0.39); (E) it preserves the Gram matrix of held-out directions partially (Spearman 0.51-0.82 two blocks out, 0.38-0.57 at the mid layer; relative Frobenius error 0.20-0.40 then 0.35-0.58); (D) it COMPOSES: the operator 2->4 followed by 4->6 identifies natural footprints at level 6 as well as the direct 2->6 operator (Pythia 1.00 vs 0.99, GPT-2 0.72 vs 0.68, SmolLM2 0.41 vs 0.41, OLMo 0.28 vs 0.31; Qwen at chance in both), with composed and direct predictions at cosine 0.54-0.90; (F) a+b: injecting unit(a+b) is classified as the a+b image in 0.83-0.96 of tokens against 0.02-0.08 for a or b, in 5/5: the network transports vectors, not neuron identities. (B, C) Zero-shot provenance of NATURAL footprints by the random-fitted operator (predicted descendants, or the inverse map back to write space) works in the GELU models (Pythia 1.00 / 0.99 / 0.59 and inverse 1.00 / 1.00 / 0.80 at +2 / +4 / mid layer; GPT-2 0.88 / 0.68 and 0.86 / 0.72), partially in SmolLM2 (0.42) and OLMo (0.36), and not in Qwen (0.11 -> chance). These track the footprint's residual alignment with the native atom (Pythia 1.00 / 0.85, Qwen 0.15 / 0.02): in the gated models a map fitted on random (off-manifold) directions captures the junk funnel of e224, not the transport of real writes, which e242 showed is predictable from the atoms' own injected images. The inverse map identifies the source without reconstructing the vector (cos(U F, w) 0.10-0.43): decodable, not linearly invertible.
- Session 11 verdict: the transported object is a distributed (tens to hundreds of dimensions), compositional, vector-specific image, produced by a transport that is one linear map for all directions in the GELU models and direction-class-dependent in the gated ones; the native coordinate is lost at each MLP add and never at the attention add; the crossover depth follows from the block gains where the charts behave; and the massive-activation neurons bound the phenomenon because their natural effect runs through the sink mechanism rather than through vector transport.
- CLOSE OF ROUND 11: e01-e250, 913 result files, 194 scripts, all synced.

SESSION 12 (from the reviewer's six-experiment program; four run, two folded or not runnable).
- e252 JOINT PHASE PROFILE (5 models, every level from the write to the last block): the regimes are STAGGERED, not one transition. The along-direction component halves at level 4 (Qwen, OLMo), 5 (Pythia), 7 (GPT-2), 8 (SmolLM2); the native K-way read of the state halves at 11-16 (never in GPT-2); the state-level descendant identity halves at 10-16; the intervention footprint's identity halves only in the last one to three blocks (Pythia 20 of 24, Qwen 23 of 24, SmolLM2 29 of 30) or never (GPT-2, OLMo). Angle preservation of injected pairs NEVER breaks: an initial cosine of 0.75 is 0.73-0.76 at every level to the last block in all five models, so the late loss of identity is a loss of the natural descendant's distinctness (context and norm effects), not of the isometry. The additivity defect doubles right after the first block (from ~0.1 to ~0.2 by level 3) and then stays. Whitened footprint energy is conserved to the mid layer and then grows in the amplifying models (GPT-2 1.0 -> 2.6 at the last block, Qwen -> 8.5, SmolLM2 -> 9.6), dips and recovers in Pythia (0.61 at level 14, 0.98 at 23), stays ~1 in OLMo: the mid-layer conservation is not a global invariant. The state-level descendant centroid beats the native-atom argmax on the state from the write level onward in this K-way form (all five), consistent with e230.
- e254 FUNCTIONAL BRIDGE (5 models): the logit footprint of a write (same-position change of the logits when the write is removed) identifies its source neuron at 0.52-0.60 against chance 0.03-0.05, and DESCENDANT geometry predicts functional geometry better than write-vector geometry in 5/5: Spearman between the neurons' logit-footprint Gram and their descendant-centroid Gram +0.61 (Qwen, mid layer), +0.57 (GPT-2), +0.41 (SmolLM2), +0.28 (OLMo), +0.23 (Pythia) against +0.08, +0.37, +0.00, -0.02, +0.10 for the write vectors. Per token, the descendant's nearest neuron agrees with the logit footprint's nearest neuron in 0.49-0.60 of tokens, the native atom's in 0.02-0.21 in the gated models and 0.48-0.60 in the GELU models. Provenance does not predict a write's importance (sessions 6-7) but the descendant predicts what kind of behavioural effect the write has.
- e251 SPARSE VS DENSE CODING at equal budget (5 models): at birth the native code is perfect and sparse (8 native atoms: FVU 0.00 vs 0.31-0.47 for the descendant PCA basis and 0.73-0.97 for the state's PCA); downstream neither code reconstructs the descendant well (mid layer, 8 coefficients: native FVU 0.53-0.84, descendant PCA 0.64-0.83, equal within 0.1), because the descendant is high-dimensional (e249); but for PROVENANCE at equal budget the dense descendant basis wins at depth: with 32 coefficients at the mid layer, source identification is 0.70 (Qwen) / 0.84 (Pythia) / 0.90 (OLMo) / 0.75 (GPT-2) by the descendant basis against 0.03 / 0.56 / 0.67 / 0.55 by the native sparse code and 0.49-0.66 by 32 random directions; at birth all three give 1.00. SmolLM2 is degenerate here (its massive neuron carries almost all footprint energy, FVU ~0.01 for every basis). Sparse at birth, distributed downstream: for identity the recoding is real; for reconstruction no small code works at depth.
- e253 MASSIVE-NEURON STATE VARIABLE (Qwen, SmolLM2, OLMo): no within-token variable explains the massive neuron's context dependence. The cosine between its transplanted descendant and its natural centroid is flat across quartiles of the neuron's own natural activation (Qwen +0.20 / +0.21 / +0.20 / +0.20 two blocks out; SmolLM2 +0.95 at every quartile two blocks out and -0.16 to -0.19 at the mid layer; OLMo +0.16 to +0.17), uncorrelated with that activation (Spearman -0.06 to +0.11), and only mildly related to the state norm (-0.47 to +0.32) and position (-0.25 to +0.18); a matched normal neuron behaves the same way. At the mid layer the transplant's cosine with the natural descendant is ~0 (Qwen +0.01, OLMo +0.02) or negative (SmolLM2 -0.18) in EVERY context: an injection at one token never reproduces the massive neuron's natural effect, whose route is cross-token (the sink it creates changes other positions' attention), not a state variable at the injected token.
- Session 12 verdict: the regimes are staggered rather than a single phase transition and the isometry never breaks; downstream the descendant is sparse at birth and distributed later for provenance, high-dimensional for reconstruction; descendant geometry predicts functional geometry better than neuron identity in 5/5; and the massive neurons' exception is cross-token, outside any single-token transport picture.
- CLOSE OF ROUND 12: e01-e254, 931+ result files, 198 scripts.

SESSION 13 (from the reviewer's five: functional neighbours, functional additivity, receiver provenance, coupling over training).
