- e255 FUNCTIONAL NEIGHBOURS (5 models, K = 21-29): neurons that are neighbours in DESCENDANT space have more similar logit footprints than neighbours in write space in 5/5 (mean logit-footprint cosine with the 3 nearest neighbours: GPT-2 0.50 vs 0.31, Qwen 0.54 vs 0.41, OLMo 0.40 vs 0.30, SmolLM2 0.49 vs 0.32, Pythia 0.15 vs 0.12) and than random neighbours in 4/5 (random 0.38 / 0.39 / 0.30 / 0.34 / 0.18; Pythia's descendant neighbours are no better than random, and in GPT-2 and Pythia write neighbours are WORSE than random). Pairs whose write vectors are dissimilar but whose descendants are similar have functional similarity 0.41 / 0.51 / 0.42 / 0.49 / 0.18 against 0.13 / 0.31 / 0.26 / 0.21 / 0.05 for dissimilar-write dissimilar-descendant pairs: functional convergence without convergence at birth, in 5/5. Held-out prediction by nearest-neighbour transfer (a held-out neuron's logit footprint predicted from its 3 nearest training neurons): descendant neighbours beat write and random neighbours in Qwen (0.66 vs 0.50 / 0.52), SmolLM2 (0.50 vs 0.41 / 0.43) and Pythia (0.34 vs 0.28 / 0.30) and not in GPT-2 (0.62 vs 0.61 / 0.61) or OLMo (0.49 vs 0.45 / 0.49), where the shared component of logit footprints already gives random neighbours a high baseline.
- e256 FUNCTIONAL ADDITIVITY (5 models): the logit footprint of m simultaneously injected writes composes exactly as well as the state descendant does (additivity error at m = 2 / 4 / 8: GPT-2 0.20 / 0.28 / 0.38 vs state 0.19 / 0.25 / 0.34; Pythia 0.17 / 0.24 / 0.34 vs 0.15 / 0.21 / 0.30; Qwen 0.27 / 0.35 / 0.45 vs 0.26 / 0.36 / 0.49; OLMo 0.25 / 0.40 / 0.58 vs 0.23 / 0.36 / 0.56; SmolLM2 0.20 / 0.30 / 0.39 vs 0.19 / 0.28 / 0.37), so the chain write -> descendant -> logit effect is compositional end to end. Identity, however, is far less legible in function space: a single injected write's logit footprint identifies it at 0.15-0.52 (state descendant 0.88-1.00), and from an 8-write mixture the logit footprint recovers identities at chance while the state recovers them at 0.65-0.89.
- e257 RECEIVER PROVENANCE (kills the cross-token route for the massive neurons): ablating the massive neuron only at its most active position leaves 0.89 (Qwen), 0.99 (OLMo) and 1.00 (SmolLM2) of the footprint energy at that position, with 0-3 receiver positions above 10% of the source norm; where receivers exist they do not identify it (Qwen 0.07, chance 0.03). The massive neurons' effect is token-local at the mid layer; why their natural descendant differs from their transplanted one remains unexplained (not their own activation, e253; not cross-token). A side result: in GPT-2 the effect of a write DOES spread across positions and stays identifiable there (a normal neuron: 25% of its footprint energy at other positions, 6 receivers per sequence, receivers identify it at 0.46 against chance 0.03; the largest neuron 0.38); in Pythia the spread is wide (20 receivers) but not identifiable (0.04); in the gated models receivers identify at 0.01-0.19. Cross-position provenance exists and is model-specific.
- e258 COUPLING OVER TRAINING (Pythia, steps 0 / 4000 / 16000 / 64000 / final): the descendant-logit geometry coupling is HIGHEST at initialisation (Gram Spearman +0.84 vs +0.40 for write-logit), when the network is nearly linear and the logit footprint is a linear image of the descendant; training decouples them (+0.37, +0.18, +0.14) as nonlinear computation grows downstream, and the coupling partly returns by the end (+0.31 vs +0.10). Descendant neighbours are more functionally similar than write neighbours at every checkpoint except 64000. Functional organisation does not emerge after dispersal; a trivial linear coupling exists at initialisation, is broken by training, and is partly rebuilt.
- Session 13 verdict: the descendant coordinate organises neurons by downstream function (neighbours in descendant space share logit footprints; convergence in function without convergence at birth), the whole chain write -> descendant -> logit effect composes for up to eight simultaneous writes, identity is legible in the state and only weakly in function, the massive neurons' anomaly is neither a within-token variable nor a cross-token route, and the descendant-function coupling is a property training reshapes rather than creates.

SESSION 14 (from the reviewer's six: matched pairs, depthwise geometry, compensation neighbourhood, interchangeability, functional dimensionality).
- e259 MATCHED PAIRS (5 models): descendant similarity predicts functional similarity after controlling for write similarity, and write similarity does not after controlling for descendant similarity: partial Spearman of D-similarity with logit-footprint similarity given W-similarity +0.41 (GPT-2), +0.29 (Pythia), +0.60 (Qwen), +0.31 (OLMo), +0.53 (SmolLM2) against +0.16, +0.01, +0.05, -0.06, +0.01 for W given D. In the 2x2 design the functional similarity follows the descendant cell, not the write cell, in every model (Qwen: hiW/hiD 0.48, loW/hiD 0.44, hiW/loD 0.30, loW/loD 0.33; SmolLM2 0.38 / 0.39 / 0.25 / 0.23; GPT-2 0.47 / 0.32 / 0.26 / 0.15; OLMo 0.33 / 0.36 / 0.22 / 0.27; Pythia 0.17 / 0.17 / 0.07 / 0.07). The ten same-descendant / different-writer pairs per model have functional similarity above the all-pairs median in every model (0.39 vs 0.31, 0.20 vs 0.11, 0.53 vs 0.37, 0.43 vs 0.31, 0.50 vs 0.28): functional convergence through transport in a matched design. Excluding the largest neuron changes nothing.
- e260 DEPTHWISE W/D/L GEOMETRY (5 models, every level): the functional geometry moves from write space into descendant space with depth. The Spearman between descendant-centroid geometry and logit-footprint geometry rises monotonically: Pythia +0.10 at the write (equal to the write-logit value) -> +0.20 at +2 -> +0.31 at the mid layer -> +0.81 at the penultimate block; Qwen +0.11 -> +0.45 -> +0.60 -> +0.88; OLMo 0.00 -> +0.25 -> +0.30 -> +0.69; SmolLM2 +0.20 -> +0.44 -> +0.53 -> +0.74; GPT-2 +0.41 -> +0.58 -> +0.65 at block 9 (falling to +0.33 in the last block). The descendant's relation to write geometry decays over the same depths (Pythia 1.00 -> 0.46 -> 0.31 -> 0.15; GPT-2 -> 0.15; near zero after one block in the gated models), while write-logit stays weak throughout (-0.05 to +0.41). One figure: W -> D strong early, D -> L strong late, W -> L weak.
- e263 FUNCTIONAL DIMENSIONALITY (5 models): function needs fewer descendant dimensions than identity. Predicting a held-out token's logit footprint from its 5 nearest training tokens in the projected descendant (top principal components) saturates by 32 dimensions (90% of the full gain at 8 dimensions in OLMo and 32 elsewhere), while identity needs 32 (OLMo), 64 (GPT-2, Pythia, Qwen) or 128 (SmolLM2). The descendant-based prediction reaches or exceeds the IDENTITY ORACLE (the neuron's own mean logit footprint): 0.26 vs 0.24 (Pythia), 0.46 vs 0.42 (GPT-2), 0.44 vs 0.38 (Qwen), 0.45 vs 0.40 (OLMo), 0.40 vs 0.33 (SmolLM2), against a global-mean baseline of 0.13-0.30: the descendant predicts what a token's write will do better than knowing which neuron wrote it, and in OLMo compressing it to 32 dimensions predicts better than the full vector (0.45 vs 0.37).
- e262 INTERCHANGEABILITY (kills the operational reading): neurons with similar descendants are NOT interchangeable under intervention. Substituting the partner's write (with the removed write's coefficient) for a removed write makes the logit change LARGER than removal alone for similar-descendant pairs in Pythia (repair -0.27), GPT-2 (-0.44) and OLMo (-0.35), and does nothing in Qwen (0.00) and SmolLM2 (-0.01); control pairs -0.39, -0.45, -0.15, -0.01 and -5.98 (a catastrophic substitution in SmolLM2). Centroid-level descendant similarity (0.11-0.75) does not make writes substitutable at the token level; at best, in the gated models, a similar-descendant substitution is harmless where a dissimilar one is harmful.
- e261 COMPENSATION NEIGHBOURHOOD (kills the descendant-neighbourhood reading of compensation): the later neurons that respond most to a removed write are only weakly aligned with either coordinate. Their |cos| with the removed write is 0.05-0.23 (random later neurons 0.02-0.05) and with its descendant centroid 0.05-0.14 (random 0.02-0.04); GPT-2 and Pythia lean to the write (0.23 vs 0.14, 0.08 vs 0.05), Qwen to the descendant (0.05 vs 0.09), OLMo and SmolLM2 equal. Neurons aligned with either carry 2-32% of the compensating energy while being 0-13% of all neurons. Compensation is a diffuse crowd in both coordinates.
- Session 14 verdict: the descendant is the functional coordinate in the geometric sense (it predicts function after controlling for the write, it inherits the logit geometry with depth, and it needs fewer dimensions for function than for identity, beating the neuron label itself), but not in the operational sense (similar descendants are not substitutable, and compensators are not descendant neighbours).
- CLOSE OF ROUND 14: e01-e263, 208 scripts.

SESSION 15 (recursion on the descendant: interaction classes, neighbourhood tracking, transition split, lineage).
- e265 (5 models): (1) INVERSE TEST: logit-footprint neighbours are descendant neighbours. A neuron's 3 nearest neighbours in logit-footprint space are among its 5 nearest descendant neighbours in 0.58 (GPT-2), 0.31 (Pythia), 0.71 (Qwen), 0.57 (OLMo), 0.75 (SmolLM2) of cases against chance 0.18-0.25, and only 3-12% of the most functionally similar pairs have dissimilar descendants: few many-to-one maps; the descendant is close to the natural preimage coordinate of function at the population level. (2) NEIGHBOUR TRACKING: a neuron's nearest neighbour in write space stops being a descendant neighbour (median rank above K/4) at level 3 (OLMo, SmolLM2), 4 (Qwen), 6 (Pythia) and never in GPT-2; its nearest neighbour in logit space becomes a descendant neighbour at level 2 in GPT-2 and Pythia (from birth), 3 in OLMo and SmolLM2, 4 in Qwen. The empirical coordinate-change depth is one to four blocks after the write. (3) CAUSAL-INFLUENCE CAPACITY: the removal KL of a token's write is predicted from its descendant by 5-nearest-neighbour regression at Spearman 0.64 (GPT-2), 0.54 (Qwen), 0.43 (SmolLM2), 0.38 (OLMo), 0.33 (Pythia) with the full vector and within 0.05 of that at 32 principal dimensions (GPT-2 0.58 at 4): importance, which readability never predicted, is moderately predictable from the descendant, in few dimensions.
- e266 TRANSITION SPLIT (5 models): the operation that builds the descendant-logit correspondence is model-specific. Summed over blocks, attention contributes +0.74 and the MLP -0.02 in Pythia (most of it in blocks 12-19), +0.57 vs +0.13 in OLMo, +0.43 vs +0.04 in SmolLM2 (block 3's attention alone +0.23), while in GPT-2 the MLP builds it (+0.34) and attention erodes it (-0.42, the last block's attention -0.19), and in Qwen the MLP builds it (+0.70) with attention neutral (-0.02). The largest single-block increment is right after the write (block 3 in GPT-2, Qwen, SmolLM2) or at the very end (Pythia block 23, OLMo block 15).
- e267 LINEAGE (5 models): a write's successors are a stable, neuron-specific set, not a shared graph. A neuron's top-20 compensators computed on two disjoint halves of its tokens overlap with Jaccard 0.53-0.76, while different neurons' compensator sets overlap at 0.01-0.08; hubs (later neurons in the top-20 of at least half the candidates) number 0-5 and carry 0-3% of a neuron's compensating energy. The thousands of successors of e223 are diffuse but reproducible per neuron: lineages, not a hub graph.
- e264 INTERACTION CLASSES (5 models; pairs at tokens where both neurons are active): the aggregate descendant similarity of a pair hides token-level independence. For the most descendant-similar low-write-similarity pairs (centroid similarity 0.13-0.75), the two neurons' single-removal logit footprints at co-active tokens have cosine -0.03 to +0.04, joint removal is additive (interaction (KL(AB)-KL(A)-KL(B))/(KL(A)+KL(B)) = -0.02 to +0.03; control pairs also ~0 except GPT-2's -0.32 and Pythia's -0.10, redundancy), and A's vector injected at B's own tokens produces a footprint nearly orthogonal to B's natural footprint (per-token cosine -0.13 to +0.08 vs centroid similarity 0.13-0.75). This is why similar-descendant neurons are not interchangeable (e262): descendant similarity is a population-level property of centroids; at the token level the two neurons' effects are independent and their descendants nearly orthogonal.
- Session 15 verdict: the descendant is the population-level preimage coordinate of function (logit neighbours are descendant neighbours, few many-to-one maps), the coordinate change happens one to four blocks after the write, a write's successors form a stable neuron-specific lineage without hubs, importance is moderately predictable from few descendant dimensions, the operation that builds functional organisation differs across models (attention in Pythia, OLMo, SmolLM2; the MLP in GPT-2, Qwen), and non-interchangeability is explained: per-token effects of descendant-similar neurons are independent and near-orthogonal.
- CLOSE OF ROUND 15: e01-e267, 212 scripts.

SESSION 16 (from the reviewer's literature pass: sufficiency, quotient, direct-descendant intervention, six-space matrix, bottleneck per depth, stability).
- e268 SUFFICIENCY (5 models, six levels each): the descendant is a sufficient statistic for the write's functional effect. Predicting a held-out token's logit footprint by nearest-neighbour regression: from the write vector 0.14-0.29 (constant over depth), from the descendant 0.26-0.62 (rising with depth), from both together never more than 0.01 above the descendant alone at any level and 0.02-0.10 BELOW it in the last blocks (Qwen 0.58 vs 0.48, SmolLM2 0.59 vs 0.51): adding the write adds nothing and eventually adds noise. QUOTIENT: high-descendant-similarity / low-write-similarity pairs are as far apart in write space as average pairs (relative distance 1.01-1.08) but closer in descendant space (0.81-0.98) and correspondingly closer in function (0.80-0.99): the functional distance tracks the descendant distance, not the write distance. SIX-SPACE MATRIX: descendant-function Spearman rises with depth in 5/5 (GPT-2 +0.57 -> +0.63, Pythia +0.15 -> +0.64, Qwen +0.35 -> +0.88, OLMo +0.12 -> +0.53, SmolLM2 +0.48 -> +0.74); the natural state's own geometry also becomes functional with depth (state-function +0.19 -> +0.48 GPT-2, +0.44 -> +0.78 Qwen, +0.26 -> +0.51 OLMo) and is comparable to the descendant's in the gated models and below it in the GELU models; write-function stays constant and weak (-0.05 to +0.41). BOTTLENECK per depth: the descendant dimensions needed for 90% of the gain in IDENTITY grow with depth (GPT-2 32 -> 256, Pythia 4 -> 256, Qwen 32 -> 128, OLMo 8 -> 128, SmolLM2 8 -> 128) while those for FUNCTION stay at 16-32 and for BEHAVIOUR (removal KL) at 4-32 with scattered exceptions: identity bits > function bits > behaviour bits at depth. The network keeps expanding the space it uses to say who, while what and how much stay compressed.
- e269 DIRECT-DESCENDANT INTERVENTION (the causal test; 5 models): injecting a neuron's descendant centroid directly into the stream at block L+1 reproduces that neuron's natural logit effect at cosine 0.62 (GPT-2), 0.55 (Pythia), 0.54 (Qwen), 0.36 (SmolLM2), 0.28 (OLMo), whereas injecting the neuron's write vector at block 3 in a foreign context reproduces it at -0.29 to +0.16: the descendant, not the write, is the vector that carries the write's downstream effect. The geometry of the injected descendants' effects follows descendant geometry (Spearman +0.76 Qwen, +0.73 SmolLM2, +0.55 GPT-2, +0.41 Pythia, +0.38 OLMo) more than write geometry (-0.04 to +0.52); in the gated models injected descendants share a large common effect (all-pairs effect cosine 0.77-0.84, the generic disturbance direction of e224) with the variation around it following descendant geometry.
- e270 STABILITY (5 models): the descendant neighbourhood geometry is moderately stable. A neuron's 3 nearest descendant neighbours agree across disjoint token halves with Jaccard 0.43-0.69 (chance 0.11-0.15), across WikiText and the Pile at 0.50 (GPT-2) and 0.53 (SmolLM2) without refitting, and across Pythia checkpoints at 0.40 (final vs step 64000) and 0.27 (vs 16000) while the write-space neighbourhoods are more stable (0.77 and 0.45): descendants change more than writes late in training.
- Session 16 verdict: the descendant is a sufficient statistic for a write's functional effect, it is the vector that causally carries that effect when injected, functional distance tracks descendant distance where write distance is uninformative, the dimensions the network spends on identity grow with depth while function and behaviour stay compressed, and the descendant geometry is stable across tokens and corpora and moderately across training.
- CLOSE OF ROUND 16: e01-e270, 215 scripts.

SESSION 17 (two gap-fillers after the reviewer's third list, whose three items round 16 had already run).
- e271 DESCENDANT INTERPOLATION (5 models): the descendant coordinate parameterises the intervention effect continuously. Across all candidate pairs, the distance between the logit effects of directly injected descendants tracks the distance between the descendants themselves (Spearman +0.51 GPT-2, +0.53 Pythia, +0.74 Qwen, +0.47 OLMo, +0.88 SmolLM2; slope 0.79-1.51, i.e. about one-to-one). Injecting a point on the segment between two descendants gives an effect with cosine 0.99-1.00 to the linear interpolation of the endpoint effects (relative error 0.08-0.26 for high-descendant-similarity pairs, 0.26-0.41 for dissimilar pairs), so the effect is linear along descendant segments and the endpoint effects are as similar as the descendants (high-D pairs 0.22-0.70, low-D pairs -0.13 to +0.27). The effect of an intervention is a near-linear function of where it sits in descendant space.
- e272 FOUR-CAPACITY LADDER (5 models, six levels): reconstructing the descendant needs the full dimension (256-2048: the 90%-of-gain point is never reached before the full basis; it is full-rank), identifying its source needs 4-256 dimensions growing with depth, predicting its function 8-64 (mostly 16-32) and its behavioural weight (removal KL) 4-256 (mostly 8-32, with early-level outliers in OLMo and SmolLM2). Reconstruction >> identity > function ~ behaviour: the descendant is not a reconstruction basis, it is a compressed coordinate for what a write does and how much it matters.
- Session 17 verdict (and end of the program): the descendant is a sufficient, causally active, continuously parameterising, low-dimensional-for-function coordinate that no small basis reconstructs. Every item on the reviewer's third list had been run in round 16; nothing remains that the existing infrastructure can cheaply distinguish.
- CLOSE OF ROUND 17: e01-e272, 217 scripts.

SESSION 18 (after the user's J-space aside and the reviewer's three "actual next" items: functional equivalence classes, subspace nesting, transport residuals; all three were new and cheap, all five models, block-2 writers read at the middle level).
- e273 FUNCTIONAL NULL SPACE (5 models): inject a descendant centroid displaced by a fixed amount (0.45-0.47 in unit-descendant units) along five direction classes (top-8, middle-8 and bottom-8 principal components of the descendant cloud, 8 random directions, 8 differences toward other descendants) at the block-(L+1) input, and measure the relative change of the logit effect. The response is the same in every class: top 0.35-0.51, middle 0.31-0.51, bottom 0.32-0.45, random 0.32-0.53 (max/min ratio 1.2-1.4 in Pythia, Qwen, OLMo and SmolLM2). Directions toward other descendants are the most consequential but only by 15-30% (0.41-0.62). There is no functional null space in descendant space and therefore no functional equivalence classes: the map from descendant to function is near-isotropic, as a near-isometric mixing map followed by the unembedding should be, and the response per unit displacement (about 0.8) matches the linearity of e271. The one exception is GPT-2's bottom-8 class at 0.94-1.04 (2.6x). Per descendant, SmolLM2's massive neuron 90 responds 0.63-0.85 against 0.27-0.39 for a typical neuron, in every class alike: its effect is twice as fragile, not anisotropically so.
- e273b BOTTOM PCS (5 models): GPT-2's bottom-8 principal components carry 0.103 of their energy on the three massive-activation channels (447, 64, 373; chance 0.004), the other four models' bottom PCs 0.000-0.002. The exception is the massive channel: nearly constant across descendants (hence a bottom PC) yet the logits are highly sensitive to it through the normalization scale. The top-8 PCs carry 0.06-0.16 of their energy on the massive channels in all five models.
- e274 SUBSPACE NESTING (5 models): identity subspace = top-d eigenvectors of the between-neuron scatter of descendants; function subspace = top-d PLS directions between descendants and (PCA-reduced) logit footprints; behaviour direction = ridge predictor of the removal KL. Energy of the function subspace inside the identity subspace at d=8: 0.76 Pythia, 0.69 GPT-2, 0.60 OLMo, 0.56 Qwen, 0.48 SmolLM2 (chance 0.004-0.014); at d=32: 0.39-0.47 (chance 0.016-0.056). The function subspace sits mostly inside the leading state PCs (0.60-0.82 at d=8, 0.69-0.71 at d=32). The linear behaviour direction lies outside the function subspace (0.00-0.11) and the identity subspace (0.00-0.07) in four models and inside the function subspace in SmolLM2 (0.48 at d=8, 0.77 at d=32). Verdict: identity and function are neither nested nor rotated; they share a leading core (half to three quarters of the 8-dimensional energy, 50-100 times chance) and rotate apart beyond it. The behaviour result is recorded but not read as a nesting verdict: the removal KL is quadratic in the logit change, so a linear direction predicting it need not lie in the linear function subspace.
- e275 / e275b TRANSPORT RESIDUALS (5 models): fit the linear transport operator T on random injections at the block-3 input read at the middle level (as e248) and compare each candidate's natural descendant centroid with T w. The first pass compared them unsigned; in the gated models 43-75% of the dominant writes carry a negative coefficient (Qwen 75%, SmolLM2 48%, OLMo 43%; GELU models 0%), so the prediction must be sign(coefficient) * T w (e275b). Sign-corrected residual 1 - cos, median: 0.53 GPT-2, 0.82 Pythia, 0.85 OLMo, 0.89 SmolLM2, 0.93 Qwen, i.e. the corpus-averaged linear transport captures the natural descendant direction only at cosine 0.07-0.47 at mid depth, consistent with e248's inverse cosines at the same levels (the provenance successes of e248 were relative, not absolute, alignment). The massive neuron: SmolLM2's neuron 90 (13x the median coefficient) has residual 1.64 (cosine -0.64), rank 1 of 23, z +7.6 against a bulk spanning 0.77-0.98; the largest-coefficient neurons of the other four models are not massive (1.4-2x the median) and sit inside the bulk (OLMo rank 2/21 z +1.6, Pythia 4/29 z +1.3, Qwen 17/28 z -0.1, GPT-2 14/24 z -0.4). The residual anti-correlates with along-survival in 5/5 (Spearman -0.74 GPT-2, -0.40 Pythia, -0.78 Qwen, -0.60 OLMo, -0.67 SmolLM2): the linear transport law fits best the neurons whose own write direction survives most. No consistent relation with coefficient size (-0.39 to +0.26) or with natural identification (-0.34 to +0.34). Verdict: where the candidate set contains a genuinely massive neuron, it is a separate mechanism, not the tail of the transport law.
- J-space (the user's aside): J-space, as recorded in the superweight study (section 22, Y29), is the Jacobian from a layer's state to the final state, averaged over positions and a corpus, read through the unembedding: a dictionary of what the model can say. WDD is a dictionary of what the model writes. The descendant of this program is the un-averaged, per-write, finite-difference image of a WDD atom under the Jacobian between two intermediate levels; the transport operator of e248 is the corpus-averaged version, i.e. the J-space construction applied between intermediate levels; and the descendant-to-logit map of e262-e269 is J-space applied to a descendant. e275b measures the gap between the averaged and the per-write object: cosine 0.07-0.47 at mid depth.
- Session 18 verdict: the descendant-to-function map has no kernel (no equivalence classes), the identity and function subspaces share a leading core and rotate apart beyond it, the massive neuron is off the transport law rather than at its tail, the sign of the coefficient must travel with any transport prediction, and along-survival predicts how well the linear law fits.
- CLOSE OF ROUND 18: e01-e275b, 222 scripts.

SESSION 19 (the reviewer's "what has the tree not discharged" list: thirteen rows; four of them were cheap to test, all five models, block-2 writers).
- e276 LEVERAGE, USED OR INDUCED (5 models): unit directions injected at the block-(L+1) input of foreign tokens at the natural footprint norm: natural descendant centroids, descendant centroids of random vectors (random directions injected at block 3 and read at L), the raw write vectors, random directions, covariance-matched random directions. Median logit response, natural over random-vector descendant: 0.96 Pythia, 1.02 GPT-2, 1.18 Qwen, 0.94 OLMo, 1.17 SmolLM2; median KL ratio 1.06, 1.00, 2.00, 1.36, 1.67. Natural over covariance-matched: logit 0.93, 0.65, 1.14, 1.02, 1.13 (GPT-2's covariance-matched directions load on massive channel 447 and out-lever everything); KL 0.79, 0.36, 1.32, 1.08, 1.31. The raw write vector at level L has the leverage of a random direction (0.98-1.05). Verdict: the downstream network is not tuned to the descendants of its own writes in magnitude; a random vector transported from block 3 is as consequential as a natural descendant (the gated models give natural descendants at most twice the KL). The descendant's effect is direction-specific (e269) and magnitude-generic: the leverage belongs to the transport, not to the write. The reviewer's "used versus induced" comes out as induced, and the transported-dictionary assumption dies again: at depth the model does not read the raw write direction more than a random one.
- e277 ENERGY BUDGET ACROSS DEPTH (5 models, six levels): coherent fraction of a per-token descendant (energy along its class centroid, centroids from the other half of the tokens): one block after the write 0.82 Pythia, 0.65 GPT-2, 0.64 Qwen, 0.61 OLMo, 0.76 SmolLM2; at mid depth 0.16, 0.18, 0.33, 0.20, 0.20; near the end 0.05, 0.13, 0.12, 0.12, 0.08. Held-out identification over the same levels: 1.00/0.99/0.51, 0.96/0.93/0.83, 0.99/0.89/0.58, 1.00/0.99/0.83, 0.95/0.73/0.49. The incoherent remainder is barely a linear function of the state: R2 from the top-64 PCs of the same-level state 0.05-0.23 one block after the write, 0.02-0.10 at mid depth, 0.00-0.05 near the end; from the birth-level state 0.04-0.20, then 0.02-0.27 (SmolLM2 alone keeps 0.19-0.27 from the birth context at every depth). Transport gain ||F||/|coef| 1.1-1.7 early, 2-15 late in the gated models. Verdict: provenance survives on a consistent component that shrinks to 16-33% of the descendant's energy at mid depth and 5-14% late; the other 67-95% is token-specific and not linearly readable from the context. That is the answer to "why does the transported dictionary fail while provenance survives": classification needs only the small consistent component (distinct per neuron, angles preserved), a decomposition would have to explain the token-specific remainder, which no fixed atom indexes.
- e278 AMPLITUDE SWEEP (5 models; the largest-coefficient candidate and two typical ones transplanted at 0.1x, 0.3x, 1x, 3x, 10x their natural coefficient): SmolLM2's massive neuron 90 (|coef| 69.6): cosine of the transplant image to the linear transport +0.72/+0.71/+0.66/+0.56/+0.56, to the natural descendant -0.90/-0.86/-0.73/-0.56/-0.85, to its own 0.1x image 1.00/0.99/0.92/0.78/0.80, operating-point shift (norm ratio of the block-3 input) 1.05/1.17/1.69/3.48/10.1. Typical neurons: linear 0.13-0.49, self-consistency 0.78-0.99 at 1x and 0.08-0.80 at 10x with shifts of only 1.2-2.3 (Pythia's largest, 2.2, loses its direction entirely at 10x: 0.08). GPT-2's largest (10.3, not massive) is law-abiding and natural-aligned at every amplitude (linear 0.52-0.68, natural 0.59-0.68). Verdict: the massive write direction is the most linear direction in the sweep, obeying the transport law at every amplitude through a tenfold operating-point shift, and its natural descendant is anti-parallel to its transplant at every amplitude including the typical one (0.1x = 7.0, shift 1.05). The anomaly is neither the vector nor its amplitude: it is context-bound. With e250 (natural and transplant agree at +2 blocks, 0.99, and are anti-parallel at mid depth, -0.75), e253 (independent of own activation, norm and position) and e257 (footprint entirely at the source token), the massive neuron's natural descendant is a downstream reaction at its own token that develops between +2 and mid depth and does not occur for an added copy. Ordinary writes have context-free descendants; the massive write has a context-bound one. Amplitude invariance of ordinary writes holds to about the natural amplitude and breaks at 3-10x.
- e279 CORE OVERLAP AND ROTATION AT EVERY BLOCK (5 models): energy of the block-j identity subspace (top-8 between-neuron scatter directions) inside the block-(j+1) one: first blocks after birth 0.38-0.53 Qwen (blocks 4-7), 0.51-0.59 OLMo (4-5), 0.63 SmolLM2 (4), 0.56-0.69 Pythia, 0.83 GPT-2; middle blocks 0.65-0.91 in all five; last block 0.43 SmolLM2, 0.57 Pythia, 0.53 Qwen, 0.52 OLMo, 0.70 GPT-2; one extra dip in SmolLM2 at block 10 (0.46). Chance 0.004-0.010. The function subspace rotates in step with the identity subspace (kept 0.35-0.91, same profile). Function-in-identity overlap (d=8) 0.44-0.77 throughout, drifting slowly. Identification collapses late (Pythia 0.96 at block 15 to 0.43 at 20; Qwen 0.78 at 15 to 0.45 at 23; SmolLM2 0.71 at 15 to 0.39 at 29) while the subspace is stable, so provenance is lost by loss of coherent energy (e277), not by a change of coordinates. Verdict: continuous drift with two rotation zones, the 1-5 blocks after birth and the final block; no mid-depth transition beyond SmolLM2's mild dip.
- The reviewer's thirteen rows against the record: identifies writes (yes); provenance survives (yes); survival is leakage (killed); descendant predictable from the write (yes, by transplant; only weakly by the averaged linear operator, e275b); descendant encodes function (yes); descendant geometry is the model's functional coordinate system (narrowed: direction-specific carriage yes, e269; magnitude tuning no, e276); why training builds it (open, and not a five-minute experiment: the record has the when, e243/e217/e239, not the why); why sparse decomposition dies while provenance survives (answered by e277: the coherent fraction); relation to superposition/SAE/transcoders (positioning, not experiment; e241 gives the superposition numbers); the invariant (answered: pairwise angles, e238, and energy, e210, both decoder-free); the abstraction boundary (answered by e279 and e260: drift with two rotation zones); a new practical capability (a workflow demonstration, i.e. writing: zero-shot data-free provenance e246, effect prediction from descendants e269-e271); the massive-neuron mechanism (narrowed by e278: context-bound reaction, not amplitude, not cross-token). The reviewer's "local versus global transport" split is contradicted by e257 (the massive footprint is entirely local); the right split is context-free versus context-bound.
- CLOSE OF ROUND 19: e01-e279, 226 scripts.

SESSION 20 (the reviewer's "change the question" list: counterfactual trajectory, functional composition, a conservation law, prediction of the future, the massive neuron's reaction; all five models, block-2 writers).
- e280 COUNTERFACTUAL TRAJECTORY (5 models, four most-dissimilar descendant pairs each): the interpolated descendant (1-a) d_A + a d_B injected at the block-(L+1) input of tokens where neither is written, the network run on. At every later level and at the logits the response has cosine 0.97-1.00 to the interpolation of the endpoint responses, the fitted position a-hat equals a within 0.01 (0.24-0.25 / 0.49-0.50 / 0.74-0.76 for a = 1/4, 1/2, 3/4) with interquartile ranges that never reach the endpoints, and 95-100% of the response lies in the endpoint plane; endpoint responses are dissimilar (cosine -0.18 to +0.32). No snapping: the network's own subsequent computation carries the continuous coordinate unchanged to the output.
- e281 FUNCTIONAL COMPOSITION (5 models; operators T(3->L), T(3->M), T(M->L) fitted on random injections, M the midpoint block): for held-out random vectors the composed prediction matches the actual image at 0.79 GPT-2, 0.50 Pythia, 0.70 Qwen, 0.39 OLMo, 0.81 SmolLM2 against 0.75, 0.43, 0.63, 0.43, 0.70 for the direct fit; injected at block L+1, the composed prediction's logit effect matches the actual image's effect at 0.81, 0.53, 0.70, 0.43, 0.80 against 0.74, 0.44, 0.67, 0.46, 0.68 for the direct fit (random 0.00). The law composes in function as in state, and the two-step product is the better estimate in 4/5. For the candidate writes the natural centroid is met at 0.07-0.33 direct and 0.08-0.48 composed (the averaged operator is a coarse version of the per-context transport, as e275b).
- e282 THE METRIC (5 models; candidate writes and as many random directions injected at block 3, images at +2, L, NB-2): gain spread (coefficient of variation) over the writes 0.08-0.21 at +2, 0.18-0.41 at L, 0.13-0.26 late in the Euclidean geometry and 0.06-0.38, 0.14-1.85, 0.14-1.24 in the whitened geometry; over random directions 0.02-0.07 Euclidean, 0.03-0.19 whitened. Pairwise-cosine preservation of the writes (Spearman) 0.57-0.72 at +2, 0.25-0.60 at L, 0.11-0.64 late in the Euclidean geometry and 0.30-0.60, 0.07-0.37, 0.01-0.28 whitened. Singular-value spread (q90/q10) of the fitted operator 11-50 Euclidean, 42-73 whitened (OLMo degenerate). Verdict: whitening makes the transport less isometric at every level in 5/5; the Euclidean geometry is the best of the tested metrics. The near-constant gain of random directions is concentration of measure over an operator whose singular values spread by 11-50x, and actual writes vary 3-6 times more than random directions: the transport is an isometry on the write manifold only in the angular sense (pairwise cosines), not in gain. The general "find M_l" question is trivially solvable for any linear map (pull the metric back) and was not treated as a test.
- e283 THE DESCENDANT AS A STATE VARIABLE (5 models): the per-token natural descendant two blocks later is predicted from the present descendant pushed through the data-free operator T(L->L+2) at cosine 0.66 GPT-2, 0.73 Pythia, 0.70 Qwen, 0.43 OLMo, 0.78 SmolLM2 (four blocks later 0.40, 0.54, 0.48, 0.25, 0.61); from the signed write through T(3->L+2) at 0.06-0.12; from a ridge on the state's top-64 PCs at 0.15-0.60; from a random vector of the descendant's norm at 0.00. Adding the state to the descendant law gains at most +0.16 (Qwen, OLMo), adding the write nothing. Centroid level: descendant law 0.47-0.78, write law 0.09-0.25. The future functional test: the logit effect of the law-predicted future centroid matches that of the actual future centroid at 0.59-0.85 two blocks later and 0.36-0.75 four blocks later (write law 0.04-0.16, random 0.00). The descendant's future depends on its present, not on its origin, and the predicted future reproduces the future effect: a state variable in the dynamical sense.
- e284 / e284b THE REACTION CARRIER (5 models; the largest-coefficient candidate and a typical one; downstream component outputs held at their unablated values at the neuron's own tokens): both-frozen sanity returns the write itself (cosine 1.00) in 10/10 cases. SmolLM2's massive neuron 90: natural footprint centroid vs transplant image -0.67; all attention outputs frozen -0.66 (no change); all MLP outputs frozen +0.49 (flipped); the MLP of block 10 alone +0.52 (the flip), MLP 11 -0.20, MLP 9 -0.65, every other component within 0.05 of natural. The context-bound reaction is carried by one component, the MLP of block 10, at the neuron's own tokens; block 10 is also the one mid-depth rotation of SmolLM2's identity coordinates (e279, kept 0.46) and the step where its identification drops (0.88 to 0.79). SmolLM2's typical neuron: natural -0.44, all attention frozen +0.02, all MLP -0.14, no single component above -0.37: its smaller disagreement with the transplant is carried by attention, spread over blocks (consistent with attention as the builder of SmolLM2's functional organisation, e266). Other models: natural vs transplant +0.14 to +0.78; freezes move it by at most +0.3 (OLMo largest: all MLP +0.45 from +0.15; Qwen largest: all attention +0.44 from +0.14), no dominant component.
- Session 20 verdict: the descendant behaves as a dynamical state variable (its trajectory is linear under interpolation to the output, the transport law composes functionally, and its future and the future effect follow from its present, not from its origin), the Euclidean geometry is the geometry of that dynamics, and the massive neuron's departure from the law is one MLP's reaction at one block. The representation question of round 19 resolves as: the network uses the descendant coordinate in the dynamical sense (it is what the next blocks act on and carry), not in the sense of being tuned to it (e276).
- CLOSE OF ROUND 20: e01-e284b, 232 scripts.

SESSION 21 (theory and literature: the user asked whether the mathematics can be derived from here and for a literature review; THEORY.md and RELATED_WORK.md written; the theory's new predictions tested in e285 and e285b).
- THEORY.md: a first-order transport theory. Descendant = coefficient x token Jacobian x unit write, d_i(l,t) = c_i(t) [J-bar_C + dJ_t] w-hat_i + O(c^2), with a generic non-isometric spectrum (singular spread 11-50), token dependence that dominates by mid depth (coherent fraction 0.16-0.33), and a linear range up to about the natural amplitude; function f = G d with G the un-averaged Jacobian lens. It derives homogeneity (e250, e278), additivity within the linear range (e241, e248), vector determination (e236-e248), sufficiency, causal carriage, interpolation and the absence of a functional null space (e268-e273, e280), the crossover of functional alignment with depth (e259, e260), the state-variable and composition results (e281, e283), generic leverage (e276), angle preservation as concentration of measure rather than isometry (e238, e282), the provenance-versus-decomposition asymmetry as a signal-to-noise inequality in the coherent fraction (identification needs kappa >~ K/D_eff, reconstruction needs kappa ~ 1; e277, e272), and the training results as J moving from the identity to a generic spectrum (e243, e217, e279). The massive neuron enters as the one write whose token-specific Jacobian term is coherent at its own tokens (e250), carried by one MLP (e284b), not an amplitude effect (e278). Not derived: the numbers, the reacting component, the training trajectory, subspace sizes.
- e285 THEORY PREDICTIONS (5 models): (1) the actual gain of the transport by input direction follows the fitted operator's spectrum: top-8 singular directions 0.33-1.12, random 0.26-0.57, middle 0.22-0.52, bottom 0.16-0.35 (ordering top > random > middle >= bottom in 5/5; bottom nonzero although the operator assigns 0.00, so the true transport has no null space). (2) The derived formula for the image cosine of a pair with input cosine 0.75, cos_out = 0.75 rho / sqrt(0.75^2 rho^2 + 0.66^2) with rho the gain ratio: measured/predicted top 0.81-0.93 / 0.83-0.94, bottom 0.40-0.74 / 0.40-0.76, random 0.71-0.76 / 0.75-0.77, within 0.07 in all fifteen cases. The e238 "isometry" is angle preservation for random-component pairs by gain concentration (rho 1.00-1.06); pairs on the operator's top directions have their cosines raised and pairs on its bottom directions lowered exactly as the formula says. (3) The additivity-versus-norm test was mis-designed (quarter-amplitude reference images are noise-dominated) and superseded by e285b.
- e285b ADDITIVITY (5 models): relative-norm error against the sum of single images has a floor of 0.51-0.73 at m = 1 because single images come from a pass with mixed directions across tokens while the reference sums are injected uniformly (cross-token confound); the norm-versus-count prediction is left open. Direction: sixteen superposed writes at constant total norm agree with the sum of single responses at cosine 0.95-0.97 (one write 0.69-0.86); one write at four times the natural amplitude falls to 0.50-0.77; at equal count the larger total norm gives the larger error in 5/5. Superposition is at least as linear as a single write; amplitude is what breaks linearity.
- RELATED_WORK.md: nine sections (classical sparse approximation; residual stream, lenses and the Jacobian lens / J-space of Gurnee, Sofroniew, Lindsey et al. 2026; linearity and dynamics: Razzhigaev et al. 2024, Szablewski's Activation Transport Operators 2025, Fernando and Guitchounts 2025/2026 Jacobian spectra; self-repair: McGrath et al. 2023, Ferreira da Silva and Heimersheim 2026 feature-specific error correction; SAEs, transcoders, cross-layer transcoders, multi-layer SAEs (Lawson et al. 2025); superposition; massive activations (Sun et al. 2024, Vemula 2026), super weights, outlier dimensions; steering and function vectors, causal abstraction; dynamical isometry). Closest neighbours: the Jacobian lens (the averaged version of the program's transport, applied to the final state), Activation Transport Operators (linear transport of SAE features between layers, from natural states), and the Fernando-Guitchounts Jacobian eigendecompositions (spectral gradient and low-rank bottleneck, learned). What the program adds: the per-write, causal, cross-token-resolved transport of the model's own atoms, its functional sufficiency and state-variable behaviour, the coherent-fraction account of why provenance outlives decomposition, and a localised exception.
- CLOSE OF ROUND 21: e01-e285b, 234 scripts; THEORY.md, RELATED_WORK.md.

SESSION 22 (the reviewer's reading of the theory, and its one compression test).
- THEORY.md revised as the reviewer asked: the theorem-like object (d = c J w-hat, mathematics conditional on the assumptions) is now stated apart from the empirical discovery (across five models the Jacobian's image of a write stays causally identifiable and functionally meaningful long after sparse decomposition fails, and is a state variable for the perturbation family), and every derived item is tagged [derived], [J], [G] or [reading].
- e286 COMPRESSION LADDER (5 models, mid depth, K 28-36 candidates, one input object: the per-token descendant; one decoder family; observable-specific supervised subspaces and PCA; dimensions for 90% of the full held-out score). Reconstruction of the descendant: 512-1024 directions (GPT-2 512 of 768, Pythia 1024 of 1024, Qwen 512 of 896, OLMo 512 of 2048; SmolLM2's "1" is its massive channel, which holds 99% of the descendant's variance). Everything causal sits in 8-32 supervised directions: the future descendant two blocks later 8-32 (32, 16, 16, 8, 16; full score 0.34-0.56), source identity 16-32 (32, 32, 32, 16, 32; note the ceiling K-1 on a between-class subspace, so identity is bounded by the candidate count here), the logit footprint 8-32 (32, 32, 16, 8, 32), the removal KL 8-32 (16, 8, 8, 16, 32). PCA subspaces need 2-4 times more for identity (32-128) and about the same for the rest. Verdict: the ladder has two rungs, not four. The physical perturbation is full-dimensional; its causal description (who wrote it, where it goes next, what it does, how much it matters) lives in a 8-to-32-dimensional subspace, with differences inside that rung within a factor of two to four (behaviour smallest in 3/5) against a factor of 16 to 64 between rungs. The reviewer's "progressively lower-dimensional descriptions" holds only as a mild ordering inside one compressed rung.
- CLOSE OF ROUND 22: e01-e286, 235 scripts.

SESSION 23 (a second reviewer session: endorsed the theory, flagged one sentence as stronger than the evidence (low-rank causal read-out is measured, low-rank shared transport is a mechanism), and asked that "unrelated" be replaced by "the write coordinate becomes a poor description while the descendant becomes a good predictor"; both applied, and the flagged sentence tested).
- e287 SHARED VERSUS TOKEN-SPECIFIC READ-OUT (5 models, mid depth): the per-token descendant split into its component in the span of the K class centroids (the shared, between-neuron part: 28-46% of the energy; 99% in SmolLM2, whose massive channel sits in the span) and the orthogonal remainder (the token-specific part, 54-72%). Decoded with the e286 decoders on held-out tokens: the span part alone reaches the full score on every observable in 5/5 (identity 0.71-0.98 = full; logit footprint 0.24-0.42 vs full 0.21-0.42; removal KL 0.38-0.60 vs 0.37-0.64; future descendant 0.38-0.55 vs 0.34-0.56). The remainder alone carries no identity (0.02-0.04, chance 0.03) and about half of the other scores (function 0.09-0.28, KL 0.13-0.47, future 0.12-0.45), which is what a random K-dimensional projection of the descendant also carries (0.14-0.35, 0.23-0.63, 0.22-0.45); and adding the remainder to the span adds nothing. Verdict: the low-rank causal read-out is the shared transport term. The token-specific term dominates the descendant's energy and is causally redundant for identity, function, behaviour and the future alike. The reviewer's caution was right as logic and is now settled as measurement: what G reads from the descendant lives in the part shared across a neuron's tokens.
- Phrasing fixed in THEORY.md and SYNTHESIS.md: the descendant stays the Jacobian image of the write; what falls with depth is the usefulness of the write coordinate as a description of it, while its usefulness for predicting effects rises. The reviewer's proposition adopted as the candidate abstract sentence: for these models and this perturbation family, a neuron write that begins as a sparse, source-specific WDD coordinate becomes a high-dimensional residual descendant whose downstream-relevant information is concentrated in a much smaller causal subspace; the descendant is a better state variable for its own future and its functional effect than the original write, even as the WDD dictionary ceases to provide a decomposition. In one line: the perturbation spreads physically, but its causal identity compresses.
- CLOSE OF ROUND 23: e01-e287, 236 scripts.

SESSION 24 (the reviewer's kill-tree: ten attacks on the causal-coordinate reading; four scripts, 21 runs, one offline analysis; two earlier claims corrected).
- ELBOW (offline, from the e286 curves): no sharp knee. Half of the full score is reached by 1-16 supervised directions, three quarters by 4-32, ninety percent by 8-32; the curves saturate gradually, behaviour fastest (8 directions give 0.65-1.06 of full), identity slowest (0.45-0.83). There is no single intrinsic causal dimension, only a soft scale of about ten to thirty directions with a mild ordering behaviour < function ~ future < identity.
- e288 KILL-TREE CORE (5 models, mid depth, 16-dimensional subspaces, chance 0.007-0.028). (1) One subspace? Identity, function and future share half to three quarters of their energy (identity-function 0.35-0.54, identity-future 0.57-0.73, function-future 0.49-0.73); the KL subspace is less aligned (0.12-0.61). A shared core, not one subspace. (2) Transfer: any of the identity, function or future subspaces decodes all four observables at close to their own-subspace scores (the function subspace gives identity 0.53-0.79, future 0.26-0.54, KL 0.36-0.62; the identity subspace gives function 0.21-0.42, equal to the function subspace's own 0.17-0.43). But a random 16-dimensional subspace drawn from the descendant covariance does nearly as well for function (0.17-0.39), future (0.28-0.51) and KL (0.32-0.63), and a plain random subspace clearly worse (function 0.10-0.31); only identity needs the supervised directions (0.60-0.93 vs 0.35-0.82 covariance-matched, 0.28-0.52 plain). (3) Adversarial split controls, K dimensions: the true centroid span (energy 0.28-0.46) gives identity 0.71-0.98, function 0.24-0.42, future 0.38-0.55, KL 0.38-0.60; a covariance-matched random K-dimensional subspace gives 0.46-0.88 / 0.22-0.41 / 0.36-0.54 / 0.35-0.63; a permuted-label centroid span 0.47-0.83 / 0.22-0.42 / 0.35-0.55 / 0.28-0.65. Function, future and KL are decoded equally well from all three; only identity is specific to the true span. CORRECTION to session 23: the read-out of function, future and behaviour lives in the high-variance, low-rank part of the descendant cloud, of which the centroid span is one basis; it is not specifically the term shared across a neuron's tokens. Only source identity is specific to the shared term. The two rungs of session 22 stand; their mechanism is the low rank of the cloud, not the shared/token-specific split. (4) The token-specific remainder (54-72% of energy) predicts its own future two blocks later at cosine 0.09-0.28 (shuffled 0.00): it is a weakly transported state of its own that the four observables do not read, not noise. (5) Closure: the subspaces recomputed two blocks later overlap the ones at L at 0.30-0.82 without any transport (stationary) and at 0.16-0.70 after transport through the fitted operator, never better than stationary. The causal subspace is approximately invariant over two blocks because the residual transport is near the identity on it; the test cannot separate "invariant under J" from "stationary in coordinates", and the operator adds nothing.
- e289 COORDINATE STEERING (5 models): injecting the natural footprint norm along the k-th descendant-space direction of the descendant-to-logit pairing moves the logits along its paired logit direction (diagonal median cosine +0.14 SmolLM2, +0.23 Qwen, +0.23 OLMo, +0.31 Pythia, +0.34 GPT-2; every one of the 40 diagonal entries positive; the paired direction is the largest of the eight in 88-100% of cases, 75% in SmolLM2), not along the other seven (off-diagonal 0.00), and random directions move them along none (|cosine| 0.03-0.04). The coordinate is causally manipulable, one direction at a time.
- e290 MASSIVE COUNTERFACTUAL (5 models): the theory's prediction was that adding the massive direction on top of its own write at its own tokens gives a response aligned with the natural removal footprint. KILLED, and the replacement is sharper. SmolLM2's massive neuron: the own-token addition response is anti-parallel to the natural footprint (-0.90), aligned with the foreign-token addition (+0.55) and with the linear law (+0.66); the foreign addition is anti-parallel to the natural footprint (-0.50). So adding the massive write at its own tokens obeys the same first-order law as everywhere else, and it is removal that is reversed: removing the write moves the downstream state in the same direction as adding more of it. The response at the massive neuron's own tokens is even in the deviation from the natural amount, a set-point response: the natural coefficient sits at an extremum of the downstream response along the transport direction, and the reacting component (the MLP of block 10, e284b) answers any deviation the same way. For the other models' largest and typical neurons the own-token addition agrees with the natural footprint (+0.63 to +0.95 in GPT-2, Pythia, OLMo; +0.14 to +0.26 in Qwen and SmolLM2's typical neuron, where the first-order picture at mid depth is only approximate, as e242 showed).
- e291 TRAINING (Pythia-410m, six revisions, level 12): at step 0 the descendant is low-dimensional and fully predictive (reconstruction 64 directions, identity 4, function 2, future 2, KL 4; full scores 0.95-1.00; centroid span 0.93 of energy): transport is the identity and the ladder is trivial. By step 1000 reconstruction needs 256 directions, by step 4000 the full 1024, and the span fraction falls from 0.93 to 0.64 to 0.32; the causal dimensions grow slowly (identity 4 -> 8 -> 8 -> 8 -> 16 -> 32, function 2 -> 8 -> 16 -> 16 -> 32 -> 32, future 2 -> 4 -> 16 -> 16 -> 16 -> 16, KL 4 -> 8 -> 8 -> 8 -> 8 -> 8) while the achievable scores fall (function 0.99 -> 0.72 -> 0.42 -> 0.31 -> 0.25 -> 0.24). The two rungs appear with dispersal in the first four thousand steps, at the same time as the along-direction erasure (e217) and the funnel (e243); the causal dimension then creeps up over the rest of training as the physical descendant stays full-dimensional.
- Skipped from the reviewer's list, with reasons: composition in causal space (a linear projection is additive by construction, and the functional half is e271 and e280); a universal cross-neuron map (the subspaces of e286 and e287 are one map for all candidates by construction); more models, corpora, dictionaries or provenance repeats.
- CLOSE OF ROUND 24: e01-e291, 240 scripts.

SESSION 25 (the reviewer's four stopping-criterion questions: what is the causal coordinate, does it transport as a coherent object, can it be manipulated, what is the rest of the descendant doing; four scripts, 20 runs).
- e292 BREAKING THE STEERING RESULT (5 models): a ridge map from descendants to logit-footprint scores, fitted on half the candidate neurons, predicts the ENTIRE logit change of an injection at the natural footprint norm at cosine 0.09-0.33 for the PLS coordinates (SmolLM2 0.09, Qwen 0.20, OLMo 0.19, GPT-2 0.32, Pythia 0.33), 0.05-0.24 for the centroids of held-out neurons (training neurons 0.07-0.33), 0.08-0.22 for covariance-matched random directions, 0.09-0.33 for the cloud's top PCs, and 0.01-0.08 for plain random directions. The response to a PLS coordinate has 0.19-0.39 of its energy inside the 32 paired logit directions against 0.07-0.09 for random 32-dimensional subspaces of the same footprint space. Verdict: the selectivity of e289 is real (the paired directions are hit three to four times above chance, and only they) but partial: most of the logit change (61-81% of its energy) lies outside the paired span, the full-vector prediction is modest, it generalises to unseen neurons with some loss, and it does not extend to directions outside the descendant cloud. The coordinate steers its paired directions; it does not dictate the whole response.
- e293 DYNAMICS OF THE CORE (5 models, 16-dimensional function and identity subspaces, fixed basis): over two blocks the core's future is linear in its present at R2 0.89-0.96 (SmolLM2 1.00, its massive channel), the map has geometric-mean gain 0.64-0.95 (GPT-2 1.16, expanding), singular-value spread 1.3-2.7, Procrustes residual from a scaled rotation 0.08-0.17, off-diagonal energy 0.01-0.04 and rotation angles at most 0.11: a near-diagonal, mildly and evenly contracting, rotation-free map. Over four blocks R2 0.69-0.90, gain 0.45-0.96 (GPT-2 1.28), spread up to 4.0, residual up to 0.33, off-diagonal up to 0.13. In a basis recomputed at the later level the off-diagonal energy is 0.30-0.77, which measures the instability of the recomputed basis, not mixing. Verdict: the causal core transports as a coherent object, each coordinate almost independently, decaying at similar rates, with 4-11% new content per two blocks.
- e294 COMMON DIMENSION BY REDUCED-RANK REGRESSION (5 models; linear R2 criterion, 90% of full): individual ranks function 1-16 (mostly 1-4), identity 16-32, removal KL 1 (OLMo 128), future descendant 16-128 (mostly 128); the joint rank equals the largest individual rank in 4/5 (128 = the future's) and is well below the sum (97-177). Verdict: the predictive subspaces are nested, one hierarchy rather than separate latents; under a linear-reconstruction criterion the future descendant needs about 128 directions, an intermediate scale between the effect-and-identity coordinate (8-32, e286, cosine and identification criteria) and the physical descendant (512-1024). Three scales, then: effect and identity, linear future, physical.
- e295 INTERVENING ON THE LEFTOVER DIMENSIONS (5 models; q = the descendant's component outside the top-32 principal directions, 43-59% of its energy, 0.00 in SmolLM2): injected at the natural footprint norm, unit q-vectors move the logits as much as z-vectors, full descendants and random directions (Pythia 13.8/13.9/13.8/13.5; GPT-2 30.0/30.3/30.1/30.6; Qwen 136/150/145/127; OLMo 21.5/24.8/22.9/20.9) with the same KL; two blocks later their response lands in the causal subspace of that level at 0.03-0.08, the same as random (0.02-0.07), against 0.28-0.61 for z-vectors; they persist like any vector (0.58-0.81 vs random 0.62-0.78) and reach other tokens no more than random (0.12-0.24 vs 0.12-0.23). Verdict: the leftover dimensions are carried by the near-identity residual transport (hence their weak self-prediction in e288) but under intervention they are indistinguishable from a random vector for the logits, the KL, the later core and the other tokens. At this resolution the rest of the descendant is not a second state variable; it is generic, carried and unread.
- Answers to the reviewer's four questions. (1) The causal coordinate: an 8-to-32-dimensional subspace inside the dominant directions of the descendant cloud, shared by identity, function and future (e288), nested with the linear-future subspace (e294). (2) It transports coherently: near-diagonal, evenly contracting, rotation-free over two blocks (e293). (3) It is manipulable one direction at a time, selectively but not completely (e289, e292). (4) The rest: generic under intervention; carried, not used (e295, e288).
- CLOSE OF ROUND 25: e01-e295, 244 scripts.

SESSION 26 (the reviewer's literature-derived program, run end to end under the original constraints: five models, no training, one to five minutes per run; nine scripts, 45 runs; items already covered or unrunnable skipped with reasons).
- e296 CAUSAL GEOMETRY. (A) A causal metric M = W W^T from the ridge map descendant -> logit-footprint scores (fitted on half the tokens): the M-cosine between descendant centroids recovers the held-out functional geometry better than the Euclidean cosine in 5/5 (Spearman 0.50 -> 0.69 GPT-2, 0.35 -> 0.60 Pythia, 0.65 -> 0.82 Qwen, 0.46 -> 0.57 OLMo, 0.49 -> 0.52 SmolLM2), and the cloud is lower-rank under M than under the Euclidean metric (participation rank 28.9 -> 6.3, 118 -> 48, 96 -> 64, 98 -> 15; SmolLM2 1.0 -> 1.1, its massive channel): Euclidean physically, low-rank causally. (K) Top-16 subspace energies: activation-variance vs descendant-variance 0.12-0.18 (chance 0.008-0.028); the ridge map's steepest directions vs either 0.01-0.02 (SmolLM2 0.09 and 0.61). The steepest directions of a ridge map are biased toward low-variance inputs, so this is read only as: the directions of largest fitted sensitivity are not the directions of largest variance, consistent with the isotropic gain of e273 and the compression results; it is not a claim that a sensitivity-dominant subspace exists. (C) In the fixed 16-dimensional function basis each coordinate two blocks later correlates with itself at 0.90-0.97 and with the best other coordinate at 0.23-0.34; self wins 16/16 in every model: persistent causal channels, not just a persistent subspace.
- e297 LAYERWISE DIMENSIONS (the within-pass analogue of the training result; supervised dimensions for 90% of the full score, identity / function / future / KL / PCA reconstruction, at every block after the write): the reconstruction dimension reaches the full scale within one to five blocks (Pythia 128 at block 3, 512 at 4, 1024 from 8; GPT-2 256 -> 768; Qwen and OLMo 256 -> 512; SmolLM2 1, its massive channel), while identity doubles slowly along the depth (8 -> 16 -> 32), function stays at 8-32 (Pythia alone grows to 64-128 in its last third), future stays at 8-16, KL at 4-32. The physical dimension explodes early; the causal dimensions creep.
- e298 ATTENTION ROUTING (per-head attention-output change at the source token over the three blocks after L; attention probabilities not returned by these models' attention implementation): held-out R2 from the 16-dimensional core +0.09 Pythia, +0.16 GPT-2, +0.12 Qwen, +0.16 OLMo, +0.15 SmolLM2; from the descendant's top-64 PCs +0.13 to +0.24; from a random 16-dimensional projection -0.00 to +0.07 (SmolLM2 +0.16, the massive channel); from the descendant's norm alone +0.01 to +0.03. The core predicts which heads react two to five times better than a random projection but explains only a tenth to a sixth of the variance: routing is at best weakly a function of the causal coordinate at this resolution.
- e299 PROPAGATION AS A FIELD. (G) The layer x token x feature tensor of the natural ablation: in Qwen, OLMo and SmolLM2 it is essentially rank one in features (top-8 feature modes hold 0.95-0.98 of the energy against 0.04-0.23 for random injections at the same tokens; participation rank 1.0-1.3 against 85-1091), the massive channel again; in GPT-2 and Pythia the WDD and random tensors have similar spectra (top-8 0.62 / 0.53 and 0.18 / 0.14). Two layer-modes hold 0.67-0.99 of the energy in every case (near-identity transport across depth). (F) The impulse response of a single injection at one token, measured at mid depth: 15-41% of the response energy lies at later tokens within 32 positions (the write 0.19-0.41, a core vector 0.14-0.26, a leftover vector 0.20-0.29, a random vector 0.18-0.35), so the Green's function is mostly local with a weak, roughly uniform spread; the natural write spreads somewhat more than the other families in 3/5.
- e300 CROSS-NEURON GEOMETRY (116-200 block-2 neurons with at least five tokens): median |cos| between neurons' core coordinates 0.22-0.28 against 0.03-0.06 for their descendant centroids, 0.02-0.04 for their writes and 0.01-0.03 for random vectors; participation rank of the cores 5.9-8.2 of 16 (SmolLM2 1.1), of the descendants 53-72, of the writes 74-151. Write similarity does not predict core similarity (Spearman 0.00-0.08), and predicts descendant similarity only in GPT-2 and Pythia (0.18-0.26); descendant similarity predicts core similarity (0.50-0.84). The cores of hundreds of neurons occupy a six-to-eight-dimensional effective space fixed by the downstream read-out, not by the writes' geometry: the core is a property of the network's receptor geometry into which every write is projected.
- e301 SECOND-ORDER WDD (seven scalings of the write at its own tokens, -1 to +2 times the natural coefficient, state and logit responses fitted as a delta + b delta^2): evenness cos(R(+1), R(-1)) at own tokens -0.69 to -0.98 for every ordinary neuron and every non-massive largest neuron (odd, i.e. linear), and +0.88 for SmolLM2's massive neuron (even; its logits +0.16), with a quadratic-to-linear energy ratio of 0.65 against 0.08-0.35 for the others; at foreign tokens the massive neuron is odd again (-0.41). At foreign tokens the quadratic share at the natural amplitude is 0.15-0.58 for all neurons (amplitude nonlinearity exists) but the response stays odd (-0.03 to -0.96). The set-point response of e290 is confirmed by the dose sweep and is unique to the massive neuron at its own tokens.
- e302 DOSE-RESPONSE AND INTERACTION IN CAUSAL COORDINATES (eight PLS coordinates): saturation F(2) / 2F(1) 0.88-1.26 (one broken coordinate in SmolLM2, 1.91, linear R2 0.09), sign asymmetry within +-0.2 for 33 of 40 coordinates (largest -0.69 OLMo, -0.45 Qwen), linear R2 0.82-1.00 in 39 of 40: linear coordinates, no thresholds, no saturation. The pairwise interaction matrix I_ij = E_ij - E_i - E_j (relative): off-diagonal mean 0.07 Pythia, 0.21 GPT-2, 0.28 SmolLM2, 0.31 OLMo, 0.48 Qwen (maximum 0.09-0.54), self-interaction (the amplitude nonlinearity of doubling one coordinate) 0.14-0.89, always larger than the cross terms, first-mode share 0.89-0.94: interactions are weak, uniform and rank one, without block or circuit structure. The coordinates are independent linear channels whose only strong nonlinearity is amplitude.
- e303 PREDICTING THE CORE FROM THE WRITE (chance 0.03-0.05): the write projected onto the core basis predicts the neuron's own core coordinate at cosine 0.71 GPT-2, 0.65 Pythia, 0.53 OLMo (identification 0.52-0.66), 0.32 SmolLM2 and 0.25 Qwen (0.18-0.22); the data-free operator does no better (0.14-0.61) and the transplant image is comparable (0.06-0.62; identification 0.26-0.71); random 0.01-0.09. In the GELU models the core coordinate is largely already in the atom, because the near-identity transport preserves the write's projection on the read-out directions; in the gated models most of it is constructed downstream. With e300: the map from writes to cores is fixed by the network, and its values for a given write are largely computable from the write.
- e304 IS THE HIERARCHY SPECIAL TO WDD WRITES? (30-vector families injected at the block-3 input: WDD writes, random unit directions, covariance-matched random directions, attention-head write directions of blocks 0-2, MLP writes of block 5): every family shows the same ladder. Reconstruction needs the full dimension for all (512-2048); vector identity 4-32 directions with full scores 0.90-1.00 for all families; function 4-32 with full scores 0.07-0.33 for all; future 4-64. Gains 0.66-1.23 for all families except covariance-matched directions in GPT-2 (3.49, the massive channel). The two-rung hierarchy is a property of residual-stream dynamics, not of neuron writes; WDD writes are not a privileged class of perturbation, which is the same lesson as e276 and e248 at the level of dimensionality.
- Skipped, with reasons: the SAE bridge (no published dictionaries for these five models on the box, and training one would break the constraint); circuit discovery from core-to-core correlations (would repeat the lineage result of e267 without a ground-truth graph to compare against); the dual basis (the PLS coordinates are orthonormal, so the dual is the basis itself: e289, e292, e302); descendant from core plus residual (an identity); core-to-core transfer across neurons (e288).
- Session 26 verdict, connecting the dots. The literature-derived program mostly sharpened rather than overturned: the causal coordinate is (i) the geometry of a causal metric under which the cloud is low-rank (e296), (ii) a set of persistent, independent, linear channels (e296, e302), (iii) fixed by the downstream read-out and largely computable from the write in GELU models (e300, e303), (iv) not specific to neuron writes (e304), (v) born early in depth as in training (e297), (vi) weakly related to attention routing (e298), (vii) mostly local in token space (e299), and (viii) violated only at the massive neuron's own tokens, whose response is even (e301). The strongest correction to the program's self-image is e304: what the descendant work characterised is the residual stream's treatment of any perturbation, and WDD's contribution is to supply the perturbations that the model itself makes.
- CLOSE OF ROUND 26: e01-e304, 253 scripts.

SESSION 27 (the reviewer's "what is P_l" program: operator identification, bottleneck localisation, persistent channels, forward versus backward; four scripts, 20 runs; bottleneck localisation by depth taken from e297).
- e305 OPERATOR IDENTIFICATION (5 models; data-free operators T(L->L+k), k = 1, 2, 4; forward read-out G from random injections; 16-dimensional spaces; chance 0.008-0.028): the function core's energy inside T's top input singular space 0.01-0.05, top output space 0.02-0.09, the update (T - I) input space 0.01-0.04, the slowest eigenmodes 0.01-0.03, the fastest 0.01-0.03, T's bottom input space ~0.02; inside G's top input singular space 0.01-0.04; the identity core and the descendant PCA the same. The gain of core directions under T equals that of random directions in every model and horizon (0.84/0.84, 0.88/0.88, 0.79/0.80, 0.54/0.53, 0.88/0.88 at k = 1). Verdict: the causal core is not an invariant, singular, slow, fast or update subspace of the transport operator, not the high-gain subspace of the read-out, and it has generic gain. It is neither a dynamical subspace nor a read-out bottleneck in the spectral sense.
- e306 SHARED COORDINATES ACROSS PERTURBATION FAMILIES (5 models; WDD, random, covariance-matched random, attention-head and later-MLP directions; 16-dimensional function and identity cores at +2 blocks, L and near the end): the function cores of physically unrelated families overlap at 0.02-0.06 two blocks after injection (within-family split-half reliability 0.27-0.60), at 0.14-0.32 at mid depth (reliability 0.24-0.53), and at 0.44-0.57 near the end (reliability 0.51-0.67; Pythia 0.57 = 0.57, Qwen 0.50 vs 0.52, SmolLM2 0.50 vs 0.52, OLMo 0.44 vs 0.51, GPT-2 0.55 vs 0.67). The identity cores stay family-specific throughout (0.02-0.16 against reliability 0.24-0.89). Verdict: the network has a shared functional coordinate system that unrelated perturbations are projected into, and it emerges with depth: family-specific two blocks after injection, partly shared at mid depth, fully shared near the output. Which vector was injected remains family-specific; what it does converges.
- e307 PERSISTENT CHANNELS ACROSS ALL DEPTHS (5 models; fixed 16-dimensional function basis from L, and from b+2; every consecutive pair of blocks): per-block gain 0.96-1.07 (early basis 0.89-1.11), self-R2 0.83-0.89 (worst block 0.39-0.73), mixing 0.02-0.06, the optimal assignment of coordinates between consecutive blocks equal to the identity at every block in 4/5 (1.00, minimum 1.00; SmolLM2 0.96-0.98 with one block at 0.25-0.81), largest off-diagonal correlation 0.41-0.49 (SmolLM2 0.67-0.86); channels born or dying (share of the core variance changing more than fourfold from the first to the last block) 0-1 in four models, 5-8 in SmolLM2 (its massive channel). Verdict: the channels keep their identities through the whole network, with unit gain, two to six percent mixing, no births and no deaths outside the massive channel. The taxonomy the reviewer asked for is nearly empty: all channels persist.
- e308 FORWARD VERSUS BACKWARD (5 models; backward sensitivity subspace = top-16 eigenvectors of the covariance of the true gradients of the 16 leading footprint directions with respect to the level-L state): its energy overlap with the forward function core 0.03-0.07, with the identity core 0.02-0.04, with the descendant PCA 0.03-0.06, with the activation PCA 0.04-0.07 (GPT-2 0.20, its massive channel), random 0.008-0.025. The first-order identity f = G d with the true Jacobian: correlation 0.36-0.45 over tokens and directions (SmolLM2 -0.02), median per-token cosine 0.58-0.67 (SmolLM2 0.45). Decoding from eight directions (identity / function / KL): the forward-backward intersection 0.38-0.72 / 0.20-0.43 / 0.36-0.63, the forward core's top eight 0.41-0.74 / 0.16-0.44 / 0.32-0.60, the backward top eight 0.25-0.52 / 0.13-0.31 / 0.18-0.54, random eight 0.22-0.42 / 0.09-0.27 / 0.22-0.54. Verdict: forward transport modes and backward sensitivity modes are nearly orthogonal; the information sits in the forward (data) core, the backward subspace decodes barely above random, and the intersection is just the forward core. The reviewer's hoped-for tiny shared space does not exist; the forward-backward decomposition does.
- BOTTLENECK BY DEPTH (from e297): physical dimension 128-256 one block after the write and 512-1024 from block four to eight on; transport (future-predictive) dimension 8-16 throughout; read-out dimensions 8-32 (function), 4-32 (KL), 8-32 growing (identity). The phase diagram is flat lines at tens under a line that rises to the full width within a few blocks.
- The answer to "what is P_l". Not an operator subspace (e305), not a sensitivity subspace (e308), not an intersection (e308). It is the dominant-variance subspace of the perturbation cloud itself (e288, e296), which the near-identity transport carries as a set of persistent channels (e307), which physically unrelated perturbations come to share as depth increases (e306), and which the read-out, being nearly isotropic in gain (e273, e305), simply reads where the perturbations are. The residual stream does not funnel perturbations into a special operator subspace; the perturbations of the model's own components live in a low-dimensional region, that region is transported almost unchanged, and function is read from it. The low-dimensional causal coordinate is the geometry of the model's own perturbations, not of its operators.
- CLOSE OF ROUND 27: e01-e308, 257 scripts.

SESSION 28 (the reviewer's twelve on the quotient picture; four scripts, 20 runs; the Qwen transfer run needed a memory fix and a solo rerun).
- e309 WHERE THE CLOUD'S GEOMETRY COMES FROM, AND CURVATURE (5 models): participation ranks at L: the natural descendant cloud 31 GPT-2, 130 Pythia, 106 Qwen, 105 OLMo (SmolLM2 1.0, its massive channel); the ordinary residual state 95, 121, 89, 260, 58; attention outputs 18-89; MLP outputs 70-394; the block-2 write rows 323-1238; random 428-854. The function core lies in the descendant cloud's own top-64 at 0.93-0.95, in the residual state's top-64 at only 0.21-0.34 (top-16: 0.11-0.17, ten to twenty times chance), in the attention outputs' at 0.06-0.17, the MLP outputs' at 0.08-0.22, the writes' at 0.05-0.17, random 0.03-0.11. Verdict: the causal coordinate is not the subspace of high residual variance, nor of any component's output variance, nor of the writes; the perturbation cloud has a geometry of its own. Curvature: the local participation rank of 50-neighbour patches is 22-30 against a global 31-130 (Pythia 30 vs 130, Qwen 26 vs 106, OLMo 28 vs 105, GPT-2 22 vs 31): the cloud is a curved manifold, locally about 25-dimensional and globally a hundred, so the 8-32-dimensional cores are local coordinates on it.
- e310 THE MANIFOLD SWEEP AND SYNTHETIC CONTROLS (5 models; 30-vector families at the block-3 input; natural manifold = top-64 directions of the block-2 output): perturbations orthogonal to the natural manifold (alpha = 0, manifold fraction 0.00) acquire the same ladder as natural ones (alpha = 1, fraction 0.37-0.76): identity 8-16 vs 4-32 directions, function 8-16 vs 8-32, future 4-32 vs 4-16, with the same full scores (identity 0.92-1.00 vs 0.77-1.00; function 0.09-0.25 vs 0.08-0.32; future 0.14-0.42 vs 0.14-0.66). Heavy-tailed, sparse, low-rank and shuffled-state perturbations show the same ladder (identity 4-32, function 8-32, future 4-32; the low-rank family's identity at 4, as it must). What does change along the sweep: the gain (GPT-2 0.98 to 3.32, the massive channel; others +14 to +25%), the physical rank of the images (falling from 190-683 to 87-639), and in Qwen and SmolLM2 the future score (0.42 to 0.66, 0.28 to 0.56). Verdict: theory A. The low-dimensional functional coordinate is a generic property of the residual dynamics: any perturbation, natural or not, acquires one; proximity to the natural manifold buys coherence of transport (lower-rank images, a more predictable future), not the funnel itself.
- e311 THE QUOTIENT BY FUNCTION (5 models; 150 perturbations: 50 WDD writes, 50 random, 50 attention writes): Spearman between physical distance at a level and final functional distance rises with depth from 0.06-0.17 one block after injection to 0.37-0.66 near the end (Qwen 0.13 to 0.66, SmolLM2 0.17 to 0.61, Pythia 0.16 to 0.40, GPT-2 0.17 to 0.38, OLMo 0.06 to 0.37). Functional distances measured through per-level data-free read-outs neither contract nor expand through the middle (median ratio between consecutive levels 0.96-1.00, fraction below one about a half) and contract mildly at the last step (0.88-0.95): the network transports an existing functional geometry and consolidates it at the end. Multiplicity: of the 5% functionally closest pairs, 38-48% are physically far at injection (baseline 50%) and 63-68% are cross-family (baseline 67%): functional equivalence is nearly independent of physical proximity and of family, a many-to-one map. Participation ranks fall from 115-139 (injected) to 41-97 (physical at L) to 23-64 (functional).
- e312 TRANSFER ACROSS PERTURBATION DISTRIBUTIONS (5 models): the function core fitted on one family decodes another family's function at 60-75% of the own-core score at mid depth (cross 0.07-0.16, own 0.11-0.22, random 0.04-0.13) and at 85-90% near the output (cross 0.15-0.31, own 0.18-0.34, random 0.07-0.16); identity does not transfer (cross at the random level) and is lost by the output for every injected family (0.04-0.12, chance 0.03). The causal metric fitted on one family scores another's functional geometry at the Euclidean level at mid depth (cross 0.22-0.53 vs Euclidean 0.26-0.44 vs own 0.56-0.76) and at the own-metric level near the output (cross 0.42-0.75 vs own 0.57-0.76). Cross-family superposition of physically dissimilar pairs: F(a + b) against F(a) + F(b) at cosine 0.98-1.00 with relative error 0.09-0.19, identical for functionally similar and dissimilar pairs. Verdict: the converged functional coordinate system and metric transfer across perturbation distributions near the output and only partly at mid depth; the convergence is linear read-out geometry, with no interaction when functionally equivalent states meet.
- The decision tree, resolved. Orthogonal perturbations join the same low-dimensional functional geometry (e310), so the funnel is a generic property of the residual dynamics; cross-family cores and metrics transfer near the output (e312); the quotient by function is many-to-one and independent of physical proximity (e311); the cloud's geometry is its own, curved, and not the residual variance (e309). By the reviewer's own criterion the exploratory phase ends here: the object is no longer WDD but the way a transformer's residual stream quotients physical perturbations into a low-dimensional, transportable, transferable functional state, with WDD supplying the model's own perturbations as the cleanest probes.
- CLOSE OF ROUND 28: e01-e312, 261 scripts.

SESSION 29 (the WDD-to-quotient connection, written in THEORY.md section 3g and tested; and the reviewer's final adversarial suite: new observables, intervention without refitting, composition; three scripts, 15 runs).
- THE CONNECTION. WDD writes the state as a ledger over atoms, x = sum_i c_i(t) w_i; transport is first-order, d_i = c_i J_t w_i; the quotient is linear, z = P^T delta. So z(joint perturbation) = sum_i c_i(t) z_i = Z c(t), with Z = P^T J-bar [w_1 ... w_N] the functional dictionary: the image of the WDD dictionary under transport and quotient. WDD reads c (which atom), the quotient reads Zc (what it does); Z has effective rank six to eight for hundreds of atoms (e300), so its fibres are the functional equivalence classes; in the GELU models Z is close to the WDD dictionary projected on the core (e303); sparse coding over transported atoms fails because the token-specific term is outside any fixed span while the quotient discards it. The two are coordinate systems of one linear map, fine and coarse, related by Z.
- e313 THE LEDGER PREDICTS THE FUNCTIONAL COORDINATE (5 models).
