(A) Random subsets of 4-8 candidate atoms ablated jointly; at the tokens where the subset carries the most ledger weight, the joint footprint's core coordinate against the ledger-weighted sum of the atoms' core coordinates (each z_i measured once, from the atom's transplant image in foreign contexts): median cosine 0.66 GPT-2, 0.54 Pythia, 0.68 OLMo, 0.34 Qwen, 0.30 SmolLM2; random-atom control -0.03 to +0.04; the same prediction in full dimension with the transplant images only 0.16-0.43. The WDD coefficients read at birth, times the atoms' functional coordinates, predict the functional coordinate of a joint perturbation far above chance, and better in the coarse coordinate than in the physical one. (B) The quotient's algebra on physically unrelated atom pairs: z(w_i + w_j) against z_i + z_j at cosine 0.97 GPT-2, 0.94 Pythia, 0.91 OLMo, 0.88 SmolLM2, 0.78 Qwen (full dimension 0.85-0.96). Verdict: the composite map is linear and the ledger is its input; WDD and the quotient are mathematically connected as claimed, most tightly in the GELU models and OLMo, more loosely in Qwen and SmolLM2, where the per-atom coordinate is more context-constructed (e303).
- e314 NEW OBSERVABLES (5 models; the core built from logit footprints only; held-out kNN; core-16 / descendant top-64 PCs / random-16 / norm alone): entropy change 0.15-0.34 / 0.19-0.30 / 0.13-0.22 / 0.00-0.15; next-token log-probability change 0.06-0.14 / 0.05-0.22 / 0.00-0.15 / 0.02-0.07; top-1 probability change 0.08-0.19 / 0.07-0.18 / 0.06-0.16 / -0.03-0.09; last-block attention-output norm change 0.18-0.53 / 0.20-0.58 / 0.11-0.33 / 0.04-0.17; next-token state-change norm 0.42-0.60 / 0.42-0.58 / 0.24-0.45 / 0.15-0.62; next-token state direction 0.07-0.47 / 0.09-0.48 / 0.05-0.38 / 0.02-0.33. Verdict: the coordinate generalises to observables it was not built from at the level of the full descendant and clearly above a random projection for the attention-norm and cross-token observables (about twice random), modestly for the entropy, and not for the behavioural scalars (next-token log-probability, top-1 probability), which nothing predicts well (at most 0.22 from 64 PCs).
- e315 INTERVENTION WITHOUT REFITTING (5 models; +/- the natural footprint norm along eight core directions; predictions made once from natural descendants): the future core coordinate two blocks later against the transport-map prediction, median cosine 0.95 GPT-2, 0.99 Pythia, 0.98 Qwen, 0.98 OLMo, 0.98 SmolLM2 (minimum over directions and signs 0.79-0.98); the logit change against the ridge prediction 0.13-0.32 (as e292); the KL positive for both signs in 8/8 directions in every model (even, as it must be) and 0.97-1.55 times random directions (no leverage advantage, as e276); the entropy change flips sign with the direction in 38-100% of coordinates (Pythia 1.00, SmolLM2 0.88, OLMo 0.62, GPT-2 0.50, Qwen 0.38), so it is not reliably odd. The cross-token measure was contaminated by dense injection (neighbouring tokens were also injected) and is discarded. Verdict: the coordinate's own dynamics are predicted without refitting at 0.95-0.99 under intervention; its projection to the logits is predicted only partly; the scalar observables behave as a quadratic (KL) and a mixed (entropy) function of it.
- The adversarial suite's three answers: new observables partly (attention and cross-token yes, behavioural scalars no); intervention without refitting yes for the state and partly for the logits; composition yes (0.78-0.97). The quotient is an approximately linear causal state representation for the state's own dynamics, and a partial one for the output.
- CLOSE OF ROUND 29: e01-e315, 264 scripts. Experiment program frozen.

SESSION 30 (the external-researcher test: can the framework explain parts of GPT-2 that SAEs, the logit and tuned lenses, the Jacobian lens or neuron-level attribution cannot? An end-to-end application study, e316 with a data-free operator atlas and e316b with exact per-neuron coordinates, five models; and a priority check of the literature, RELATED_WORK.md section 10).
- e316 OPERATOR ATLAS (blocks 0-5, 9k-49k atoms; coordinates z_i = P^T (w_i T_b) from data-free transport operators): validation against exact transplant coordinates for 240 atoms, cosine 0.58 GPT-2, 0.57 SmolLM2, 0.40 Qwen, 0.30 Pythia, 0.28 OLMo (identification 0.07-0.34 at chance 0.004): a coarse approximation. Atlas structure: effective rank 6.8-11.3 over tens of thousands of atoms, 16 classes all spanning three or more blocks, within-class cosine 0.55-0.58, ledger weight spread evenly over classes (heaviest 0.09-0.10). (a) Ablating the 64 heaviest-class block-2 neurons jointly gives a functional contribution no more coherent than 64 random neurons (coherence 0.18-0.39 vs 0.18-0.21; predicted-vs-actual core cosine 0.10-0.72 vs 0.09-0.24, the 0.72 being SmolLM2's massive channel). (b) Substituting a block-2 candidate by a same-class block-4 atom with the coordinate-matched coefficient makes the logit deviation larger, not smaller (restoration -0.19 to -0.49; a random atom -0.02 to -0.05). (c) The atlas predicts the logit effect of joint ablations at cosine 0.01-0.14, direct logit attribution 0.01-0.10, an averaged-Jacobian lens of the writes 0.00-0.02, transport plus read-out without the quotient 0.01-0.10: all poor.
- e316b EXACT ATLAS (every block-2 neuron's coordinate measured by injection over about 24 tokens): effective rank 6.9-11.6, within-class cosine 0.51-0.64. (a) Class ablation again no better than random (coherence 0.15-0.41 vs 0.20-0.24; predicted-vs-actual 0.05-0.36 vs 0.20-0.39). (b) Same-block substitution by the nearest same-class atom (coordinate cosine 0.76-0.81) with the matched coefficient: logit restoration -0.22 to -0.59, core restoration -0.20 to +0.12 (GPT-2 +0.12 the only positive); a random atom -0.07 to 0.00. Equivalence in the 16-dimensional coordinate does not make two neurons substitutable, even at the level of the coordinate itself. (c) For random six-neuron subsets, the ledger-weighted coordinates predict the joint core coordinate at 0.21-0.38 (candidates in e313: 0.30-0.68), and the logit effect at 0.00-0.03 against 0.02-0.16 for direct logit attribution (GPT-2 0.16) and 0.01-0.06 for transport plus read-out: at the logits the atlas is the worst of the three.
- Verdict of the application study: NEGATIVE for new explanatory power at the level of GPT-2's outputs. The functional coordinate is a good state variable for the intermediate residual stream (its own future under intervention at 0.95-0.99, e315; ledger-to-coordinate prediction at 0.21-0.68, e313, e316b), but its classes are not functional units, coordinate-equivalent neurons are not interchangeable (as e264 first found), and no attribution method built on it beats direct logit attribution for joint effects, all of which sit below cosine 0.2. An external researcher gains, from this program, a validated intermediate-level object and a set of negative results about what it does not buy; not a new way to explain GPT-2's behaviour that the lenses or SAEs lack.
- Literature priority check (RELATED_WORK.md, section 10): Transformer Field Theory (Olivieri and Perez Rodriguez, May 2026) owns first-order transport, Green functions, composition and the local linear regime; Fernando and Guitchounts (May 2026) own the Jacobian spectral bottleneck, which e305 and e308 distinguish from the functional coordinate; Sarkar and Deka (May 2026) define a causal dimensionality through SAE attribution patching and a representational-causal wedge, close in name, different in construction and in result (no null space here, e273, e285); the J-lens (July 2026) is the averaged read-out the program tested against; ROTATE (COLM 2026) and VPD (Goodfire, April 2026) are the weight-first neighbours; Sharma, Dawes and Raval (April 2026) draw the decodable-versus-used distinction the program draws between provenance and function. Not found as such: the read-out-induced equivalence structure on arbitrary perturbations measured by held-out prediction and intervention, its acquisition off the natural manifold, its cross-family convergence, its independence from the operators' spectra, and its algebraic link to the write dictionary tested by joint ablation.
- CLOSE OF ROUND 30: e01-e316b, 266 scripts. The program is done.

SESSION 31, PART 1 (the quotient program, waves one to four: e317-e338, 22 scripts, ~110 runs; the box lost its network for about two hours mid-program and the queued waves ran through it).
- e317 ONE QUOTIENT FOR NINE OBSERVABLES: sixteen-dimensional quotients fitted to the logit footprint, the removal KL, the entropy change, the top-1 change, the next-token log-probability change, the state two and four blocks on, the last-level state and the source identity overlap pairwise at 0.37-0.45 on average, against split-half reliabilities of 0.38-0.51 and chance 0.008-0.028; decoding any observable from another observable's quotient gives 0.89-1.13 of the own-quotient score. The quotient is universal across observables, not observable-relative.
- e318 HORIZON: the quotient with respect to the state k blocks later overlaps the logit quotient at 0.55-0.70 one block on and converges slowly to 0.58-0.79 at the last level.
- e319 RESOLUTION: coarsening the footprint to its top-r directions gives a quotient of dimension min(r, 8-16); the intrinsic dimension is a property of the map, not of the observable's resolution.
- e322 BIRTH: the quotient fitted at birth (a subspace of write space) decodes the footprint as well as the mid-level quotient (0.24-0.42 vs 0.21-0.45) though the two subspaces barely overlap (0.05-0.12; GPT-2 0.45); applied unchanged at mid depth it still decodes at 0.21-0.40. Redundant encodings of one function at different levels.
- e320 LONG-HORIZON CHANNELS (fixed basis): from the middle level the optimal assignment stays the identity to the last block in GPT-2, Pythia and OLMo (0.88-1.00; diagonal energy 0.66-0.82; R2 0.51-0.63) and not in Qwen (0.88 but diagonal 0.26, R2 0.16) or SmolLM2 (massive channel); from an early basis the identity survives in GPT-2 and OLMo (1.00), Pythia (0.88), not Qwen (0.25) or SmolLM2 (0.06).
- e321 REFITTED BASES: with the quotient refitted at every level, consecutive coordinates keep their index 65-76% of the time (worst step 0.12-0.56), matched correlation 0.76-0.82, consecutive bases share 0.58-0.77 of their energy; composing the matchings over the depth returns only 6-38% of coordinates to themselves. The subspace persists; individual coordinates persist only in a fixed basis, and a refitted basis rotates inside it.
- e323 NONLINEAR READ-OUT: kNN on the 16-dimensional quotient (0.21-0.45) beats a linear decoder on the full descendant (0.24-0.36) in 4/5 and matches kernel ridge on the full descendant (0.30-0.42); linear decoding of the 16 coordinates is 0.04-0.29. Linear coordinate, nonlinear read-out; the sixteen coordinates keep what a nonlinear decoder can extract from the whole descendant.
- e324 LOCAL QUOTIENTS: quotients refitted in eight regions of the cloud overlap the global one at 0.15-0.21 and each other at 0.03-0.09, but decode no better than the global quotient inside their regions (0.26-0.51 vs 0.24-0.49): the region-specific bases are refit noise, and the global quotient suffices.
- e325 FLAT CLASSES: the midpoint of two equivalent, physically distant atoms has the average coordinate (cosine 0.73-0.86, as for physically near pairs 0.74-0.90; dissimilar pairs 0.46-0.78); effects noisier (0.16-0.50).
- e326 EQUIVALENCE ALGEBRA: for a ~ a' and b ~ b', the sums a + b and a' + b' are as equivalent in the coordinate (0.59-0.80) as the pairs themselves (0.59-0.72) and more than re-paired sums (0.18-0.65); at the logits the equivalence is weak for pairs and sums alike (0.01-0.31).
- e327 SCALAR AND SIGN: z(alpha a) equals alpha z(a) at cosine 0.75-1.00 for alpha in 0.5, 2, -1, 4 (0.90-1.00 in 4/5); equivalent pairs stay equivalent under scaling and sign in the coordinate (0.21-0.85), not at the logits (about 0).
- e328 EQUIVALENCE UNDER TRANSPORT: pairs equivalent at the middle level (0.81-0.89) remain so two blocks on (0.55-0.79; random 0.00-0.28) and decay toward the random level at the last block (0.20-0.52 vs 0.13-0.36).
- e329 AMPLITUDE AND DIMENSION: from 0.25x to 2x the dimensions are constant (identity 8, function 16-32, future 16); at 4x-16x the function dimension falls (to 4-8), the images leave linearity (0.61-0.95 cosine to the 0.25x image at 8-16x) and the quotient rotates away from the 1x one (overlap 0.09-0.42). The causal dimension does not expand in the nonlinear regime; it collapses.
- e330 EQUIVALENCE AND AMPLITUDE: equivalent pairs stay coordinate-equivalent at 4x and 8x (0.84-0.97), but so do random pairs (0.27-0.92 at 8x): at high amplitude all responses collapse toward a common direction, which is the same collapse as e329.
- e331 MASSIVE-CHANNEL FAMILIES: perturbations concentrated on the three massive channels have their own quotient (overlap with the random family's 0.11-0.25 against 0.38-0.66 for random at 4x vs 1x), similar dimensions, higher gain in the gated models (1.17-1.80 vs 0.71-1.26), and an even response at 4x in OLMo (+0.42, the set-point signature), odd elsewhere.
- e332 THE FUNCTIONAL DICTIONARY IS NOT PRIVILEGED: exact coordinates of 1024 block-2 atoms have participation rank 7.9-11.9; transported random directions 5.6-10.5; a matching pursuit needs 6 atoms to represent a random quotient point to 10% error, exactly as many random 16-vectors or transported random directions (6-7); the fraction of a transported atom's energy inside the quotient is 0.019-0.045, the same as a random direction's (0.014-0.061). WDD atoms are not a sparse or concentrated basis of the quotient.
- e333 THE WHOLE MLP WRITE FROM THE LEDGER: the coordinate of ablating the entire block-2 MLP output is predicted by the full ledger times exact atom coordinates at 0.40 GPT-2, 0.39 Pythia, 0.36 SmolLM2, 0.29 Qwen, 0.14 OLMo, but by the top-8 atoms at -0.19 to +0.20 and the top-32 at -0.24 to +0.30 (negative in the GELU models): the functional coordinate of a block's write is carried by its many small-coefficient atoms, which is what a sparse ledger drops. Random coefficients 0.00; the write pushed through the operator equals the operator sum (0.03-0.39), as linearity requires.
- e334 NO VOCABULARY SEMANTICS: atoms in the same functional class share vocabulary projections no more than atoms in different classes (cosine 0.001-0.008 vs about 0; Jaccard of top-20 tokens 0.001-0.002 both); Spearman between vocabulary similarity and coordinate similarity 0.03-0.07. The quotient's classes are not readable through the logit lens.
- e335 UNIVERSAL BASIS: a basis fitted to nothing (the cloud's top-16 PCs) scores within 0.02 of the own quotients for logits, future and KL and lower for identity (0.47-0.80 vs 0.63-0.96); the smallest basis at which every observable reaches 90% of its own score is 16 (GPT-2, Pythia) or 32 (Qwen, OLMo, SmolLM2).
- e336 NO NULL SPACE, NO COSETS: the logit response along the read-out's bottom-8 directions equals the random response (ratio 0.86-1.02); the difference a - a' of two equivalent atoms is a full-strength perturbation (logit response equal to random differences and to a alone; core norm 0.03-0.46 vs 0.04-0.56 random). Functional equivalence is not a coset of a null space; the quotient is statistical, not an exact linear quotient.
- e337 HOMOMORPHISM: F(a + b) vs F(a) + F(b) over forty cross-family pairs at the natural amplitude: cosine 0.99-1.00, relative error 0.08-0.15 (worst pair 0.10-0.41), the same in the core. Additivity holds globally at this amplitude.
- e338 FIBRES: among 1024 atoms, the functional spread of physical neighbourhoods equals that of random sets (0.48-0.64 vs 0.49-0.66), the physical spread of functional neighbourhoods equals random (0.67-0.68 vs 0.68), the fibre dimension equals random (18.2-18.8), and functionally closest pairs are physically far 40-47% of the time (baseline 50%). Physical and functional geometries are unrelated at the atom scale; the fibres have no detectable structure.
- Interim reading. The quotient is one object for every observable, homogeneous, additive, transported with decay, persistent as channels in a fixed basis while its refitted basis rotates, read out nonlinearly, without a null space or coset structure, without fibre structure, collapsing rather than expanding beyond the linear range, and not founded on the WDD atoms as a privileged or semantic basis; the sparse part of the WDD ledger is not where a block's functional coordinate lives.

SESSION 31, PART 2 (waves five to seven: e339-e356 minus e350 and e354, 16 scripts, 81 runs; the quotient program totals 40 scripts and 191 runs today, 231 with the morning's e310-e316b).
- e339 THE COORDINATE FROM THE STATE ALONE: the natural descendant's core coordinate is predicted on held-out tokens by the residual state at L at R2 0.54-0.67 (SmolLM2 -0.10), by the birth-level state 0.51-0.65, by the downstream state 0.51-0.66, by the source identity 0.45-0.74, by the atom's coordinate times its coefficient (no context) 0.32-0.75, by the coefficient alone 0.02-0.04; combining state and coefficient 0.65-0.73; the descendant's own PCs 0.99. Two thirds of the coordinate is implicit in the state or the source; a third is constructed.
- e340 LEAKAGE AUDITS AND PERMUTATION TEST: function / identity / KL from the quotient 0.21-0.45 / 0.47-0.80 / 0.36-0.64; from the norm alone 0.06-0.19 / 0.02-0.11 / 0.16-0.64 (GPT-2's KL is largely norm); from position 0.05-0.21; from the source identity 0.14-0.29 / 1.00 / 0.13-0.56; from the coefficient 0.05-0.19; from the original write 0.20-0.34 / 0.95-1.00 / 0.13-0.52; from a random 16-subspace 0.12-0.34 / 0.36-0.62 / 0.22-0.60; from a covariance-matched random 16-subspace 0.21-0.42 / 0.45-0.85 / 0.32-0.63. The permutation test: a quotient fitted to SHUFFLED targets scores 0.20-0.43 / 0.48-0.83 / 0.32-0.62, the same as the real one; shuffling the descendants instead collapses everything (0.04-0.18 / 0.03-0.05 / about 0). Verdict: the supervised fit contributes nothing measurable; the quotient's scores are those of the cloud's dominant-variance directions, whichever target selects them.
- e341 WHAT READS THE COORDINATE: the later MLPs. The change of the MLP activations in the three blocks after L (top-64 PCs) is predicted from the 16 coordinates at R2 0.48-0.62 (from 64 descendant PCs 0.67-0.82; random-16 0.11-0.26); head-output norm changes only at 0.09-0.16 (as e298); activation-sparsity change about 0; top-32 recruited neurons overlap 0.12-0.21 against a 0.02-0.08 baseline. (SmolLM2's massive channel saturates all inputs.)
- e342 TRYING TO DESTROY THE QUOTIENT: sixteen families at 1x and 4x, own-quotient score over random-16 score never below 1.11 (weakest: transport-top directions 1.11-1.36 in Qwen, SmolLM2, GPT-2; one-hot 1.19 GPT-2; parallel-to-mean, sparse, massive-channel and covariance-amplified at 4x 1.36-1.64 in Pythia and OLMo). No family fails, with the caveat that own scores are modest everywhere (0.15-0.59) and WDD writes themselves are among the least decodable families (0.08-0.21 own).
- e343 TOKEN TOPOLOGY: quotients fitted on early vs late positions overlap at 0.12-0.38 (chance 0.01-0.03) and decode each other as well as themselves; sequence halves 0.26-0.47, cross equal to own; alphabetic vs non-alphabetic tokens overlap only 0.03-0.16 and asymmetrically: the alphabetic quotient decodes non-alphabetic tokens at 0.31-0.43 (own 0.44-0.51) while the non-alphabetic quotient decodes alphabetic tokens at 0.10-0.32 (own 0.16-0.38). Context-general across positions and texts; the punctuation-and-number tokens have a narrower quotient of their own.
- e344 NORMALIZATION: norm-preserving injections (direction change only) keep the dimension, the scores and the identity of standard injections (overlap of quotients 0.18-0.53, the vectors differ); pure norm scalings carry no identity (0.06) and a generic function score (own 0.27-0.44, random 0.15-0.33); 4x amplitude lowers identity in OLMo (0.29) and rotates the quotient (0.17-0.36).
- e345 FINE AMPLITUDE: along the core directions the paired response stays linear (deviation under 20%) up to 10x the footprint norm in GPT-2 and SmolLM2, 3x in Qwen and OLMo, beyond 30x in Pythia; sign asymmetry appears at 1-30x; KL leaves its quadratic law at 3-30x; the second derivative relative to the slope is -0.07 to +0.03.
- e346 WHAT THE DISCARDED DIMENSIONS KNOW: the top-64 PCs of the complement of the core decode identity 0.67-0.89 (core-16 0.43-0.77), function 0.21-0.39 (0.17-0.43), future 0.35-0.53 (0.26-0.54), token identity 0.58-0.74 (0.46-0.69), position 0.09-0.43, sequence 0.19-0.51; a random 64-subspace does the same. The information is redundant across the cloud; the core is one of many equivalent carriers, not the carrier.
- e347 COMPRESSION BASELINES: dimension for 90% of the full kNN score: PLS 4-16, PCA 4-32, CCA 8-16, kernel PCA 8-16, reduced-rank ridge 4-32, random 32-128, ICA 32-64, random Fourier features 128; at 16 dimensions PCA, PLS, CCA and kernel PCA all sit within 0.03 of the full descendant. Supervision buys nothing over PCA.
- e348 CANCELLATION AND NORM-PRESERVING STEERING: injecting the negative of the footprint centroid at the block-(L+1) input restores 15-18% of the ablation's logit deviation; injecting the negative of its core component alone restores 1-6%; a random vector -7 to -24%. The logit effect is not carried by the 16 coordinates. Norm-preserving steering keeps 95-100% of the unconstrained paired response: steering is direction-driven.
- e349 TRAINING: function dimension 2 at step 0, 8 at 1000, 16 at 4000-16000, 32 at 64000 and final; own-16 falls 0.99 to 0.21 while random-16 falls 0.99 to 0.12; identity 1.00 to 0.65; the WDD-vs-random quotient overlap near the output rises from 0.07 (step 0) and 0.25 (1000) to 0.48-0.50 (4000 onward), never at mid depth (0.05-0.15); the off-manifold family's dimension 2 to 8 to 32, the same as the random family's; a random family's images stay linear at 4x (0.93-0.99) at every checkpoint. The cross-family convergence and the funnel for off-manifold perturbations appear by step 4000, with dispersal.
- e351 ATTENTION-ONLY AND MLP-ONLY TRANSPORT: with the attention outputs of blocks 3..L frozen, a random family's images keep cosine 0.90-0.96 to normal and a quotient overlap of 0.30-0.62; with the MLPs frozen, cosine 0.28-0.80, overlap 0.03-0.37, gain 1.33-2.69 (the MLPs are the dampers); with both frozen the image is the injected vector and its own-quotient score is comparable (0.11-0.34). Transport of a token's own image is the identity path plus the MLPs; attention adds little to it.
- e352 THE LIGHT CONE: with the attention outputs of the later blocks frozen, no energy reaches other tokens (0.00 at every level); normally the fraction of the response at later tokens grows from 0.08-0.20 two blocks after injection to 0.36-0.75 at the last level, and the width holding 90% of it grows to the whole remaining context (168-320 tokens); the write and a random vector spread alike.
- e353 COORDINATE ALGEBRA: triples add with error 0.07-0.50, reduced to 0.01-0.20 by pairwise correction (third-order terms small); superposing 2, 4, 8 coordinates at the footprint norm each gives errors 0.12-0.65, 0.12-0.61, 0.15-0.82 (the total norm grows as the square root of the count, and the response leaves the linear range); permuting the coordinates before injection permutes the effects (1.00).
- e355 THE FUNCTIONAL KERNEL: the kernel of logit effects over 150 mixed perturbations has participation rank 4.3-22.4 with a long tail (41-71 eigenvalues for 90% of the trace); the coordinate kernel has rank 2.2-10.8 and aligns with it at 0.09-0.47, the full-image kernel at 0.27-0.46, random 0.00. The low dimension is partly a kernel phenomenon and partly not: the functional kernel's tail is not in the coordinates.
- e356 ONE-TO-MANY: the same direction injected in different contexts has coordinate coherence 0.25-0.41, effect coherence 0.14-0.27, image coherence 0.27-0.53; the context variation of the coordinate is not linearly predictable from the token's state (R2 -0.16 to -0.36); the model's own writes are no more coherent than random directions.
- What the program established about the quotient (with the morning's e310-e316b). Positive: it is one object for every observable (e317, e335) and for every perturbation family (e310, e342); its dimension is a property of the map, 8-32 at the natural amplitude (e319, e329); it is homogeneous and additive in the coordinate (e327, e337, e353), linear along its directions to 3-10x (e345), read out nonlinearly (e323), read by the later MLPs (e341), transported by the identity path and the MLPs with attention carrying the cross-token spread to the whole context (e351, e352), persistent as a subspace across depth and as channels only in a fixed basis (e320, e321), context-general across positions and texts with a narrower version for punctuation-and-number tokens (e343), two thirds implicit in the state and the source (e339), born by step 4000 of training together with the cross-family convergence (e349), and it collapses rather than expands beyond the linear range (e329, e330). Negative, and decisive for the wording: a quotient fitted to shuffled targets scores the same (e340), PCA equals PLS equals CCA (e347), the discarded 64-dimensional complement decodes everything as well as the 16-dimensional core (e346), there is no null space and no coset structure (e336), the fibres are random sets (e338), the core component alone cancels nothing at the logits (e348), and the WDD atoms are neither a sparse nor a concentrated nor a semantic basis of it (e332, e334), with a block's functional coordinate carried by the small-coefficient tail of the ledger rather than its sparse head (e333). The accurate description is therefore not "a 16-dimensional causal coordinate" but: the descendant cloud of any perturbation family is a low-rank object whose every moderate-dimensional high-variance projection carries the same causal information redundantly, so that a transformer's response to perturbations is compressible but not structured into a privileged subspace, a null space or classes.
- CLOSE OF ROUND 31: e01-e356, 290 experiment scripts plus helpers, 1,458 result files. Not run from the reviewer's list, for the record: the SAE bridge (no dictionaries), architecture ablations that require retraining, cross-model coordinate alignment (no shared basis across models), LayerNorm removal (changes the model), width/depth scaling (five models only).

SESSION 32 (phase 2 on a rented A100 80GB, 2026-09-23, anchored on the LessWrong post "What if not Circuits?" and its comments; e357-e373, 21 experiment scripts plus analyses and helpers, about 255 runs on the five models; the post was treated as an anchor, not an agenda, and three angles that came up mid-run were chased: first-order selection versus use, the curvature of the loss in head-scale space, and the reliability ceiling of logit signatures).
- e357 INDUCTION POSITIVE CONTROL (repeated random tokens, loss on the second copy; heads mean-ablated; the top heads by single effect and random heads; GPT-2 and SmolLM2 with 24 sequences and top-16, the others with 12 and top-12): induction works everywhere (first-copy loss 10.95-11.13, second copy 0.12-0.57). Top heads interact 2.7-9 times more than random heads (0.40-0.94 vs 0.05-0.15), with super-additive signed interaction +0.21 to +0.82 (28-68% of top pairs super-additive, 1-14% sub-additive: redundancy), and joint ablation of the top-8 is non-additive at 0.38-1.75 against 0.07-0.22 for random 8-sets (joint over sum of singles 7.1 GPT-2, 5.7 Pythia, 1.8-1.9 Qwen and OLMo, 1.0 SmolLM2). Spectral clusters of the interaction matrix do not recover the attention labels (ARI -0.01 to 0.22), and the ablation-critical heads are often not attention-labelled induction heads (Qwen 1 of 12, OLMo 3 of 12). The same heads on natural text interact at 0.12-0.47 against 0.06-0.14 for random heads (1.3x GPT-2, 1.4x SmolLM2, 2x Pythia, 4x Qwen, 3.6x OLMo), while heads selected by their natural-text effect interact at 0.23-0.41 against 0.08-0.14 with non-additivity 0.28-0.52 against 0.08-0.10 (overlap with the induction set 0.06-0.42). Neurons ranked by attribution: 0.09-0.26 vs 0.01-0.06 on the task (attribution against exact single effect Spearman 0.94-0.98 in GPT-2).
- e358 IOI POSITIVE CONTROL (four templates, both name orders, logit difference, per-group means): baseline logit difference 3.10-6.19 at accuracy 0.98-1.00. Top heads interact at 0.13-0.22 against 0.03-0.10 (1.4x Qwen to 7x GPT-2); non-additivity of the top-8 0.18-0.30 against 0.04-0.12. In GPT-2, 10 of the top 12 heads are published IOI heads (negative name movers 10.7 and 11.10 with effects +1.42 and +0.79, S-inhibition 8.10, 7.9, 7.3, name movers 9.9, 10.0, 9.6, backups 11.2, 9.7); clusters match the published classes at ARI 0.24 (output movers vs the S-inhibition side); ablating the top 8 together moves the logit difference by -0.08 against a sum of singles of +1.35 (series structure plus self-repair). On natural text the same heads interact at 0.09-0.20 against 0.07-0.13 (Qwen below random), though their natural interaction clusters match the IOI clusters in GPT-2 (ARI 0.69) and OLMo (0.67). IOI-attributed neurons interact strongly only in Qwen (0.33 vs 0.01).
- e366 CONDITIONAL NATURAL TEXT (the induction-task top-12 and 12 random heads, natural text scored separately at induction-applicable positions, 9-10% of tokens, at size-matched random positions, and at all positions): interaction at applicable positions 0.21-0.50 against 0.05-0.13 for random heads, at matched random positions 0.12-0.33 against 0.04-0.11 (the top heads' interaction is 1.3-2.2 times higher at applicable than at matched positions in every model; strongest in SmolLM2, 0.41 vs 0.19); non-additivity 0.24-0.80 applicable vs 0.18-0.62 matched. Mean single effect at applicable against matched positions: 9x GPT-2 (+0.021 vs +0.002), 12x SmolLM2, 1.5x Pythia, but 0.5x Qwen and 0.25x OLMo, whose induction-task top heads matter more away from induction positions (they are mostly not attention-labelled).
- e359 BATCH-LEVEL CO-SELECTION (the post's proposal: s_u(B) = <dL_B/dtheta_u, theta_u> for every head's output slice and a sample of MLP rows; 160 natural, 64 induction and 64 IOI batches): natural batches have a weak global mode (share 0.02-0.06) and 7-11 modes above a shuffled null; after removing the global mode, modularity is barely above shuffled (0.07-0.14 vs 0.05-0.06) and the strongest correlations are same-layer (0.34-0.74 of the top 100 vs 0.03-0.08 chance): co-selection structure is mostly layer structure. Mixed batches add a batch-type mode (share 0.31-0.41). The mixed-batch test of induction-selected heads (+0.76 to +0.81 in SmolLM2 and Pythia) is circular (heads chosen on the same batch-type contrast) and is superseded by e363.
- e363 CO-SELECTION CONTROL (circuit defined without co-selection, by attention on repeated random tokens; heads only; 192 natural batches of 4 x 256 tokens; each batch's induction content): the attention-defined induction heads co-select above random sets at z +2.2 GPT-2, +6.5 SmolLM2, +2.4 Pythia, +8.5 Qwen, +1.5 OLMo after global-mode removal (layer-matched z +2.0, +4.9, +2.6, +9.1, +1.1; mean correlations only +0.03 to +0.18), and their selection tracks the batch's induction content only in SmolLM2 (-0.31 against a null of 0.00 +- 0.09) and Qwen (-0.12 +- 0.08). Previous-token heads co-select in Pythia (+7.3) and Qwen (+4.5). In mixed batches the global mode absorbs the circuit (SmolLM2 raw +0.37, global-removed +0.01).
- e363 ACROSS PYTHIA TRAINING: no induction heads at step 512; the induction heads' co-selection is z +5.0 at step 1000, +8.3 at 2000, +10.8 at 3000, +9.4 at 4000, +11.1 at 8000, +11.0 at 16000, +6.5 at 33000 and +2.4 at the final step 143000; batch-content tracking -0.07 to -0.22 from step 1000 to 33000 and 0.00 at the end.
- e363 / e368 ACROSS OLMO TRAINING (steps 1000, 4000, 16000, 64000, 256000, 1.0M and the final 1.454M): attention-defined induction heads are absent at step 1000, one at 4000 (seven previous-token heads, which co-select at z +7.4), four at 16000 and five or six after. Batch-level co-selection of the induction heads is z +5.9 at 16000, +4.0 at 64000, +3.4 at 256000, +2.4 at 1.0M and +1.5 at the end: the Pythia rise-and-fade replicates at batch level. Token-level co-selection does not fade monotonically (z +8.4, +4.0, +5.6, +5.7, +7.7; AUC 0.94, 0.65, 0.86, 0.91, 0.96), possibly because OLMo-0724's last stage anneals on a different data mix.
- e368 TOKEN-LEVEL CO-SELECTION (the selection signal split by write position, c_h(t) = <dL/dz_h(t), z_h(t)>, over 8160 natural tokens): induction heads co-select at z +2.8 GPT-2, +15.0 SmolLM2, +4.8 Pythia, +8.4 Qwen, +7.7 OLMo (layer-matched +3.1, +8.3, +3.4, +5.7, +5.9), two to three times the z of 64-token windows in SmolLM2 and OLMo; the applicable-position contrast finds the attention-defined induction heads at AUC 0.41, 0.87, 0.52, 0.88, 0.96. Across Pythia training (steps 1000, 2000, 4000, 16000, 33000, final): z +7.5, +9.9, +18.4, +16.4, +9.3, +4.8 and AUC 0.86, 0.87, 0.91, 0.83, 0.76, 0.52: token-level co-selection of the circuit rises after it forms, peaks at 4000-16000 and fades by the end.
- e369 TOKEN-LEVEL ABLATION CO-EFFECTS (every head mean-ablated once, per-token loss change over 4080 natural tokens, analysed as e368): induction heads co-vary at z +5.3 GPT-2, +11.6 SmolLM2, +7.6 Pythia, +12.1 Qwen, +6.5 OLMo; the applicable-position contrast finds them at AUC 0.82, 0.97, 0.53, 0.76, 0.94. In GPT-2 ablation finds the circuit (0.82) where the gradient does not (0.41); in Pythia neither does at the final step (the circuit is highly redundant: single-head effects at applicable positions +0.006).
- e370 / e370b SELECTION IS NOT USE: induction-applicable positions are confident (p of the correct token 0.65-0.75, 38-55% above 0.9, against 0.17-0.30 elsewhere), so softmax saturation cannot explain GPT-2 (SmolLM2 is more confident and keeps its gradient). At applicable positions the induction heads' sign-flipped gradient selection against their ablation effect: GPT-2 -0.0020 vs +0.0184, SmolLM2 +0.0195 vs +0.0235, Pythia +0.0013 vs +0.0059, Qwen +0.0093 vs +0.0081, OLMo +0.0153 vs +0.0164. Over all tokens, the induction heads' mean selection is about zero (-0.0014 GPT-2, -0.0002 SmolLM2) while their mean use is positive (+0.0035, +0.0024); across heads, selection and use correlate at Spearman +0.36 to +0.39 (GPT-2, SmolLM2); median |t| of per-head mean selection 2.1 GPT-2 (evaluated off its training distribution) and 1.3 SmolLM2, against 2.0-2.5 for use. At a trained optimum every head's mean first-order selection vanishes; what remains is the token-level variation of local mis-scaling, which tracks use where a head is locally under-scaled and not where it sits at its optimum.
- e371 CURVATURE IN HEAD-SCALE SPACE (each head's write multiplied by a_h; gradient, Hessian and per-chunk gradient correlation at a = 1; zero-ablation is a_h = 0, so second order predicts E_i = -g_i + H_ii/2 and E_ij - E_i - E_j = H_ij): single zero-ablation effects are ranked by the first-order prediction at Spearman 0.22-0.72 and by the second-order one at 0.25-0.94 (0.63-0.94 in 8 of 10 conditions), but magnitudes fail (second-order R2 negative in 7 of 10; best 0.74 Pythia induction, 0.64 SmolLM2 natural). Measured pairwise interactions exceed the local curvature by 2.4-48x (top block: GPT-2 0.225 vs 0.039, Qwen induction 0.671 vs 0.014, OLMo induction 1.527 vs 0.229) and are ranked by H_ij at only Spearman 0.11-0.53. Co-selection tracks the normalised Hessian at 0.04-0.37 and the interactions at 0.05-0.32. The loss is far from quadratic in head scales over [0, 1]: circuit interactions found by ablation are a large-move property that local quantities (gradients, co-selection, curvature) do not reproduce.
- e372 THE LOSS ALONG A HEAD'S SCALE (a_h in 1, 0.75, 0.5, 0.25, 0 for the top-8 and 8 random heads, and for the top-8 together; shape index (L(0.5) - L(1)) / (L(0) - L(1)): 0.5 linear, 0.25 quadratic with zero slope, lower when the loss rises only near zero): top single heads have median shape 0.18 / 0.04 GPT-2 (induction / natural), 0.17 / 0.18 SmolLM2, 0.30 / 0.20 Pythia, 0.04 / 0.28 Qwen, 0.12 / 0.17 OLMo, against 0.24-0.57 for random heads: an important head can be halved at little cost and fails only near full removal. The quadratic model at a = 1 captures a median 3-65% of a top head's zero-ablation effect (GPT-2 3-19%, Qwen 3-7%, SmolLM2 40-53%, Pythia 51-65%) and 1-54% of the top-8 joint effect (joint over sum of singles 0.44-3.08; OLMo's joint path saturates, shape 0.84). This is why e371's local quantities underpredict circuits.
- e369 ACROSS PYTHIA TRAINING: the induction heads' ablation co-effects are z +21.5 at step 1000, +22.8 at 4000, +21.4 at 16000, +16.5 at 33000 and +7.6 at the end (AUC 0.89, 0.91, 0.66, 0.89, 0.53), against gradient co-selection z 7.5, 18.4, 16.4, 9.3, 4.8 at the same steps: first-order co-selection carries 35-81% of the ablation signal during training, and both fall at the end.
- e364 / e364b CO-SELECTION ACROSS PYTHIA TRAINING (the e359 measurement at eight checkpoints): the heads' residual co-selection matrix agrees with the final one at Spearman 0.00 (step 1000), 0.03 (4000), 0.13 (16000), 0.24 (33000), 0.40 (63000), 0.63 (93000), 0.75 (123000) against a permuted null of 0.00-0.01; its communities never persist (ARI -0.01 to 0.16 natural, up to 0.28 mixed); the heads' mean selection strength persists better (0.08, 0.31, 0.49, 0.62, 0.69, 0.84, 0.90). Co-selection structure is re-formed throughout training, not a persistent object.
- e360 PER-NEURON DRIFT, PYTHIA-410M (46 checkpoints, blocks 2, 8, 16, all 4096 neurons: write and read vectors, attribution, and a basis-free logit signature, the unit write injected at 5% of the residual norm and the centred logit change sketched on a fixed random 256-dimensional vocabulary projection; TF32 matmuls, shown harmless by e360e: TF32 against fp32 with the same token assignment agree at cosine 1.00 in every block). e360d/e360e RELIABILITY CEILINGS: two independent token assignments at the same weights agree at median per-neuron cosine 0.93 / 0.98 / 1.00 (blocks 2 / 8 / 16) at step 1000, 0.48 / 0.77 / 0.96 at step 33000, and 0.28 / 0.64 / 0.95 at the final step, precision-independent: the logit effect of an early-layer neuron becomes strongly context-dependent over training (the training-time form of e356's one-to-many result), a late-layer neuron's does not. Ceiling-corrected signature similarity to the final checkpoint (blocks 2 / 8 / 16): 0.05 / 0.05 / 0.07 at step 1000, 0.16 / 0.13 / 0.23 at 4000, 0.34 / 0.33 / 0.48 at 16000, 0.63 / 0.57 / 0.68 at 33000, 0.77 / 0.79 / 0.87 at 63000, 0.96-1.02 from 93000. Write vectors (aligned) against the final: 0.27-0.29 at 1000, 0.43-0.47 at 4000, 0.60-0.72 at 16000, 0.77-0.84 at 33000, 0.93-0.95 at 63000, 0.98-0.99 at 93000; attribution Spearman 0.08-0.31, 0.50-0.74, 0.73-0.85, 0.83-0.88, 0.91-0.92, 0.96. A neuron's implementation settles before its function: its logit signature keeps moving after its own weights have stopped, driven by the downstream network, which is function drift under a fixed implementation, the reverse of drift with stable function. Consecutive-checkpoint change is largest across the induction transition (signature 512->1000 0.51-0.62, 1000->2000 0.43-0.52; writes 1000->2000 0.65-0.71). The per-neuron late dissociation (33000 to final) is noise-limited (Spearman -0.26 to +0.24).
- e360c TURNOVER AND SPECIATION (Pythia): of the final top-5% neurons by attribution, 0.13-0.20 are already top-5% at step 1000, 0.44-0.55 at 16000, 0.59-0.65 at 33000, 0.84-0.93 at 123000 (0.72-0.86 Jaccard at 138000 against the final, the boundary's own noise); no dead neurons at any step; spherical k-means of the signatures splits and merges equally at every step (split entropy within 0.1 bit of merge entropy): no directional speciation at this resolution; kNN persistence tracks the ceilings.
- e362 PER-NEURON DRIFT, OLMO-1B (19 checkpoints to step 1.454M, blocks 2, 5, 10): writes (aligned) against the final 0.29 at step 1000, 0.29 at 32000, 0.52 at 256000, 0.75 at 550000, 0.92 at 1.0M, 0.98 at 1.4M; attribution Spearman 0.39 at 32000, 0.73 at 256000, 0.91 at 1.0M; final top-5% share 0.21 at 32000, 0.43 at 256000, 0.68 at 1.0M (median first entry of a final top-5% neuron at step 400000-550000 of 1.454M). OLMo's neurons keep changing their implementation through most of a 3T-token run, where Pythia's settle by a third of theirs. Signature plateaus at 1.15M-1.4M (0.18 / 0.30 / 0.78) sit at the reliability ceiling. e362d CEILINGS AND CORRECTION: two token samples at the same weights agree at 0.53 / 0.75 / 0.93 (blocks 2 / 5 / 10) at step 32000, 0.31 / 0.53 / 0.88 at 550000, 0.17 / 0.29 / 0.80 at the end (the context-dependence of early-layer writes grows in OLMo too); ceiling-corrected signatures against the final trail the writes at every checkpoint (step 256000: 0.27 / 0.23 / 0.41 against writes 0.49-0.52; 400000: 0.46 / 0.40 / 0.56 against 0.64; 550000: 0.53 / 0.51 / 0.66 against 0.75-0.76; 700000: 0.62 / 0.60 / 0.75 against 0.82-0.83): function lags implementation in OLMo as in Pythia.
- e361 THE QUOTIENT AT OTHER DEPTHS (writers of block b = 2, a quarter, a half and three quarters of the depth, read a quarter of the depth later; 20 conditions): function dimension 8-32 (16 in 15 of 20); PLS-16 0.23-0.71, PCA-16 within 0.03 of it, the shuffled-target quotient within 0.03 of it in all 20, random-16 below by 0.01-0.16; cross-observable overlap 0.41-0.58 at or above the split-half reliability of the logit quotient (0.15-0.47) in 19 of 20; the discarded complement decodes function within 0.05 of the core (0.23-0.69 vs 0.23-0.71) and identity as well or better in 13 of 20; the WDD ledger predicts the functional coordinate of joint ablations at 0.33-0.92 against a random control of -0.25 to +0.13 in all 20. The session-31 quotient results are not a block-2 artefact.
- e365 / e365b INDUCTION DEVELOPMENT AND THE QUOTIENT (Pythia, 13 checkpoints): induction gain 0 until step 512 (the previous-token score first rises there, 0.36), 7.86 nats at 1000 (prefix score 0.86), about 11 from 2000 on. The top-8 induction heads' interaction rises from 0.23 (1000) to 0.59 (4000) and 0.65 (final) against 0.02-0.04, their signed interaction from +0.08 to +0.94 and joint non-additivity from 0.38 to 2.01 (step 16000): the circuit keeps adding redundancy long after it works (heads with prefix score above 0.2: 12 at 1000, 21 at 33000). The block-2 quotient's function dimension is 1-2 until step 512, 8 at 1000-2000, 16 from 3000 (32 at 33000); the quotient directions' logit gain over random directions peaks at the induction transition (1.71x at 512, 1.89x at 1000) and decays to 1.1x; the kNN decodability of function from the full descendant falls from 0.99 at initialisation to 0.22; the linearity of the quotient's logit response falls from R2 0.89 to 0.20. Basis-free drift of the quotient: its residual subspace overlaps the final one at 0.02-0.05 until step 4000 and 0.18 at 33000; its logit image at 0.24-0.39 from step 512, while random directions' images overlap the final random image at 0.33-0.70 and the quotient image is no closer to the final quotient image than to the final random image (0.38 vs 0.39 at 33000). The functional coordinate does not persist through training in function space either; what persists is the unembedding's generic image.
- e373 DOES THE QUOTIENT'S GROWTH NEED THE INDUCTION CIRCUIT? (Pythia block-2 quotient at level 12, same tokens and candidates, with the checkpoint's top-8 induction heads or 8 random heads zero-ablated): the descendant cloud's participation rank falls from 19.7 to 13.3 at step 1000, 39.1 to 27.6 at 2000, 60.9 to 47.6 at 4000 and 108.5 to 104.6 at the end when the induction heads are removed, against 19.6, 39.0, 52.9 and 103.7 for random heads (a third of the spread specific to the induction circuit at 1000-2000, a tenth at 4000, none at the end); the kNN decodability of function rises when they are removed (0.74 to 0.86, 0.55 to 0.66, 0.44 to 0.50, 0.22 to 0.23), random heads leave it unchanged; the function dimension is not a stable readout (8 to 4 at step 1000, 8 to 16 at 2000, 16 unchanged later). Right after the transition the induction circuit supplies part of the descendant's spread and context dependence; later growth of the quotient does not depend on it.
- Interim reading. (1) The unit statistics the earlier sessions used do detect known circuits on task data in all five models, and they are largely blind to them in natural-text population averages; scoring at the positions that use the circuit recovers part of the signal. The old natural-text negatives (e264, e302) are statements about population averages, not about the absence of circuits. (2) First-order co-selection is a weak and model-dependent circuit detector at the final checkpoint, stronger at token resolution, strongest just after a circuit forms, and fading as training converges, because at an optimum it measures local mis-scaling rather than use. (3) Ablation-level circuit structure is a large-move, non-quadratic property: gradients, co-selection and curvature at the trained point underpredict it by an order of magnitude and rank it weakly. (4) Neurons settle their implementation before their function; the function of early-layer neurons becomes strongly context-dependent; co-selection structure and the quotient's coordinates are re-formed through training rather than carried.

SESSION 33 (phase 3, the same A100 on the evening of 2026-09-23, after re-reading the original prompt: fundamentals over bigger numbers, one to five minutes per run, older mathematics where it helps, and attacks on the program's own conclusions; e374-e382, 9 scripts plus helpers, about 70 runs; five pretrained models, Pythia checkpoints, and 13 tiny transformers trained from scratch, the first training in the program, which the original prompt's "toy model ur way" allows).
- e375 WDD READ THROUGH A READER FINDS KNOWN CIRCUIT EDGES. The original WDD reading (OMP, k = 64, over the model's own write atoms, from the state alone) of the residual state at the key positions of each attention-defined induction head, each selected atom weighted by its share of that head's key vector (frozen input norm, an exact additive decomposition): for all 20 induction heads (four per model), the exact decomposition from the real per-head writes names a previous-token head as the top key input (previous-token heads carry 21-79% of the key; for non-induction control heads of the same layer 0-6%, except where GQA shares the key), and reader-weighted WDD places a previous-token head at rank 1 in 14 of 20 readers and rank 2 in the other 6 (plain WDD, without the reader, has mean rank 2.9 and ranks up to 8), among 32-224 upstream heads. For GPT-2's IOI name movers' queries, the exact top input is the S-inhibition head 8.6 for all three, and reader-weighted WDD ranks an S-inhibition head first for two (fourth for the third; plain WDD 2, 2, 18). WDD recovers the right head but a small part of its share (0.02-0.13 against 0.21-0.79), and near-twin heads absorb credit (GPT-2's 4.7 alongside 4.11), the original misattribution failure mode.
- e381 WDD AS A GENERAL EDGE FINDER (every head of every layer, query, key and value inputs, natural text): on the edges that matter (the exact top upstream head carries at least 10% of the input), reader-weighted WDD's top upstream head is the exact one for 0.23 GPT-2, 0.14 SmolLM2, 0.58 Pythia, 0.69 Qwen and 0.02 OLMo of readers (plain WDD 0.16, 0.03, 0.48, 0.35, 0.00; chance 0.005-0.034), top-3 overlap 0.18-0.47. Far above chance in four models, reliable in none; the induction circuit's clean result reflects a distinctive, dominant writer.
- e374 WHERE THE REMOVAL KNEE LIVES. For the top-6 heads of each model, the loss around full strength is an ordinary quadratic (local exponent of the even part 2.0-2.1 in every model): no degenerate direction, so the singular-learning-theory reading of local blindness is killed. The knee is a large-move effect: the loss predicted by moving the logits linearly between full strength and removal has shape 0.10-0.34 (median over top heads), close to the measured 0.04-0.30, so most of the knee is the softmax reading moving logits; in GPT-2 and Qwen the internal state also moves less than linearly (at half strength 0.28-0.37 of the way), in SmolLM2, Pythia and OLMo about linearly (0.45-0.50). The downstream head most affected by a removal changes its winning key at half strength about as a linear margin crossing predicts in OLMo (0.23 vs 0.22) and later than predicted in Qwen (0.22 vs 0.47) and GPT-2 (0.52 vs 0.69). Removing the top-6 together raises the induction loss by 5-11 nats and is 2.7-32 times the quadratic extrapolation; the joint path is robust to halving in GPT-2, Pythia and Qwen (shape 0.09-0.15) and fragile in SmolLM2 and OLMo (0.52, 0.81: halving the six heads already moves OLMo's logits 93% of the way).
- e376 NEAR-TWINS DO NOT MAKE BACKUPS AND SERIES DO NOT MAKE SUB-ADDITIVITY ON THE LOSS. Across 276 pairs per model and dataset, the signed loss interaction correlates with the two heads' write cosine at -0.11 to +0.22 (weakly positive in four models), with WDD confusability at -0.19 to +0.27 (inconsistent), and with composition (one head reading the other) at -0.06 to +0.29, positive in 9 of 10 conditions: pairs in series look like backups on the loss.
- e377 INTERACTION SIGNS DEPEND ON THE READOUT. The same top-12 pairs on the loss and on linear readouts (the correct token's logit, and its logit centred over the vocabulary): super-additive pairs on the loss 0.44-0.77 against sub-additive 0.03-0.38; on the centred logit 0.14-0.32 against 0.30-0.61, with sub-additive pairs at least as common in every model; 26-48% of pairs change sign between the loss and the logit. Most of what the loss shows as backup redundancy is not visible in logit space.
- e382 THE READOUT'S CONVEXITY (Jensen). Had the two single effects added exactly in logit space, the loss would show 97-100% of top pairs as super-additive (the convexity of cross-entropy alone); the measured loss interaction nets this against the logit-space interaction. The split between readout and circuit is order dependent: evaluating the logit-space interaction at the joint point gives a circuit term of -0.10 to -3.42 (sub-additive in 61-80% of pairs), evaluating it at the intact model gives +0.15 to +1.29 (sub-additive in 0-2% of pairs), because at the intact model the correct token dominates and at the joint point its competitors do. The loss interaction therefore has no unique attribution to circuit and readout; only linear readouts give a path-independent reading of the circuit.
- e380 PHASE 2'S REDUNDANCY GROWTH RE-MEASURED. For Pythia's top-8 induction heads at steps 1000, 2000, 4000, 16000, 33000 and the end: on the loss, the mean pairwise interaction rises +0.09, +0.34, +1.34, +1.37, +1.47, +1.29 and joint over sum 0.69, 0.69, 1.08, 3.19, 3.61, 2.94 (phase 2's result); on the correct token's logit the interaction is +0.04, -0.07, +0.14, +0.22, +0.28, +0.12 and joint over sum 0.63, 0.49, 0.55, 0.84, 0.93, 0.91, below 1 at every checkpoint. In logit space the circuit is never redundant; the growth phase 2 reported is mostly the loss becoming more convex as the circuit becomes confident.
- e378 STITCHING ACROSS TRAINING TIME (Pythia steps 1000-143000, cuts at blocks 4-20). Lower parts are not swappable late in training: the final model's upper part under the step-63000 lower part loses 0.28-0.42 nats (more than the whole 0.115 gain from step 63000 to the end), under the step-33000 lower part 0.49-0.72; the old upper parts reject the final lower part too (0.14-0.89). The lower part's age explains 45-59% of the stitched-loss variance and the upper's 6-15% (raw); an affine map fitted at the cut repairs about half of the late penalty (step 33000 at cut 12: +0.49 to +0.24; step 63000 at cut 4: +0.42 to +0.06) and shifts the variance toward the upper part (34-68%). The two halves keep co-adapting to the end, and write vectors at cosine 0.95 of their final form are not functionally final.
- e379 TOY INDUCTION TRANSFORMERS (13 variants, 4000 steps, trained in about 4 minutes each). Every converged toy without head dropout (seeds, weight decay 0 or 0.3, 3 layers, 90% repeated data, 12000 steps) learns induction (loss 0.000-0.091) with a sub-additive circuit: joint over sum 0.20-0.53 on the loss and 0.20-0.62 on the logit (0.91 on the loss for the 12000-step run); head dropout 0.2 makes the same circuit strongly super-additive (3.79 on the loss, 1.31 on the logit), so redundancy is manufactured by training noise. At convergence the circuit heads' mean gradient selection is about zero (t -0.1 to +2.2) while their ablation use is 1.3-5.1 nats; in runs that never converge it stays large (t +5.0 head dropout, +8.0 when induction never forms), and selection tracks use across heads at Spearman +0.89 to +0.94 when the model is far from any optimum and -0.45 to +0.28 once converged. Co-selection of the circuit heads is high while the circuit is being built (z up to 6-13) and near zero once it is (z -1.9 to +2.8), and stays high only in runs that never finish (head dropout z +4.3 and +7.4, slow learning rate +5.8). A constant learning rate converges too; its top pairs are +1.61 on the loss and -0.31 on the logit.
- Reading. (1) The model's own write atoms, read by WDD from the state and weighted by a reader's input map, recover a known circuit edge without any intervention, and give an above-chance but unreliable edge list elsewhere. (2) The knee of unit removal lives mostly in the softmax readout, not in a degenerate loss landscape, and the same convexity makes pairs look like backups: the redundancy phase 2 reported for induction circuits, and much of what ablation studies call backup behaviour, is a readout effect; linear readouts show sub-additive, series-like structure, and toy models show real redundancy only when trained with noise. (3) First-order selection vanishes at convergence and co-selection lights up only while a circuit is under construction, now shown under direct manipulation. (4) Implementation does not settle before function: the two halves co-adapt to the end.

SESSION 34 (phase 3b, the same evening: an audit of which of the ~340 experiments depend on a convex readout, re-reads of the affected ones, two WDD-native follow-ups, a self-repair test in toys, and a literature check of every phase-3 claim; e383-e386, 4 scripts, 20 runs).
- AUDIT. Seven experiments measured pairwise or joint interactions on a convex readout: e357, e365, e366, e371, e372 and e376 on the cross-entropy loss, e264 on KL. All others work in state space, coordinate space or on logit changes (WDD identification, descendants, the quotient, transport, drift, co-selection) or on a linear readout (e358's IOI logit difference, e302's logit-change vectors), and are unaffected. e371, e372, e376 and e365 were re-read in session 33 (e374, e377, e380, e382); e264 and e357/e366 here.
- e383 e264 RE-READ IN LOGIT SPACE. The KL interaction of a neuron pair equals the Fisher inner product of the two single logit effects (d_A' H d_B, H the softmax covariance): per-token correlation 0.73-0.98, means within 0.001-0.1 of each other in all five models. It measures whether two footprints overlap under the readout, not whether the circuit adds. In logit space the descendant-similar pairs are additive (|d_AB - d_A - d_B| at 0.019-0.042 of the single effects; next-token interaction 0.000-0.002), so e264's conclusion stands; the redundancy it reported for GPT-2's and Pythia's control pairs (-0.32, -0.10) is the Fisher overlap of anti-aligned footprints (-0.22, -0.085), a readout effect.
- e384 e357 AND e366 RE-READ (mean-ablation as in the originals). Joint over sum for the top-8 induction heads, loss against the centred next-token logit: GPT-2 4.99 against 0.94, SmolLM2 1.12 against 0.79, Pythia 5.96 against 1.72, Qwen 1.62 against 0.82, OLMo 1.92 against 0.91. The loss inflates the ratio 1.4-5.3x; on the logit the circuit is additive or sub-additive in four models and redundant only in Pythia (1.72 under mean-ablation; 0.91 under zero-ablation, e380). Natural text at induction-applicable positions, top against random heads on the centred logit: 0.28/0.20, 0.24/0.09, 0.16/0.05, 0.25/0.08, 0.45/0.11, against 0.21/0.13, 0.41/0.12, 0.21/0.05, 0.36/0.08, 0.50/0.13 on the loss: e366's conditional contrast survives the readout change.
- e385 DO READERS READ WHAT WDD CAN READ? On natural text, over every reader input (query, key, value of every head of layers 1 and up) and every upstream head's write at every position: writes that WDD identifies in the state (at least one atom selected) carry 1.33-1.46 times more of the readers' input share than their share of write-norm mass predicts (GPT-2 1.46, SmolLM2 1.36, Pythia 1.37, Qwen 1.33, OLMo 1.46), above 1 in every write-norm quintile (1.06-1.57). As a predictor of the strongest single entries identification adds nothing to write norm (AUC 0.51-0.56 alone; norm 0.86-0.93; both 0.82-0.92). An aggregate link between WDD's prominence law and what downstream heads use, not a per-edge tool.
- e386 SELF-REPAIR IN TOYS (4000-step toys, direct effect of each head through the frozen final norm and unembedding against its total effect on the correct logit; compensation = the share of the direct effect the network makes up when the head is removed; in a 2-layer attention-only model the last layer can be repaired only by the final norm's rescaling). Annealed runs without dropout: last-layer induction heads repaired 0-19% (median +0.01 base, +0.01 zero weight decay, -0.37 another seed); head dropout 0.2: +0.32 to +0.49; constant learning rate: +0.26. The 3-layer toy's top heads act mostly indirectly, so the measure does not apply there.
- LITERATURE CHECK (RELATED_WORK section 13). The exact hook-based split of a head's query or key input over upstream components is standard (ARENA's decompose_qk_input; Franco and Crovella, NeurIPS 2025, recover IOI circuits from single passes with frozen norms), so e375's contribution narrows to the state-only approximation over the model's own write atoms, which the exact split beats. That first-order selection vanishes at convergence is 1989-1990 pruning lore (Mozer and Smolensky; Optimal Brain Damage). Real models trained without dropout show self-repair (McGrath et al. 2023 in Chinchilla; Rushing and Nanda 2024 in Pythia) and small attention-only models trained without dropout have shown redundancy on accuracy (Singh et al. 2024), so the toy result is a toy-and-readout statement; e384's Pythia (no dropout) keeps logit-space redundancy, and e386's constant-learning-rate toy repairs 26%, both consistent with the prior work. Not found: the measured sign-flip rate of head-pair interactions between loss and logit, gradient co-selection of known circuits across checkpoints, block swaps between checkpoints of one language-model run, and the readout-versus-trajectory split of head-scale curves.
- Reading. None of the ~340 experiments needs redoing wholesale: seven used a convex readout for interactions, all seven are now re-read, and what changes is the size of redundancy claims (inflated 1.4-5.3x by the loss; genuine only in Pythia among the five) and one retracted redundancy reading (e264's control pairs). WDD read through a reader is a validation of WDD's provenance semantics, not a new circuit-discovery capability; its distinct, WDD-native finding is that readers preferentially use the writes WDD can identify.

SESSION 35 (a fresh-slate pass: the questions an outside researcher would ask on meeting WDD with all prior results in hand; e387-e390, 4 scripts, 22 runs; one to five minutes each except the first depth sweep of e388).
- e387 THE ANATOMY OF READING. For every head's query, key and value input on natural text, the exact share of every upstream component (heads, MLP blocks, embeddings) at every position. (1) Fan-in: a reader input draws half its content from 8-14 components and 80% from 25-55 (participation ratio 17-33 in the trained models); heads supply 53-69% of the |input shares|, MLP blocks 30-46%, embeddings about 1%. (2) How much WDD sees: components with at least one atom among WDD's first k picks carry 23-31% of the readers' |input| at k = 8, 39-52% at k = 64 and 48-69% at k = 256. (3) The preference of readers for identifiable writes (e385) is mostly geometric: split each reader's direction into its part along the state and the orthogonal part, and the preference at k = 64 falls from 1.17-1.56 to 1.05-1.27; within quintiles of each write's alignment with the state it is 1.16-1.23 in GPT-2 and 1.31-1.39 in OLMo but about 1 (0.98-1.15) in SmolLM2, Qwen and Pythia. (4) In development (Pythia): at initialisation the raw preference is already 1.71 and the state-orthogonal one 1.01; reading concentrates before the induction transition (step 512: half of an input from 7 components, WDD's 64 atoms capture 73% of what is read, MLP outputs supply 73% of the input) and then spreads (step 16000: 16 components, 43% captured; heads supply 57-61% from step 4000 on); the orthogonal preference appears by step 512 (1.10) and stays at 1.08-1.14.
- e388 WDD JUDGED AS A SPARSE AUTOENCODER (the residual state after a block replaced by its k-sparse WDD reconstruction at every position but the first, the model run on; loss recovered = (L_mean - L_splice) / (L_mean - L_clean); three depths per model). At k = 8 WDD recovers 0.51-0.80 (GPT-2), 0.61-0.88 (SmolLM2), 0.33-0.50 (Pythia), 0.62-0.81 (Qwen), 0.31-0.50 (OLMo) of the loss, against -0.10 to 0.35 for OMP over a randomly rotated copy of the same dictionary (same Gram matrix, no provenance) and 0.11-0.40 for PCA-8 fitted on other text; at k = 32, 0.70-0.97 against 0.26-0.87 and 0.22-0.67; at k = 64, 0.84-0.99 against 0.65-0.97 and 0.29-0.78; at k = 128 every overcomplete dictionary recovers 0.89-1.00 (PCA-128 0.40-0.91). A non-learned, provenance-labelled dictionary keeps 84-99% of the loss at 64 atoms, and its advantage over dictionaries without provenance lives at small k.
- e389 WHICH ATOMS CARRY THE ADVANTAGE (middle depth). The model's MLP write rows alone recover as much as the full dictionary at k = 8 (GPT-2 0.51 vs 0.52, SmolLM2 0.72 vs 0.72, Pythia 0.33 vs 0.37, Qwen 0.69 vs 0.69, OLMo 0.32 vs 0.31); token and position embeddings alone 0.03-0.37, head write bases alone -0.02 to 0.21, the rotated full dictionary 0.02-0.29.
- e390 PROVENANCE OR TRAINED GEOMETRY (Pythia, final states at the middle depth, MLP rows taken from other checkpoints of the same run). Loss recovered at k = 8 / 32: rows from step 0 (cosine to the final rows 0.06) 0.14 / 0.54, step 1000 (0.12) 0.17 / 0.56, step 4000 (0.35) -0.00 / 0.47, step 16000 (0.61) 0.21 / 0.64, step 33000 (0.78) 0.29 / 0.71, step 63000 (0.93) 0.33 / 0.73, the model's own 0.33 / 0.73. The advantage needs the writers that wrote the state (or near-identical ones); trained rows that differ (step 4000) do no better than random.
- Reading. The outside researcher's questions get three answers. Is "readers read what WDD reads" deep? Mostly not: it is present in random networks, and a genuine part survives only in GPT-2 and OLMo. Is WDD's sparse picture of the state functionally faithful? Yes, and specifically because of provenance: at 8-32 atoms the model's own neuron write vectors keep far more of the computation than a rotated copy, PCA, embeddings or head bases, and only the rows that actually wrote the state do. How much of what the model reads can a sparse reading see? About half at 64 atoms, more when reading is concentrated (before the induction transition) and less as training spreads it.

SESSION 36 (the vision chain, 2026-09-24 00:20-00:45 box time: if WDD's sparse code keeps the computation, what is it a code of? e391-e394, 4 scripts, 20 runs, five models at the middle depth).
- e391 THE CODE IS NOT A RECORD OF THE LARGEST WRITES. The middle-depth state is an exact sum of components (embedding, every head's write, every MLP neuron's write, biases). Keeping at each position the k components that deviate most from their own mean, every other component at its mean, recovers at k = 8 / 64 / 256 / 1024: GPT-2 0.32 / 0.79 / 0.87 / 0.87, SmolLM2 0.18 / 0.65 / 0.82 / 0.92, Pythia 0.29 / 0.53 / 0.63 / 0.75, Qwen 0.14 / 0.53 / 0.77 / 0.90, OLMo 0.28 / 0.66 / 0.85 / 0.90. WDD's OMP code from the state alone recovers 0.96, 0.99, 0.92, 0.98, 0.91 at k = 64 (e388). The largest MLP neurons alone 0.13-0.29 at k = 8 and 0.40-0.78 at k = 256; k random components 0.00. Ninety percent of the loss needs 32-64 of the model's own words and 1024 or more actual writes (not reached by 1024 in GPT-2 and Pythia).
- e393 SUPPORT AND COEFFICIENTS. Refitting the coefficients of the k largest actual writes to the whole state raises k = 64 from 0.79 to 0.89 (GPT-2), 0.65 to 0.85 (SmolLM2), 0.53 to 0.68 (Pythia), 0.53 to 0.74 (Qwen), 0.66 to 0.69 (OLMo): the dropped tail lies 61-76% in the span of the kept directions at k = 64 (30-42% at k = 8) and the refit absorbs it. WDD's own support stays above (0.91-0.99), so the choice of directions matters as well as the coefficients. Only 20-37% of WDD's MLP atoms are among the k largest actual neuron writes at k = 8, 11-27% at k = 64.
- e392 THE MODEL'S OWN WORDS AS A REPLACEMENT MODEL (every block's output replaced during the forward pass by its k-sparse WDD code). Excess loss over clean at k = 16 / 64 / 128: GPT-2 +4.22 / +2.05 / +0.43, SmolLM2 +5.19 / +3.70 / +0.53, Pythia +5.36 / +5.09 / +3.26, Qwen +5.35 / +4.43 / +1.28, OLMo +5.98 / +5.32 / +3.77 nats; rotated dictionary +4.78 / +4.26 / +1.82, +5.53 / +5.22 / +2.37, +5.93 / +5.25 / +4.95, +5.64 / +5.69 / +2.46, +9.62 / +7.48 / +6.07. One layer at a time the code keeps 84-99% of the loss at 64 atoms; at every layer at once the errors compound, and only GPT-2 and SmolLM2 at 128 atoms per layer come within about half a nat.
- e394 ATOMS AS ATTRIBUTION NODES (32-atom code, one atom removed at a time, per-position loss change). Effects are spread (participation ratio 12.8-18.0; 13-16 of 32 atoms carry 80%); gradient-times-coefficient ranks them at Spearman 0.33 GPT-2, 0.41 SmolLM2, 0.67 Pythia, 0.40 Qwen, 0.54 OLMo; MLP rows carry 63-86% of the effect, in proportion to their share of atoms (59-81%). Faithful as a code, not a sparse causal graph.
- Reading. WDD's code is a re-description, not a trace: the model writes densely, yet a few dozen of its own write directions, chosen by the state and refitted, carry nearly all of what the rest of the network uses. The description is faithful one layer at a time and not as a standalone forward pass, and its atoms are not sparse causal nodes. This suggests treating the weights as a vocabulary and asking how short the model's description of itself is (session 37).

SESSION 37 (the "purer direction": self-description length and private languages; e395-e398b, 5 scripts, 13 runs; Pythia-410m checkpoints and PolyPythia seeds, middle depth, loss recovered as e388).
- e395 SELF-DESCRIPTION LENGTH ACROSS TRAINING (each checkpoint's own vocabulary and states). At step 0 the own vocabulary is a rotated vocabulary: FVU identical at every k (0.93, 0.86, 0.76, 0.58, 0.35, 0.12, 0.01 for k = 4-256; the clean-to-mean gap is 0.01 nats, so loss recovered is undefined there). Loss recovered at k = 16, own against rotated: step 256 0.88 / 0.81, 512 0.81 / 0.76, 1000 0.81 / 0.67, 2000 0.76 / 0.61, 4000 0.75 / 0.63, 16000 0.65 / 0.46, 63000 0.62 / 0.32, final 0.55 / 0.32. Self-description length (k at 90% of the loss) 32 own words at steps 256-1000 and 64 from step 2000 to the end; the rotated vocabulary needs 32, 32, 64, 64, 64, 128, 128, 128. The 4096 largest actual writes recover 1.05 (step 256), 1.00, 0.98, 0.95, 0.95, 0.90, 0.89 and 0.84 (final): the writing becomes denser while the description stays short, a redundancy above 64x at the end. Variance compressibility arrives later and separately: 4 own words leave FVU 0.84-0.91 up to step 4000, 0.50 at 16000 and 0.16 at 63000 and the end (rotated 0.92-0.93 throughout).
- e396 MUTUAL INTELLIGIBILITY ACROSS TRAINING (vocabulary of checkpoint i describing the states of checkpoint j, loss recovered in j's own forward pass). k = 16, rows vocabulary, columns states of steps 1000, 4000, 16000, 33000, 63000, 143000: 1000: 0.81 0.73 0.58 0.54 0.50 0.44 | 4000: 0.79 0.75 0.50 0.34 0.25 0.13 | 16000: 0.76 0.74 0.65 0.60 0.55 0.46 | 33000: 0.77 0.75 0.64 0.63 0.60 0.53 | 63000: 0.77 0.75 0.66 0.65 0.62 0.56 | 143000: 0.76 0.75 0.66 0.64 0.61 0.55 (k = 64 the same pattern within 0.81-0.97). From step 33000 the vocabularies are interchangeable (within 0.03 of the target's own words, sometimes above); later words read early states almost as well as the early model's own (0.76-0.79 against 0.81 at step 1000) while early words read late states worse (step 1000 on the final states 0.44 against 0.55): the vocabulary grows by accretion and converges. Median MLP-row cosine between neighbouring checkpoints 0.40, 0.64, 0.85, 0.88, 0.93 (0.12 between steps 1000 and 143000). The exception is step 4000, whose words describe each later stage worse (0.50, 0.34, 0.25, 0.13), below the older step-1000 words and below a random rotation of the final vocabulary (0.32).
- e397 FALSE FRIENDS. On the final states, the step-4000 vocabulary recovers -0.28, -0.12, 0.13, 0.49, 0.81 at k = 4, 8, 16, 32, 64 while leaving FVU 0.77, 0.71, 0.62, 0.48, 0.29; its random rotation recovers 0.05, 0.12, 0.21, 0.42, 0.74 at FVU 0.93-0.36 like every rotated vocabulary (rotations of the five vocabularies: 0.19-0.32 at k = 16). On the step-63000 states: -0.13, 0.00, 0.25, 0.61, 0.85 against 0.07, 0.17, 0.28, 0.54, 0.83. Not numerical (condition number of the picked atoms 1.5-1.8 in every cell; a float64 refit gives the same values) and not geometry (effective rank of the step-4000 vocabulary 848 against 812-970 for the others; common direction 0.03). At 4-8 words the step-4000 words capture three times the variance random words capture and leave the function worse than the mean state; at 32-64 words the same words beat their rotation. The picked MLP rows are ordinary rows (same-index cosine to the final rows 0.35, the median of all rows). Pre-registered: false friends predicted the rotated step-4000 vocabulary at the random level, stated as at least 0.3 at k = 16; it gave 0.21, inside the 0.19-0.32 spread of rotations: by the letter neither prediction, in substance false friends (the geometry is ordinary, the harm is carried by the orientation, clearest at k = 4-8). The step-1000 words, with a row cosine of only 0.12 to the final rows, still beat their rotation (0.44 against 0.31 at k = 16).
- e398 PRIVATE LANGUAGES ACROSS INDIVIDUALS (the standard Pythia-410m run and PolyPythia seeds 1 and 2, final checkpoints, six ordered pairs, loss recovered at k = 16, mean over pairs). Own words 0.59, own rotated 0.36. The other individual's vocabulary as is 0.36; translated by the orthogonal map that best aligns the two input-embedding matrices 0.36 (that map aligns the embeddings themselves at R2 -0.10: the two lexicons are not rotations of each other), by the one aligning the unembeddings 0.37 (R2 0.24); by an orthogonal map fitted on paired residual states (8 other sequences, levels 3, 6, 9, 12) 0.48 against 0.37 for random words through the same map; by a ridge-linear state map 0.66, above the own words. At k = 64 everything lies within 0.88-0.93. The orthogonal state map aligns the two individuals' held-out states at R2 0.04-0.13 (negative when the standard run, whose states are larger, is the source), the linear map at 0.28-0.38, yet the translated state itself spliced in recovers 0.48-0.81 (orthogonal) and 0.67-0.82 (linear) of the loss; the embedding-fitted map gives -0.03 to -0.17. Pre-registered: raw at the random level (confirmed); state translation recovering most of the own-minus-random gap at k = 64 (the k = 64 gap is 0.04, too small to judge; at k = 16 the orthogonal translation recovers half); lexicon translation between random and state translation (refuted: at the random level).
- e398b THE LINEAR MAP'S CONTROL. Random words pushed through the same ridge-linear state map recover 0.59 at k = 16 (two rotations, 0.59 and 0.59), equal to the target's own words; the target's own words and its rotated words through its own self-map stay at 0.59 and 0.37. The subspace of one network's state that another network's state predicts linearly is small in variance (R2 0.28-0.38) and large in function: random directions confined to it describe the state as well as the network's own words. The translated words add 0.07 over random words through the linear map and 0.11 through the orthogonal map, a third to a half of the own words' advantage (0.23). Pre-registered criterion (control within 0.05 of the translation): 0.07, just outside; the linear translation's margin over the own words is this 0.07.
- Reading. A network's own weights are a vocabulary in which its computation has a short description, and training makes them so: at initialisation the own vocabulary is exactly as good as a random rotation of itself, by step 256 it is better, and from the induction transition on 16 own words keep 0.12-0.30 more of the loss than rotated ones. The description length stays at 32-64 words while the writing becomes denser, so at the end the model's writing is more than 64 times redundant with respect to its own shortest description at the middle depth. Within one run the vocabulary grows by accretion and converges, with one stage (step 4000) whose words are false friends for the later model. Across individuals the language is mostly private: the shared tokens do not translate it, a third to a half of the own-word advantage survives the best translation, and what two individuals share linearly is where the function is.

SESSION 38 (an external review, relayed by the user, weighed item by item, and the controls it asked for, run as small experiments; e399-e406, 8 scripts plus sd_common, 26 runs; 8 sequences with bootstrap intervals over sequences unless stated).
- TRIAGE OF THE REVIEW.
  - Accepted and tested:
    - second-order / projection artefact (e399);
    - a coordinate system shared with the operators that act on the state (e399 readers);
    - a functional description length with function-aware selection (e400);
    - a learned-dictionary reference at equal size (e403);
    - a per-target null for the checkpoint matrix (e402);
    - replication across architectures, depths and a second family (e401, e406);
    - more sequences and several rotation seeds;
    - citation of Herrmann, Csordás and Schmidhuber 2025;
    - keeping write sparsity, Euclidean re-description and functional description apart.
  - Not accepted:
    - "leakage" as a general threat. The evaluation runs through downstream weights that are not in the dictionary. The one concrete shortcut, the current-token embedding, is ruled out by MLP rows alone (e389, e399).
    - the reading of e398 as "the translated vocabulary equals the target's". e398b: the fitted linear map lifts random words to the own level, and the translated words add 0.07.
    - a step-0 row in the functional matrix. There is no function to preserve at initialisation: the clean-to-mean gap is 0.01 nats.
  - Deferred: a 20-30-paper novelty matrix, to a write-up.
- e401 REPLICATION.
  - At a random initialisation of all five architectures, at a quarter, half and three quarters of the depth, own and rotated FVU agree within 0.004 (SmolLM2, Pythia, Qwen), 0.009 (OLMo) and 0.014 (GPT-2). GPT-2's exception is at a quarter depth, where the state still carries its token and position embeddings verbatim.
  - Trained, rotated minus own FVU at k = 16 is at least 0.14-0.57 at every depth.
  - OLMo-1B across training, loss recovered at k = 16, own against the mean of three rotations: step 1000 0.71 / 0.29, 4000 0.63 / 0.27, 16000 0.43 / 0.15, 64000 0.70 / 0.06, 256000 0.84 / 0.26, 1454000 0.66 / 0.08. Training creates self-describability in a second family.
- e399 WHAT MAKES THE OWN WORDS GOOD (Pythia, middle depth; loss recovered at k = 16 at steps 1000 / 16000 / 143000).
  - own 0.83 / 0.67 / 0.60; three rotations 0.69-0.70 / 0.44-0.46 / 0.30-0.32.
  - covA (Gaussian words with the own vocabulary's second moment) 0.78 / 0.46 / 0.33.
  - mix8 (signed sums of 8 own words of one family) 0.81 / 0.49 / 0.29.
  - covX (Gaussian words with the states' covariance, fitted on other text) 0.94 / 0.79 / 0.74; covX_sqrt 0.92 / 0.75 / 0.69.
  - MLP rows only: writers 0.81 / 0.62 / 0.56 against their rotation 0.66 / 0.41 / 0.29. Downstream readers (input rows of later blocks, gain-scaled and centred) 0.74 / 0.57 / 0.41 against 0.65 / 0.41 / 0.21. Readers of the writing blocks 0.67 / 0.45 / 0.15.
  - Pre-registered (final checkpoint): covA at the rotation level (confirmed); mix8 at the rotation level (confirmed); writers well above their rotation (confirmed); covX below own in loss recovered (refuted: covX is above own at every checkpoint). At step 1000 covA and mix8 were not at the rotation level (refuted there).
- e405 FROM ACCENT TO VOCABULARY. The share of the own-minus-rotation gap at k = 16 carried by covA / mix8:

  | Step | covA | mix8 |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | 256 | 0.44 | 0.66 |
  | 512 | 0.88 | 1.34 |
  | 1000 | 0.62 | 0.85 |
  | 2000 | 0.70 | 0.79 |
  | 4000 | 0.53 | 0.68 |
  | 8000 | 0.41 | 0.45 |
  | 16000 | 0.05 | 0.18 |
  | 63000 | 0.21 | 0.25 |
  | 143000 | 0.07 | -0.07 |

  Early, the own vocabulary's advantage is mostly its second moment and span, an accent. From step 16000 it is carried by the individual words. Pre-registered window 2000-8000: partly (the fall begins after step 2000 and completes between 8000 and 16000).
- e400 FUNCTIONAL DESCRIPTION LENGTH (words chosen under the network's Fisher metric at the middle depth, normalised, 1% ridge).
  - The states' top-8 principal directions hold 0.22 / 0.52 / 0.86 of the variance at steps 1000 / 16000 / 143000 and 0.042 / 0.017 / 0.015 of the Fisher trace (Fisher participation ratio 189 / 500 / 456): the huge directions that appear during training carry almost no local sensitivity.
  - Loss recovered at k = 16, Euclidean / Fisher: own 0.83 / 0.89, 0.67 / 0.74, 0.60 / 0.73; rotations 0.70 / 0.85, 0.44 / 0.65, 0.31 / 0.49; covX 0.94 / 0.95, 0.79 / 0.80, 0.74 / 0.75. The own-minus-rotation gap under the functional pursuit is 0.04 / 0.09 / 0.24, against 0.13 / 0.23 / 0.29 Euclidean.
  - At k = 4 the functional pursuit lifts the own words 0.53 to 0.70, 0.33 to 0.44 and 0.24 to 0.41. The 90% length is unchanged (32 at step 1000, 64 at 16000 and at the end).
  - The step-4000 words on the final states: Euclidean -0.37 / 0.11 at k = 4 / 16, Fisher -0.10 / 0.27, against their rotation 0.06 / 0.20 and 0.20 / 0.54. On the step-16000 states under Fisher: 0.59 against 0.65.
  - Pre-registered (final checkpoint): a shorter self-description (refuted); the own advantage persisting at half its size or more (confirmed: 0.24 of 0.29); the false friends vanishing under the Fisher metric (refuted: they deepen).
- e404 THE FALSE FRIENDS ARE MEDIATED BY THE HUGE DIRECTIONS (final states; M = the states' top-8 principal subspace, fitted on other text).
